<a class="locale-pair" data-locale-counterpart href="/practice/" lang="zh-CN">阅读中文对应页</a>

The Practice Bank collects original problems that recur across the curriculum. Each entry names prerequisite Learning Units, a Hardware gate, acceptance criteria, hints, a solution, and its Source basis. It is not a copied question dump.

## How to use it

Review the linked prerequisite, then produce your own artifact before reading the reviewed solution. Open hints in order. The reviewed solution checks boundaries rather than replacing the work. This page publishes only the 89 complete entries below and uses no empty preview entries.

## Published entry index

<ResourceIndex group="practice" locale="en" />

A planning ID is a stable label for cross-curriculum discussion, not a URL-stability promise. Filtering changes only the cards visible in the current browser. The 89 complete prompts, prerequisite links, and reviewed solutions below remain in the static page.

The R3 release contract explicitly classifies these 10 entries as the Nsight report-analysis subset: `PB-R3-002`, `PB-R3-003`, `PB-R3-004`, `PB-R3-005`, `PB-R3-007`, `PB-R3-008`, `PB-R3-009`, `PB-R3-010`, `PB-R3-011`, and `PB-R3-012`. They ask the learner to design or audit timelines, metrics and replay, report custody, Environment Manifests, and bounded conclusions. Static analysis is not a profiler run and grants no CUDA Evidence Status.

The **2026-09-10 R4 aggregate static review** qualifies eight library-and-algorithm-choice entries alongside that unchanged R3 subset. These are classifications within the existing 82 entries, not new questions. They require a reasoned selection or rejection under semantics, version, resource, lifetime, and evidence constraints rather than ranking libraries by name.

| Qualifying entry | Distinct decision |
| --- | --- |
| [PB-R4-001](#pb-r4-001) | Library, primitive, DSL, or custom kernel, including ownership cost and a revisit trigger |
| [PB-R4-002](#pb-r4-002) | Thrust algorithm, iterator, and stream composition with valid ranges and completion |
| [PB-R4-003](#pb-r4-003) | CUB device reduction versus inclusive/exclusive scan, with per-version storage and determinism contracts |
| [PB-R4-004](#pb-r4-004) | Warp/block reduction or scan, participant scope, scratch reuse, and algorithm exclusions |
| [PB-R4-008](#pb-r4-008) | cuBLASLt candidate rejection, cache invalidation, and a semantics-preserving fallback |
| [PB-R4-011](#pb-r4-011) | cuDNN engine candidates under numerical policy, total workspace, and build constraints |
| [PB-R4-012](#pb-r4-012) | Attention representation, router ordering, and strict-pin failure versus permitted fallback |
| [PB-R4-016](#pb-r4-016) | Sparse format and operation-specific preprocessing choices, including active-buffer lifetime |

<span id="pb-r0-001" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R0-001: Repair an Evidence Status record

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R0-001 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Core mental models; foundational.
- **Prerequisite:** [O02: Recording Evidence Honestly](/en/start/evidence-status/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; analyse a text record only.
- **Related Learning Unit:** O02.
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-26.

**Prompt:** A release note says: “The Linux build was skipped after the container registry timed out. A contributor pasted `PASS` from a personal GPU, so the project is Compile-Checked and Runtime-Verified. Web tests also prove that the browser explainer counts as a CUDA run.” Rewrite it as an honest record.

**Constraints:**

- Use only O02's controlled Evidence Status values.
- Invent no manifest, build log, observation date, or maintainer reproduction.
- Keep expected observations separate from recorded observations.

**Expected evidence:** A sentence-by-sentence status repair naming the evidence available for each claim, the missing material, and the status that remains.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- A blocked build has no Compile-Checked.
- A community `PASS` without a complete Environment Manifest, logs, criteria, and date grants no Community-Observed.
- No Runtime-Verified appears before maintainer reproduction; Pending Hardware Verification remains when GPU behavior is required.
- Web tests and browser models grant no CUDA Evidence Status.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>For every sentence, ask who did what and which reviewable artifact remains.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Blocked is not passed, and one `PASS` line is not a complete community report.</details>

**Solution:** “The declared compile work did not run because the container registry timed out, so there is no Compile-Checked. The community supplied an incompletely recorded `PASS` with no manifest, logs, criteria, or date, so Community-Observed cannot yet be assigned. Maintainers have not reproduced the subject in a declared Reference Environment; if acceptance requires GPU behavior, the runtime axis remains Pending Hardware Verification. Web-quality tests validate the site and grant no CUDA status. Neither expected nor recorded observations have a qualifying record.”

**Common errors:** Assigning status after calling the job blocked; omitting a manifest to be polite to a contributor; treating web CI as a CUDA compile; removing Pending Hardware Verification without maintainer evidence.

**Source basis:** The site's original [O02 contract](/en/start/evidence-status/) and the separation of compile and run phases in NVIDIA `nvcc` 13.3.1 [supported phases](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-compiler-driver-nvcc/index.html#supported-phases).

<span id="pb-r0-002" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R0-002: Complete a manifest and repair a support claim

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R0-002 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Debugging and correctness; foundational.
- **Prerequisite:** [O03: Reading an Environment Manifest](/en/start/environment-manifest/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; use a hypothetical record and run no CUDA.
- **Related Learning Unit:** O03.
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-26.

**Prompt:** A record contains only “8 GB GPU, CUDA 12, WSL, kernel 0.2 ms” and claims “complete tier; this site supports the environment.” List the missing manifest fields and repair both conclusions.

**Constraints:**

- Do not guess GPU model, compute capability, driver, Toolkit patch, or measurement method.
- Cover correctness and performance fields, but leave result unobserved.
- Apply only O03's Supported Environment and GPU Capability Tier definitions.

**Expected evidence:** A missing-field checklist, two repaired support and tier conclusions, and a note that isolates the method-free performance text as unreviewed raw input.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- The manifest separates GPU identity, compute capability, GPU count, driver, Toolkit, components, NVCC, host compiler, OS, workload and shape, memory, permissions, exact command, correctness, and observation date.
- The performance appendix has baseline, hypothesis, clocks or power, warm-up, synchronization, timer or profiler version, statistics and sample method, and interpretation boundaries.
- The conclusion states that WSL is not this site's Supported Environment and 8 GB alone cannot select the Modern Single-GPU Capability Tier.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Split “CUDA 12” into Toolkit patch, driver, and relevant component versions.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>`0.2 ms` is uninterpretable without synchronization, tool version, samples, and baseline.</details>

**Solution:** Create hardware, software, workload, execution, correctness, date, and performance groups. Mark every unknown “to collect” and name a query method. Move `0.2 ms` to unreviewed raw text and leave result empty. Repair support to: “Native Linux is the only Supported Environment; WSL is an unsupported comparison.” Repair the tier to: “Modern Single-GPU requires compute capability 8.0 or newer, at least 8 GB, and the required count, features, and permissions; the current record cannot decide.”

**Common errors:** Treating “CUDA 12” as a complete software coordinate; replacing compute capability with memory capacity; accepting latency without synchronization; converting NVIDIA product support into this site's responsibility.

**Source basis:** The site's original [O03 contract](/en/start/environment-manifest/), NVIDIA [CUDA Compatibility](https://docs.nvidia.com/deploy/cuda-compatibility/minor-version-compatibility.html), and the CUDA Programming Guide [compute capabilities](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/05-appendices/compute-capabilities.html).

<span id="pb-r0-003" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R0-003: Review an indexing prediction before execution

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R0-003 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Concepts and implementation; foundational.
- **Prerequisite:** [F01: From Prediction to a First CUDA Kernel](/en/foundations/first-cuda-kernel/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; use static reasoning only.
- **Related Learning Unit:** F01.
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-26.

**Prompt:** For one-dimensional vector addition with `N = 65` and `blockDim.x = 32`, give the block count, total launched thread count, final valid index, and bounds state of `threadIdx.x = 0` and `1` in the final block. Explain why a bounds check remains necessary.

**Constraints:** Write equations first, run no program, use zero-based indices, and do not equate launch extent with logical data extent.

**Expected evidence:** A coordinate table and an ownership explanation of no more than five sentences.

**Acceptance criteria:** Derive 3 blocks, 96 launched threads, and final valid index 64; identify local thread 0 in the last block as valid and 1 as out of bounds; explain that 31 out-of-bounds threads must skip access.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Use `ceil(65 / 32)` for block count.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>The final block has `blockIdx.x = 2`, so its global indices start at 64.</details>

**Solution:** `ceil(65 / 32) = 3`, giving 96 launched threads. The final valid element is index 64. Local thread 0 in the final block has global index 64 and is IN BOUNDS; local thread 1 has global index 65 and is OUT OF BOUNDS. Every remaining local thread is also out of bounds, so the kernel must guard access with `index < N`.

**Valid alternative:** A number line or VIS02-style static coordinate table is valid when equations and bounds reasons remain reviewable.

**Common errors:** Rounding down to two blocks, treating index 65 as the final element, or believing surplus threads were not launched.

**Source basis:** The original indexing model in [F01](/en/foundations/first-cuda-kernel/) and [VIS02](/en/visuals/indexing/), plus NVIDIA [Introduction to CUDA C++](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/02-basics/intro-to-cuda-cpp.html).

<span id="pb-r0-004" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R0-004: Repair incomplete error and correctness boundaries

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R0-004 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Debugging and correctness; foundational.
- **Prerequisite:** [F01](/en/foundations/first-cuda-kernel/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review a hypothetical flow.
- **Related Learning Units:** F01 and O02.
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-26.

**Prompt:** A program checks only `cudaMalloc` and final output text, then claims “no error and GPU-correct.” Write the missing boundaries and define the acceptance rule for one element.

**Constraints:** Use EX02's declared Runtime flow and tolerances, invent no output, and separate API success from numerical correctness.

**Expected evidence:** An execution-ordered checklist and one computable numerical acceptance condition.

**Acceptance criteria:** Include three allocations, two H2D copies, launch error, synchronization, D2H copy, CPU comparison, and three frees; accept when absolute `1e-5` or relative `1e-5` passes; explain why one output line cannot replace these checks.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Launch return and kernel completion are separate boundaries.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Failure requires both absolute and relative criteria to reject, not either one.</details>

**Solution:** After three `cudaMalloc` calls, check both H2D copies. After launch, call `cudaGetLastError`, then wait and check execution with `cudaDeviceSynchronize`. Check D2H copy, compare each element with an independent CPU reference, and check all three `cudaFree` calls. An element passes when `abs(expected-actual) <= 1e-5` **or** that difference is `<= 1e-5 * max(abs(expected), abs(actual))`. API success and numerical acceptance are both required.

**Valid alternative:** A state machine or sequence diagram may replace the checklist when it preserves the same order and two independent acceptance layers.

**Common errors:** Synchronizing without checking launch, combining tolerances with AND, or treating the host test as a GPU result.

**Source basis:** The canonical error/correctness contract in [F01](/en/foundations/first-cuda-kernel/) and [EX02](/en/examples/vector-addition/), plus NVIDIA [CUDA Runtime API](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/index.html).

<span id="pb-r0-005" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R0-005: Review a LAB02 evidence request

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R0-005 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Evidence review and Lab preparation; foundational.
- **Prerequisite:** [F01](/en/foundations/first-cuda-kernel/), with [O02](/en/start/evidence-status/) and [O03](/en/start/environment-manifest/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review a text record and run no CUDA.
- **Related Learning Units:** F01, O02, and O03.
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-26.

**Prompt:** A LAB02 request contains only “CUDA 13, vector add PASS, 0.01 ms” and asks for Runtime-Verified. Decide which status can be assigned now and list the completion path.

**Constraints:** Do not guess environment patch, GPU, or logs. Do not treat a performance number as correctness. Compile-Checked may refer only to the existing canonical EX02 Lane records.

**Expected evidence:** A status decision, gap list, and pre-run record template.

**Acceptance criteria:** Keep runtime Pending Hardware Verification; grant neither Community-Observed nor Runtime-Verified; require a complete manifest, exact commands, raw logs, date, and criterion result; label unsupported `0.01 ms` as uninterpretable raw text.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>A bare `PASS` lacks subject, criteria, environment, logs, and date.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Runtime-Verified also requires maintainer reproduction in a declared Reference Environment.</details>

**Solution:** The request grants neither Community-Observed nor Runtime-Verified; the runtime axis remains Pending Hardware Verification. Completion requires GPU identity, compute capability, count, driver, Toolkit patch, NVCC, host compiler, native Linux, workload, memory, permissions, exact commands, CPU/tolerance criteria, date, exit status, and raw logs. Move `0.01 ms` to uninterpretable raw text without synchronization, tool version, samples, and baseline. Evaluate Runtime-Verified only after maintainers reproduce the predefined criteria in a declared Reference Environment.

**Valid alternative:** A complete community record that meets its criteria may qualify for Community-Observed while Pending Hardware Verification remains. It is still not maintainer reproduction.

**Common errors:** Upgrading runtime because EX02 is Compile-Checked, treating unknown CUDA 13 as the 13.3.1 Lane, or preserving an unsupported performance number as an observation.

**Source basis:** The original evidence contracts in [LAB02](/en/labs/vector-addition/), [O02](/en/start/evidence-status/), and [O03](/en/start/environment-manifest/).

<span id="pb-r1-001" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-001: Repair lifetime, build, and error boundaries

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-001 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Debugging and correctness; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [O04: C++17 Refresher for CUDA Learners](/en/start/cpp17-for-cuda/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review source and build design only. No GPU is required.
- **Related Learning Unit:** O04.
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-26.

**Prompt:** A `.cu` patch saves `data()` from a local `std::vector<float>` in an asynchronous copy task, then returns before the task completes. An RAII owner for a CUDA allocation throws from its destructor when `cudaFree` fails. A `__host__ __device__` helper calls a throwing host-only function. CI compiles only the caller with a host compiler and declares that both host and device paths built. The runtime path wraps a kernel launch in `try/catch` but ignores `cudaError_t`, the launch check, and the completion check. Write the smallest complete repair.

**Constraints:**

- Run no build, sanitizer, or CUDA command, and invent no diagnostic or output.
- Treat pointee lifetime, ownership, build boundaries, and error boundaries independently.
- A raw pointer is a non-owning view. The repair must say when its owner may be destroyed.

**Expected evidence:** An ownership timeline through asynchronous completion, a host/device build-stage table, and an execution-ordered error checklist.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Identify that destroying the local vector first leaves the saved view dangling. Keep the owner alive through asynchronous completion or establish an explicit completion boundary before scope exit.
- Keep the CUDA owner's destructor non-throwing. Supply an explicit `close` or `reset` path when cleanup failure must remain observable.
- Separate host-only throwing code from the device-safe helper, expose device definitions to the `nvcc` path, and record compilation, optional device linking, and host linking separately. A successful host object proves neither the device path nor execution.
- Check every relevant `cudaError_t` and separate launch error from completion error. A host exception adapter may convert an error only after preserving the CUDA failure details.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Copying a raw pointer copies an address value. It does not extend the vector or allocation lifetime.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>`try/catch` observes host exceptions. CUDA Runtime failures first cross return-value, launch, and completion boundaries.</details>

**Solution:** Put the staging buffer and CUDA allocation in an operation owner that lives until a stream event or explicit synchronization confirms completion. The other minimal repair is to synchronize before the local owner is destroyed. Mark every raw pointer as a view and carry its extent and address-space meaning. Make the destructor perform non-throwing best-effort cleanup and expose explicit `close` when cleanup failure must be reported. Split the throwing host adapter from the device helper, compile the `.cu` file and device-visible definitions with `nvcc`, and record any device link separately from the final host link. Check allocation, copy, and other Runtime API results, inspect launch error after launch, and inspect completion at a synchronization or event boundary. Only a host adapter may throw, after retaining the error name, message, and call site. No build artifact proves that a GPU path ran.

**Valid alternative:** Use an operation object that owns asynchronous state, or make the copy complete synchronously within the current scope. Either choice must keep the owner alive through the final legal access and preserve the same build and error boundaries.

**Common errors:** Nulling only one pointer copy; making a non-owning view call `free`; throwing from a destructor; letting host-only `throw` enter the device path; treating host compilation, linking, or zero warnings as CUDA execution evidence.

**Source basis:** The site's original [O04 contract](/en/start/cpp17-for-cuda/), the [C++ Core Guidelines](https://isocpp.github.io/CppCoreGuidelines/CppCoreGuidelines), NVIDIA [CUDA Runtime API Error Handling](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/group__CUDART__ERROR.html), and the [`nvcc` compilation trajectory](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-compiler-driver-nvcc/index.html#the-cuda-compilation-trajectory).

<span id="pb-r1-002" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-002: Repair a build record that hides producer failure

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-002 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Debugging and reproducibility; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [O05: Reproducible Linux Command-Line Work](/en/start/linux-command-line/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; design the record only. No GPU is required.
- **Related Learning Unit:** O05.
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-26.

**Prompt:** A script runs `make -C "$build_dir" all 2>&1 | tee "$record_dir/build.log"`, writes the following `$?` to `build.exit`, and reports build success because that value is zero. It also saves the complete `env` and xtrace, but has no literal command, separate stdout and stderr, actual start and end times, or sealing hash. Repair the failure decision and design a minimal public record.

**Constraints:**

- Run no command and fill in no exit status, timestamp, log content, hash, or other invented output.
- Record no secret, token, credential, or unapproved full environment. Xtrace cannot replace literal input.
- The canonical record must preserve command, stdout, stderr, exit status, UTC timestamps, and SHA-256 hash boundaries.

**Expected evidence:** A failure-propagation explanation and an ordered artifact manifest stating when each file is written, closed, and included in hashing.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Explain that the original `$?` belongs to `tee` by default, so a successful consumer can hide a failed producer. `2>&1` also discards channel identity.
- Before execution, preserve a fixed `command.sh`, NUL-delimited argv, logical and physical working directories, and an allowlisted environment with no secrets.
- Capture UTC time before and after the stage, write stdout and stderr separately, and copy producer status before any other simple command.
- After logs close, hash a fixed artifact list with SHA-256. Do not include `SHA256SUMS` in itself, and do not claim that a hash proves command correctness.
- If the pipeline remains, enable `pipefail`, immediately copy `PIPESTATUS`, and preserve producer and `tee` status separately. A combined log is only a convenience view.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>A pipeline's default status comes from its final stage, not from the `make` command you care about.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Copy `$?` or `PIPESTATUS` into a shell variable before running `printf`, timestamp, or hash commands.</details>

**Solution:** The canonical path may remove `tee`. First preserve immutable `command.sh`, `argv.nul`, paths, and the allowlisted environment, then write `started-at.txt`. Run `make` with stdout and stderr redirected to separate files. Immediately execute `build_status=$?`; only then write that value to `build.exit` and capture `ended-at.txt`. After every stream closes, generate `SHA256SUMS` over an explicit list containing command, argv, paths, environment, times, stdout, stderr, and exit files, excluding the checksum file itself. The template defines names and ordering without supplying any unobserved value. Remove complete `env` and any xtrace that could expand credentials from the public record.

**Valid alternative:** If interactive display requires `tee`, use `pipefail` and copy `PIPESTATUS` before the next simple command, recording `make` and `tee` independently. A merged stream cannot recover stdout/stderr identity, so retain separate canonical channel artifacts or explicitly narrow the record's scope.

**Common errors:** Reading `$?` after `printf`; retaining only the aggregate pipeline status; treating a combined log as two channels; hashing files before they close; leaking secrets because a full environment or xtrace seems “more reproducible”; filling a template with a guessed `0` or time.

**Source basis:** The site's original [O05 contract](/en/start/linux-command-line/), the [GNU Bash 5.3 Reference Manual](https://www.gnu.org/software/bash/manual/bash.html), and [GNU Coreutils 9.11](https://www.gnu.org/software/coreutils/manual/coreutils.html).

<span id="pb-r1-003" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-003: Classify architecture claims and calculate bounded arithmetic intensity

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-003 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Architecture reasoning and quantitative analysis; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [O06: Architecture Refresher: Rate, Delay, and Data Movement](/en/start/architecture-refresher/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; use static classification and arithmetic only.
- **Related Learning Unit:** O06.
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-26.

**Prompt:** Classify each review draft as latency, throughput, bandwidth, concurrency, or occupancy, then repair its overreach: A. “Kernel latency is 40 us.” B. “One batch completes 2,000,000 items/s.” C. “64 MB of requested bytes crossed from device DRAM to an SM in 4 ms, so this is actual bandwidth.” D. “Two kernels use different streams, so they executed concurrently.” E. “Occupancy is 75%, so the kernel reached 75% of peak throughput.” Then calculate a separate static model: `N = 4096`; each element counts exactly one multiply and one add; at the declared device-DRAM boundary, each element has exactly one 4-byte read and one 4-byte write, with no other traffic counted by the model.

**Constraints:**

- The drafts are not recorded observations. Add no timer, profiler, timeline, device, or workload facts.
- Arithmetic intensity must declare its operation-count rule, memory boundary, read/write byte convention, and workload scope.
- Infer no achieved performance, memory-bound diagnosis, speedup, overlap, or utilization from the calculation.

**Expected evidence:** A five-row claim-classification table, one complete intensity equation, and a scope statement naming what neither artifact guarantees.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- A becomes latency only after naming start and end events and units. B is throughput and needs a work amount and time window.
- C has the shape of requested or effective bandwidth with a path and direction, not profiler-attributed actual bandwidth. It also needs a fixed MB convention and elapsed boundary.
- D says only that independent work in different streams may be eligible to overlap; concurrency requires a timeline. E is an active-warp to maximum-active-warp ratio, not a throughput percentage.
- Derive `8192 operations / 32768 bytes = 0.25 operations/byte` and restrict it to the supplied counting rule and device-DRAM boundary.
- Make no bottleneck, runtime, bandwidth-ceiling, or performance-ranking claim.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>For each draft, ask whether its subject is one event, a time window, a data path, temporal overlap, or a resident-warp ratio.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Each element contributes 2 counted operations and 8 boundary bytes.</details>

**Solution:** A is a latency draft missing start and end boundaries. B is a throughput draft missing a precise window and workload scope. C uses requested bytes, so it can form an effective-bandwidth calculation only after path, direction, time, and MB meaning are complete; it is not actual bandwidth. D establishes overlap eligibility, not observed concurrency. E is an occupancy value and cannot be converted into achieved throughput. In the static model, the operation count is `4096 * 2 = 8192` and the byte count at the declared boundary is `4096 * (4 + 4) = 32768`, so `I = 8192 / 32768 = 0.25 operations/byte`. The ratio measures neither traffic nor time and supplies no compute or bandwidth ceiling, so it supports no performance conclusion.

**Valid alternative:** Replace the classification table with a claim map containing subject, boundary, unit, established conclusion, and excluded conclusion, provided the five terms and intensity convention stay distinct.

**Common errors:** Treating throughput as single-request latency; omitting the bandwidth path; inferring overlap from different streams; treating occupancy as utilization; replacing the declared DRAM boundary with cache traffic; declaring memory-bound behavior from intensity alone.

**Source basis:** The site's original [O06 terminology and model boundaries](/en/start/architecture-refresher/), CUDA C++ Best Practices Guide v13.3 [Bandwidth](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-c-best-practices-guide/index.html#bandwidth) and [Occupancy](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-c-best-practices-guide/index.html#occupancy), and the [primary Roofline technical report](https://www.osti.gov/biblio/1407078) by Williams, Waterman, and Patterson.

<span id="pb-r1-004" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-004: Replace a product chronology with abstraction history

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-004 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** History and architecture explanation; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [O07: Why GPUs Became Programmable](/en/start/programmable-gpus/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; rewrite concepts and attribute sources only.
- **Related Learning Unit:** O07.
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-26.

**Prompt:** Rewrite this statement as an abstraction-pressure explanation: “Every new GPU generation made the last obsolete. Shaders turned graphics chips into general processors, and Brook and CUDA merely kept making GPUs faster than CPUs for every task.” Proceed through fixed-function state, programmable graphics stages, general-purpose computing on graphics processing units, or GPGPU, and CUDA in that order. Attribute concrete historical facts to primary sources.

**Constraints:**

- For every transition, state the previous abstraction's expressive pressure, the interface response, and the boundary that remained.
- Make no product ranking, universal-speed claim, complete-CPU-replacement claim, unsourced “first,” or date-only causal argument.
- The claim-to-source map may use only owner sources already cited by O07, with each concrete fact traceable.

**Expected evidence:** A four-stage causal paragraph followed by a claim-to-source map. Both must keep interface capability separate from performance conclusions.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Fixed-function state configures predefined operations. Pressure to express new effects leads to limited programmable stages while graphics invocation, inputs, outputs, and the surrounding pipeline remain.
- GPGPU maps arrays, computation, and results onto textures, fragment invocation, and render targets. Brook supplies a stream and kernel surface while its implementation still passes through graphics mechanisms.
- CUDA makes heterogeneous kernels, grids, thread blocks, memory locality, and scheduling the primary model, connecting block independence with scalable scheduling.
- Claim no universal GPU or CUDA speed advantage. Attribute specific facts to the Khronos specifications, Brook project or paper, CUDA Programming Guide, and the 2008 CUDA paper.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Do not begin with “which product appeared in which year.” Begin with “what could programmers not yet express naturally?”</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>“Programmable” is not identical to “general purpose.” Check who controls invocation, input, output, and surrounding stages.</details>

**Solution:** Fixed-function graphics interfaces efficiently sent many graphics items through a specialized pipeline, but new effects had to be selected from a predefined menu, creating pressure for an application-controlled instruction surface. Vertex and fragment programs opened selected stages, and Cg improved source-level expression, while invocation, resource limits, and surrounding graphics semantics remained pipeline-controlled. GPGPU then encoded nongraphics problems as textures, fragment work, and render targets. Brook hid part of that mapping behind streams and kernels but still relied on graphics mechanisms. CUDA reframed the problem around host/device division, kernels, grids, thread blocks, and explicit memory locality. Block independence lets the runtime schedule work across different resource counts. This abstraction shift expands what can be expressed; it proves no universal workload speedup. Attribute programmable-stage claims to the Khronos ARB specifications and programmable vertex-engine paper, Brook claims to Stanford BrookGPU and its paper, and CUDA claims to the CUDA Programming Guide Introduction and Nickolls et al. 2008 paper.

**Valid alternative:** Use a four-column pressure, response, retained-boundary, and source table instead of continuous prose, but do not reduce it to products and dates.

**Common errors:** Treating a shader as an unconstrained general-purpose interface; conflating the target surfaces of Cg and Brook; calling graphics-mediated GPGPU CUDA; substituting release date for causation; appending an unsupported “GPUs are always faster” conclusion.

**Source basis:** The site's original [O07 abstraction-pressure framework](/en/start/programmable-gpus/), the Khronos [ARB_vertex_program](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/extensions/ARB/ARB_vertex_program.txt) and [ARB_fragment_program](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/extensions/ARB/ARB_fragment_program.txt) specifications, the [Stanford BrookGPU project](https://graphics.stanford.edu/projects/brookgpu/), [CUDA Programming Guide v13.3 Introduction](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/01-introduction/introduction.html), and the [2008 CUDA paper by Nickolls et al.](https://doi.org/10.1145/1365490.1365500).

<span id="pb-r1-005" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-005: Triage an incomplete Reference Environment candidate

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-005 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Environment review and evidence boundaries; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [O08: Preparing a Reference Environment Candidate](/en/start/reference-environment-candidate/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review candidate material and run no CUDA.
- **Related Learning Unit:** O08.
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-26.

**Prompt:** A Reference Environment candidate packet contains only “Ubuntu 24.04, 12 GB GPU,” a screenshot with a `CUDA Version: 13.0` banner, one `vector add PASS` line, and a temporary hosted-notebook shell. It records no native or container boundary, device identity or count, direct compute-capability query, Driver KMD, installed Toolkit or components, selected Toolkit Lane or target, exact command, logs, predeclared criteria, maintainer control, or separate baseline. It requests the Modern Single-GPU Capability Tier, a Reference Environment declaration, and Runtime-Verified. Review every gate.

**Constraints:**

- Infer no missing coordinate from a product name, memory capacity, or banner. Guess no compatibility path and add no observation.
- Apply O08's scope, manifest, tier, compatibility, control, baseline, and declaration gates in order.
- Make no Reference Environment declaration, Evidence Status upgrade, or CUDA execution.

**Expected evidence:** A gate table with known facts, missing facts, current decision, and next action, followed by an explicit declaration and status conclusion.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Supported scope is not established because the native or container boundary is unknown. The manifest lacks hardware, software, workload, execution, correctness, and custody coordinates.
- Tier remains indeterminate because 12 GB does not replace direct compute capability, count, features, and permissions. Do not assign the Modern Single-GPU Capability Tier.
- Compatibility remains indeterminate. The `CUDA Version` banner cannot fill the installed Toolkit field, and KMD, Toolkit or components, Lane, target or PTX dependency, and path restrictions are absent.
- A temporary shell does not establish maintainer control. A `PASS` line is not a separately specified baseline with predeclared criteria and retained logs.
- Keep the candidate incomplete and undeclared. Grant no Community-Observed, Runtime-Verified, or other status upgrade.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Separate “can open a shell” from “can pin the configuration, schedule exclusive validation, reboot, and retain evidence.”</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>The banner, Toolkit, KMD, and driver-supported CUDA API are distinct coordinates. A successful baseline also needs criteria written before execution.</details>

**Solution:** The packet does not first establish that the candidate lies inside the site's native Linux Supported Environment, and no complete manifest exists. Twelve GB supplies only one capacity fragment and cannot select a tier. The banner does not identify the installed Toolkit. Without KMD, exact Toolkit or components, selected Lane, target, and path-specific restrictions, the compatibility outcome is `indeterminate`. A temporary notebook does not show that maintainers can pin the stack, control permissions, reboot, or retain the machine and artifacts, so the control gate fails. `vector add PASS` has no separate subject, revision, command, input, correctness method, predeclared criteria, exit status, date, or raw logs, so there is no baseline. Confirm scope first, complete the manifest and direct query, then select a tier, review compatibility against current owner documentation, establish maintainer control, and predeclare a baseline only after all earlier gates are complete. Until then, preserve an undeclared candidate and upgrade no status.

**Valid alternative:** A stop/go checklist or decision tree is acceptable when unknown values never default to pass and baseline remains separate from declaration.

**Common errors:** Guessing compute capability from memory; treating an `nvidia-smi` banner as the Toolkit; treating temporary login as maintainer control; adding criteria after execution; using one `PASS` line to cross baseline, declaration, and Runtime-Verified boundaries at once.

**Source basis:** The site's original [O08 candidate and declaration gates](/en/start/reference-environment-candidate/), NVIDIA [CUDA Toolkit Archive](https://developer.nvidia.com/cuda-toolkit-archive), [Minor Version Compatibility](https://docs.nvidia.com/deploy/cuda-compatibility/minor-version-compatibility.html), CUDA Programming Guide [Obtain the GPU Compute Capability](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/05-appendices/compute-capabilities.html#obtain-the-gpu-compute-capability), CUDA Runtime API [Version Management](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/group__CUDART____VERSION.html), and the [NVIDIA-SMI documentation](https://docs.nvidia.com/deploy/nvidia-smi/index.html).

<span id="pb-r1-006" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-006: Predict thread ownership and hierarchy identities in a partial 2D launch

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-006 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Execution-hierarchy reasoning and correctness; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [F02: Understanding the CUDA Execution Hierarchy](/en/foundations/execution-hierarchy/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; use static calculations only and run no CUDA.
- **Related Learning Unit and resources:** [F02](/en/foundations/execution-hierarchy/), [VIS01](/en/visuals/kernel-journey/), [VIS02](/en/visuals/indexing/), and [EX03](/en/examples/multidimensional-indexing/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-26.

**Prompt:** An x-fastest, row-major logical array has width `19` and height `9`. It uses `blockDim = (8, 5)`, with `gridDim` obtained by ceiling division on each axis. For `threadIdx = (2, 3)`, `(3, 3)`, `(2, 4)`, and `(7, 4)` in boundary block `blockIdx = (2, 1)`, calculate the global coordinate, x-fastest linear block ID, block-local linear thread ID, `warpInBlock`, lane, and data ownership. Calculate a row-major `dataIndex` only for a thread that owns data. Finish by explaining why these identities are neither a schedule nor automatic data ownership.

**Constraints:**

- Write the per-axis ceiling-division, global-coordinate, linear-block, local-thread, warp, and lane equations first.
- Check x and y independently before deciding whether to calculate an accessible `dataIndex`. Do not clamp coordinates or equate launch extent with logical extent.
- Execute no CUDA, invent no output or performance, and infer no start or completion order from block, warp, or lane values.

**Expected evidence:** A four-row table containing block, global, local, warp, and lane identities; per-axis bounds; ownership; and optional `dataIndex`, followed by a paragraph separating identity from scheduling.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Derive `gridDim = (3, 2)`, 40 threads per block, and `linearBlock = 2 + 3 * 1 = 5` for the boundary block.
- Map `(2, 3)` to global `(18, 8)`, local `26`, warp `0`, and lane `26`. It owns the final logical element at `dataIndex = 170`.
- Map `(3, 3)` to `(19, 8)`, local `27`, warp `0`, lane `27`, out of bounds only on x; map `(2, 4)` to `(18, 9)`, local `34`, warp `1`, lane `2`, out of bounds only on y; map `(7, 4)` to `(23, 9)`, local `39`, warp `1`, lane `7`, out of bounds on both x and y. None receives an accessible `dataIndex`.
- State that only `3 * 4 = 12` threads in this boundary block own data, while the other 28 are still launched and must skip access. No identity number promises scheduling order.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>This block's global origin is `(2 * 8, 1 * 5) = (16, 5)`. Its valid local x values are only `0..2`, and its valid local y values are only `0..3`.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Use `localThread = threadIdx.x + 8 * threadIdx.y`, then calculate `floor(localThread / 32)` and `localThread % 32` separately.</details>

**Solution:** Per-axis ceiling division gives `gridDim = (ceil(19 / 8), ceil(9 / 5)) = (3, 2)`. X-fastest 2D block linearization gives `linearBlock = blockIdx.x + gridDim.x * blockIdx.y = 5`. All four threads belong to that block, but their local identities are 26, 27, 34, and 39, with `(warp, lane)` values `(0, 26)`, `(0, 27)`, `(1, 2)`, and `(1, 7)`. Their global coordinates are `(18, 8)`, `(19, 8)`, `(18, 9)`, and `(23, 9)`. Only `(18, 8)` satisfies both `gx < 19` and `gy < 9`, so only it owns data, at `dataIndex = 18 + 19 * 8 = 170`; the other three stop before flattening or access. The boundary block's valid local region is 3 columns by 4 rows, so 12 threads own data and 28 do not. Block, local-thread, warp, and lane numbers describe hierarchy identity, not block scheduling, warp completion order, or data validity.

**Valid alternative:** An annotated `8 x 5` block diagram or identity ledger may replace the table when the equations, per-axis bounds, warp and lane values, and sole valid `dataIndex` remain reviewable for all four threads.

**Common errors:** Rounding `gridDim` down; directly adding 2D `threadIdx` components; turning local ID 34 into data index 34; assuming every thread in warp 1 is out of bounds; inferring execution order from block ID 5 or warp ID 0; flattening and accessing an invalid coordinate.

**Source basis:** The site's original hierarchy and ownership contract in [F02](/en/foundations/execution-hierarchy/), scheduling boundary in [VIS01](/en/visuals/kernel-journey/), coordinate model in [VIS02](/en/visuals/indexing/), and application-layout boundary in [EX03](/en/examples/multidimensional-indexing/), plus NVIDIA [Writing CUDA SIMT Kernels](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/02-basics/writing-cuda-kernels.html).

<span id="pb-r1-007" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-007: Repair a 3D bounds defect that flattens too early

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-007 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Debugging and multidimensional correctness; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [F03: Make Multidimensional Indexing and Bounds a Correctness Contract](/en/foundations/multidimensional-indexing/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review and repair source only. Run no CUDA.
- **Related Learning Unit and resources:** [F03](/en/foundations/multidimensional-indexing/), [VIS02](/en/visuals/indexing/), and [EX03](/en/examples/multidimensional-indexing/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-26.

**Prompt:** A 3D kernel calculates `gx`, `gy`, and `gz`, but checks only x, then flattens and accesses before y and z have passed their bounds:

```cpp
if (gx >= width) {
  return;
}

const std::size_t index = ((gz * height) + gy) * width + gx;
output[index] = input[index];
```

Repair the order while preserving an x-fastest row-major layout. Then, for `width = 13`, `height = 6`, `depth = 5`, and `blockDim = (5, 4, 2)`, calculate `gridDim` and classify `threadIdx = (2, 1, 0)`, `(3, 1, 0)`, `(2, 2, 0)`, and `(2, 1, 1)` in boundary block `blockIdx = (2, 1, 2)`. Give a linear index only for a legal coordinate.

**Constraints:**

- Preserve the per-axis global coordinates, logical extents, and `((gz * height) + gy) * width + gx` mapping. Do not clamp, change an extent, or shrink the launch to hide the fringe.
- Any invalid active axis must stop the thread before flattening or any array access. The index type must hold the complete element count.
- Execute no CUDA, supply no output or performance, and do not convert a static patch, host-only check, or browser model into CUDA Evidence Status.

**Expected evidence:** A minimal source patch, a four-row per-axis bounds table, and one sentence stating that the static review produced no compilation or runtime evidence.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Repair the guard with an early return on `gx >= width || gy >= height || gz >= depth`, or surround flattening and access with all three positive `<` predicates.
- Derive `gridDim = (3, 2, 3)`. The four local coordinates map to global `(12, 5, 4)`, `(13, 5, 4)`, `(12, 6, 4)`, and `(12, 5, 5)`.
- Only `(12, 5, 4)` is legal, with `dataIndex = ((4 * 6) + 5) * 13 + 12 = 389`. The remaining coordinates fail only x, y, and z respectively and stop before flattening.
- Do not treat an integer produced by a flattening expression as proof of legal access, and claim neither Compile-Checked nor Runtime-Verified from the repair itself.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>An early return joins the three invalid predicates, so one true predicate must return.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>The boundary block's global origin is `(10, 4, 4)`. The final legal element has coordinate `(12, 5, 4)`.</details>

**Solution:** The minimal repair moves the complete guard before flattening:

```cpp
if (gx >= width || gy >= height || gz >= depth) {
  return;
}

const std::size_t index = ((gz * height) + gy) * width + gx;
output[index] = input[index];
```

Per-axis ceiling division gives `gridDim = (ceil(13 / 5), ceil(6 / 4), ceil(5 / 2)) = (3, 2, 3)`. The selected block starts at `(10, 4, 4)`, so the global coordinates are `(12, 5, 4)`, `(13, 5, 4)`, `(12, 6, 4)`, and `(12, 5, 5)`. The first passes all three bounds and maps to index 389, the final element among 390. The other three fail x, y, and z respectively and may neither calculate an accessible index nor touch the arrays. Source review and hand calculation establish only the repair rationale. They compile or execute no CUDA and create no Evidence Status.

**Valid alternative:** Surround flattening and every access with `gx < width && gy < height && gz < depth`. This is equivalent to the early return when no flattening or array operation remains outside the condition.

**Common errors:** Joining invalid predicates with `&&`; retaining an x-only guard; flattening before checking y and z; applying modulo or clamping to invalid coordinates; treating CUDA geometry as the source of row-major layout; treating host-only or VIS02 results as device execution.

**Source basis:** The site's original per-axis bounds and application-layout contract in [F03](/en/foundations/multidimensional-indexing/), static indexing model in [VIS02](/en/visuals/indexing/), and canonical indexing and evidence boundaries in [EX03](/en/examples/multidimensional-indexing/), plus NVIDIA [Writing CUDA SIMT Kernels](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/02-basics/writing-cuda-kernels.html).

<span id="pb-r1-008" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-008: Rebuild a host-device lifecycle and repair its evidence claims

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-008 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Lifecycle debugging and evidence review; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [F04: The Explicit Host-Device Resource Lifecycle](/en/foundations/host-device-lifecycle/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review ordering, ownership, and public evidence only. Run no CUDA.
- **Related Learning Units and resource:** [F04](/en/foundations/host-device-lifecycle/), [O02](/en/start/evidence-status/), [O04](/en/start/cpp17-for-cuda/), and [EX03](/en/examples/multidimensional-indexing/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-26.

**Prompt:** A single-kernel review packet shuffles its actions into this order: `cudaFree(d_output)`, D2H copy-back, initialize `h_input` and `h_expected` plus `h_output` storage, kernel launch, host comparison followed by immediate return, H2D transfer, `cudaDeviceSynchronize`, allocate `d_input` and `d_output`, `cudaGetLastError`, and `cudaFree(d_input)`. Rebuild the canonical success path. For the host containers, device-pointer handles stored on the host, and two device allocations, mark owner, acquisition state, last use, release responsibility, and return boundary. The packet also claims: “Source, a website build, and host-only checks are enough to make EX03 Compile-Checked; a static lifecycle ledger is enough to make it Runtime-Verified.” Repair both claims.

**Constraints:**

- Run no build, host test, or CUDA command, and invent no log, output, timing, or performance.
- Keep a pointer value separate from the allocation it names. Neither the kernel nor `cudaMemcpy` acquires allocation ownership.
- Release each successfully acquired allocation exactly once and only after last use. Save the comparison verdict first and return only after cleanup.
- Base Evidence Status conclusions on the current public records in [O02](/en/start/evidence-status/) and [EX03](/en/examples/multidimensional-indexing/). Do not upgrade a static review into an observation.

**Expected evidence:** An ordered lifecycle ledger with at least action, owner, state before, state after, last use, and release or return obligation columns, followed by a Compile-Checked and runtime claim-repair table.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Order host initialization, both checked device allocations, H2D, launch, `cudaGetLastError`, `cudaDeviceSynchronize`, D2H, host comparison with a saved verdict, release of both acquired allocations, and return of the verdict.
- The host C++ scope owns `h_input`, `h_output`, and `h_expected`; host pointer variables are only handles. Host control flow owns each release obligation created by successful `cudaMalloc`, while the kernel uses buffers without taking ownership.
- In the stated flow, successful H2D ends `h_input`'s last use, the successful completion boundary ends `d_input`'s last use, successful D2H ends `d_output`'s last use, and comparison ends this use of `h_output` and `h_expected`. Device allocations are released explicitly; host containers are released when their C++ owners leave scope. A failure path releases only acquired allocations and never reads a result that was not established.
- EX03's compilation-evidence array is empty, so it is not Compile-Checked. Source availability, a website build, and host-only checks do not populate that axis. EX03 runtime remains Pending Hardware Verification with empty recorded observations and no declared Reference Environment or qualifying GPU execution, so it is not Runtime-Verified.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>A successful `cudaMalloc` creates both a usable allocation and a release obligation. Letting the host pointer handle leave scope does not replace `cudaFree`.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>The launch check covers submission only. Device-input last use ends after the successful completion check, while device-output last use ends after successful D2H.</details>

**Solution:** First establish host input, independent expected data, and output storage. Then acquire and check `d_input` and `d_output` separately. Successful H2D ends `h_input`'s last use in this stated flow. Next submit the kernel, immediately check `cudaGetLastError`, and then check `cudaDeviceSynchronize`. Perform D2H only after successful completion, and compare `h_output` with `h_expected` only after successful D2H, saving the verdict. Call and check `cudaFree` once for each acquired device allocation, then return the saved verdict; the host containers are released when their C++ owners leave scope. A failure-safe variant stops creating dependencies at the failure point and cleans up only items marked acquired in the ledger. Host variables `d_input` and `d_output` are only handles, and host control flow, not the kernel, carries each device-allocation release obligation. Repair the evidence statement to: “EX03 has no qualifying Lane compile record and its compilation axis is empty, so it is not Compile-Checked. A static ledger, source, website build, and host-only checks execute no GPU kernel. EX03 runtime remains Pending Hardware Verification and cannot be declared Runtime-Verified.”

**Valid alternative:** Use one cleanup block or an explicit non-throwing RAII owner as reviewed in [O04](/en/start/cpp17-for-cuda/). Either form must preserve acquisition state, last-use boundaries, exactly-once release, saved-verdict then cleanup then return, and the independent evidence decision.

**Common errors:** Believing pointer copying duplicates or transfers allocation ownership; releasing input before synchronization; releasing output before D2H; returning immediately on comparison mismatch and skipping cleanup; double-freeing an unacquired or released resource; treating a website build, host-only test, or static table as an EX03 compilation or runtime observation.

**Source basis:** The site's original explicit lifecycle in [F04](/en/foundations/host-device-lifecycle/), lifetime and ownership boundaries in [O04](/en/start/cpp17-for-cuda/), evidence contract in [O02](/en/start/evidence-status/), and canonical lifecycle and current evidence record in [EX03](/en/examples/multidimensional-indexing/), plus NVIDIA [CUDA Runtime API](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/index.html).

<span id="pb-r1-009" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-009: Repair stale, immediate, and deferred error attribution

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-009 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Error-lifecycle debugging; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [F05: CUDA Errors Are Often Asynchronous](/en/foundations/asynchronous-errors/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review a static error-lifecycle record and run no CUDA.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [F05](/en/foundations/asynchronous-errors/), [VIS19 Error-Surfacing Timeline](/en/foundations/asynchronous-errors/#vis19), [EX04](/en/examples/error-handling-lifecycle/), and [LAB03](/en/labs/break-and-repair-indexing/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-26.

**Prompt:** One host thread first records a failed Runtime operation but does not consume its last-error state. A pre-launch `cudaPeekAtLastError` observes that unspecified old failure and is ignored. The thread then submits a prevalidated legal no-op launch; the adjacent `cudaGetLastError` returns the same non-success state, and the reviewer blames the new launch. Because the getter resets the state, a second interval begins cleanly: a launch with a deliberate device-access defect is accepted, its adjacent `cudaGetLastError` returns success, and `cudaDeviceSynchronize` later returns failure. The reviewer now says synchronization caused the defect. Rebuild both intervals with correct origin, observation, state-transition, and control-flow labels.

**Constraints:**

- Keep direct `cudaError_t` returns separate from last-error state, and state whether each operation reads, resets, or waits.
- Do not assign a fixed enum value or diagnostic string to either hypothetical failure.
- Stop dependent D2H and verification work after a failed launch or completion boundary; invent no runtime output or Evidence Status.

**Expected evidence:** A two-interval ledger with columns for prior state, operation, direct return or queried state, reset effect, completion effect, attributed origin, and permitted next step, followed by two repaired reviewer conclusions.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Classify the first adjacent getter as observing stale state because the same failure was visible before the legal target launch; do not attribute it to that launch.
- State that `cudaPeekAtLastError` observes without resetting, while `cudaGetLastError` observes and resets to `cudaSuccess`; neither establishes device completion.
- Classify the second immediate success as launch-boundary information only, then place the deliberate access defect at device execution and its host observation at the synchronization return.
- Treat `cudaDeviceSynchronize` as the reporting/completion boundary, not automatically the origin, and claim no compilation or runtime evidence from the static repair.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>If a non-success value is already visible before the target launch, source-line proximity after the launch cannot establish new attribution.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Draw separate rows for launch submission, the adjacent getter, device execution, and synchronization. Only one of those rows waits for preceding device work.</details>

**Solution:** In interval one, the old failed Runtime operation creates the state; the pre-launch peek proves that state predates the target launch and leaves it unchanged. The legal no-op launch does not establish a new failure, and the adjacent getter retrieves the still-stale item and resets the state. The correct conclusion is “an earlier failure remained unconsumed; this trace cannot blame the target launch.” In interval two, the clean starting state and successful adjacent getter show only that no immediate launch failure was observed. The accepted kernel later reaches the deliberate invalid access during device execution. `cudaDeviceSynchronize` waits for preceding work and returns that deferred failure, so D2H and host verification must not proceed. The correct conclusion is “the execution defect originated on the device and became host-visible at the completion boundary.” Neither interval supplies an actual run record.

**Valid alternative:** Run each diagnostic scenario in a separate process, as EX04/LAB03 require, and still record the preflight state plus direct synchronization return. Process isolation narrows attribution but does not turn an immediate getter into a completion check.

**Common errors:** Silently discarding a preflight error; treating last-error state as one queue per kernel; saying `cudaGetLastError` synchronizes; treating immediate success as successful execution; blaming the synchronization call for the device defect; continuing to copy or verify output after failed completion.

**Source basis:** The site's original [F05 error-state and observation contract](/en/foundations/asynchronous-errors/), [EX04 scenario contract](/en/examples/error-handling-lifecycle/), and [SRC-CUDA-015 record](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-015), plus NVIDIA Runtime API [Error Handling](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/group__CUDART__ERROR.html) and [API synchronization behavior](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/api-sync-behavior.html).

<span id="pb-r1-010" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-010: Rebuild a capability-to-compiler-target decision

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-010 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Capability and compiler-target review; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [F06: Compute Capability Is a Feature Contract](/en/foundations/compute-capability/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review capability, limit, and compiler records without probing a GPU.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [F06](/en/foundations/compute-capability/), [VIS20 Compute-Capability Contract Filter](/en/foundations/compute-capability/#vis20), [O03](/en/start/environment-manifest/), and [F08](/en/foundations/launch-geometry/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-26.

**Prompt:** A packet directly identifies one selected GPU and records compute capability 9.0. Its kernel requires an architecture-specific 9.0 feature. The capability table records 1024 as the device maximum threads per block, and the proposed block is `(32, 32, 1)`. The packet chooses baseline `compute_90` plus `sm_90`, says the same pair must work in NVCC 11.8.0, the 12.9.2 archive, and the selected Toolkit 13.3.1, and declares the block “recommended and faster” because its product equals the maximum. Repair the feature, limit, target, and unresolved-environment decisions.

**Constraints:**

- Preserve GPU identity, compute capability, required feature set, numeric limits, Toolkit, NVCC, virtual target, real target, driver, artifact, and runtime result as independent fields.
- Use only the selected target rows reviewed by F06; do not infer compiler support from the GPU or from numeric ordering.
- Make no performance, compatibility, compilation, or runtime claim from a maximum or documentation lookup.

**Expected evidence:** A decision ledger for the three NVCC coordinates, one block-limit clause, and a final list of facts still required before build, launch, correctness, or measurement conclusions.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Replace the baseline target pair with `compute_90a` / `sm_90a` because the stated source requirement uses the architecture-specific 9.0 set, and restrict that code to exact capability 9.0.
- Record that the selected NVCC 11.8.0 subset has no qualified target pair, while the 12.9.2 archive and selected Toolkit 13.3.1 coordinates list `compute_90a` / `sm_90a`; reject one unchanged three-Lane plan.
- Classify 1024 as a device validity maximum. `(32, 32, 1)` reaches that aggregate and passes only if every axis, function-specific limit, register/shared-memory requirement, and launch boundary also passes; it is not a recommendation.
- Leave driver loading, embedded artifact code, host compiler, OS, permissions, build result, correctness, and performance unresolved.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>`compute_90` names the baseline virtual contract. The suffix is part of the feature scope, not decorative spelling.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Read the target matrix by compiler row before reading the device limit. A compiler's accepted targets and a GPU's thread maximum answer different questions.</details>

**Solution:** The direct query establishes capability 9.0 for the selected GPU and selects the applicable feature and limit rows, but it does not identify any compiler. Because the kernel requires the architecture-specific set, baseline `compute_90` / `sm_90` is semantically insufficient; the reviewable plan is `compute_90a` / `sm_90a`, exact to 9.0. F06's selected matrix lists no qualified pair for NVCC 11.8.0 and lists that pair for the 12.9.2 archive and selected Toolkit 13.3.1, so the required source cannot use one unchanged target plan across all three Lanes. The 1024-thread device maximum makes the `(32, 32, 1)` product only provisionally device-legal. Function attributes and other resources can still make it illegal, and equality with a ceiling predicts no speed. Each viable compiler plan still needs a build record, artifact/driver review, actual launch and completion checks, result verification, and a separate measurement contract.

**Valid alternative:** Remove or replace the architecture-specific source dependency, then review a baseline `compute_90` / `sm_90` plan independently in each exact compiler. That broadens scope by changing the program requirement; it is not a suffix-free spelling of the original program.

**Common errors:** Guessing capability from a product name; treating a feature row as a compiler target list; pairing `compute_90a` with baseline `sm_90`; assuming a larger capability number accepts exact-9.0 features; turning `maxThreadsPerBlock` into a preferred block size; claiming all Toolkit Lanes built because their documentation was read.

**Source basis:** The site's original [F06 decision chain](/en/foundations/compute-capability/) and [SRC-CUDA-016 record](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-016), NVIDIA [Compute Capabilities and Feature Set Compiler Targets](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/05-appendices/compute-capabilities.html#feature-set-compiler-targets), and the exact [NVCC 11.8.0](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/11.8.0/cuda-compiler-driver-nvcc/index.html), [12.9.2 archive](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/12.9.2/cuda-compiler-driver-nvcc/index.html), and [current NVCC](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-compiler-driver-nvcc/index.html) owner pages.

<span id="pb-r1-011" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-011: Repair Runtime/Driver ownership and error boundaries

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-011 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Mixed-API lifecycle debugging; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [F07: Distinguish CUDA Runtime API and Driver API Roles](/en/foundations/runtime-driver-api/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review a mixed-API design and run no CUDA.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [F04](/en/foundations/host-device-lifecycle/), [F05](/en/foundations/asynchronous-errors/), [F07](/en/foundations/runtime-driver-api/), [VIS21 Runtime/Driver API Boundary](/en/foundations/runtime-driver-api/#vis21), and [EX04](/en/examples/error-handling-lifecycle/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-26.

**Prompt:** A mixed design calls `cudaSetDevice` and `cudaMalloc`, then `cuInit(0)`, obtains the current context with `cuCtxGetCurrent`, loads a `CUmodule`, resolves a `CUfunction`, and calls `cuLaunchKernel`. The helper labels the obtained context “owned,” ignores every `CUresult` because a later `cudaGetLastError` is supposed to cover them, unloads the module, calls `cuCtxDestroy` on the obtained context, then calls `cudaDeviceSynchronize` and `cudaFree`. Repair initialization, current-context ownership, module/function lifetime, direct status checks, asynchronous completion, and teardown authority.

**Constraints:**

- Keep Runtime `cudaError_t`, Driver `CUresult`, and Runtime last-error state as distinct observation channels.
- Do not grant context-destruction authority merely because a handle was queried; every release must match an explicit acquisition/ownership rule.
- Preserve allocation, module, function, and context validity through the launch's completion boundary; invent no output or performance result.

**Expected evidence:** An owner/lifetime ledger for context, allocation, module, and function; a corrected ordered flow; and separate submission, completion, and attribution clauses.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Check `cuInit(0)` and each Driver call's own `CUresult`, and check each Runtime call's own `cudaError_t`; do not use `cudaGetLastError` as a substitute for `cuLaunchKernel`'s direct return.
- Treat the context returned by `cuCtxGetCurrent` as a current handle with no demonstrated teardown authority in this packet. Keep it current and valid, and do not call `cuCtxDestroy` unless a matching context-creation ownership path is documented.
- Keep the application-owned `CUmodule` loaded and its `CUfunction` valid through completion. Synchronize through one documented boundary in the same context, inspect that return directly, then unload the module and free the Runtime-owned allocation after last use.
- State that Driver submission can still be followed by a deferred execution error at context, stream, or event completion; API choice does not remove F05's attribution boundary.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>A queried current handle answers “which context applies?” It does not answer “which helper may destroy shared state?”</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Place one status cell immediately beside `cuLaunchKernel` and another beside the chosen synchronization call. They cover different stages.</details>

**Solution:** Establish the Runtime-selected device, call and check `cudaMalloc`, then call and check `cuInit(0)`. Query and record the context current on this host thread without claiming ownership that the packet never acquired. Load the module and resolve the function under that context, checking each `CUresult`. Keep the allocation, context, module, and function valid while calling `cuLaunchKernel`, and inspect its direct return as the submission result. A Runtime last-error getter cannot replace that Driver return. Use a documented completion operation such as `cuCtxSynchronize` while the same context is current and inspect its own `CUresult`; a failure there may report earlier asynchronous device work rather than originate at synchronization. Only after successful completion and any required result transfer or verification may the application unload its module and free the allocation through its recorded Runtime owner. It must not destroy the borrowed current context. This static design review creates no CUDA evidence.

**Valid alternative:** Explicitly retain a primary context or create a Driver-managed context, then pair that acquisition with the exact documented release path and keep all Runtime interoperability clauses explicit. An all-Runtime or all-Driver implementation is also valid when it preserves the same ownership, completion, and correctness boundaries.

**Common errors:** Treating both APIs as different device stacks; assuming each Runtime call maps one-to-one to a Driver call; destroying a primary/current context owned elsewhere; unloading a module before its work completes; using `cudaGetLastError` to check `CUresult`; treating a successful launch return as successful execution.

**Source basis:** The site's original [F07 role and ownership contract](/en/foundations/runtime-driver-api/), [F05 asynchronous-error contract](/en/foundations/asynchronous-errors/), and [SRC-CUDA-015 record](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-015), plus NVIDIA [Driver versus Runtime API](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-driver-api/driver-vs-runtime-api.html), [Runtime/Driver interactions](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/group__CUDART__DRIVER.html), and [Driver synchronization behavior](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-driver-api/api-sync-behavior.html).

<span id="pb-r1-012" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-012: Select legal block shapes before measurement

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-012 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Launch-geometry correctness and resource review; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [F08: Launch Geometry Is a Correctness and Resource Decision Before Speed](/en/foundations/launch-geometry/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; perform static geometry and resource checks only.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [F03](/en/foundations/multidimensional-indexing/), [F06](/en/foundations/compute-capability/), [F08](/en/foundations/launch-geometry/), [VIS22 Block-Shape Constraint Explorer](/en/foundations/launch-geometry/#vis22), and [LAB03](/en/labs/break-and-repair-indexing/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-26.

**Prompt:** A fixed logical extent is `width = 1000`, `height = 513`. The selected device record gives `maxThreadsDim = (1024, 1024, 64)`, `maxThreadsPerBlock = 1024`, and grid limits larger than every grid below. The selected kernel reports `maxThreadsPerBlock = 768`; its declared register and shared-memory needs fit for candidates at or below that function limit. Classify A `(32, 32)`, B `(48, 16)`, C `(64, 8)`, and D `(1025, 1)`. For every legal candidate, derive an overflow-safe grid, coverage, and fringe count while preserving the same two-axis bounds check. The packet calls B fastest because it has slightly less fringe than C. Decide which candidates may proceed to correctness verification and what remains before measurement.

**Constraints:**

- Do not round or pad the logical extent, remove either axis bound, or evaluate an unchecked product.
- Check positive inputs, per-axis device limits, aggregate device limits, function-specific limits, and grid limits as separate gates.
- Produce no latency, occupancy, throughput, or fastest-shape claim; this exercise runs no kernel.

**Expected evidence:** A four-row legality ledger, exact grid/coverage/fringe arithmetic for B and C, the preserved kernel guard, and a pre-measurement checklist.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Reject A because 1024 threads exceed the function-specific limit 768 even though the device aggregate limit passes; reject D because x exceeds its axis limit and the aggregate device limit.
- Accept B provisionally with grid `(21, 33)`, coverage `1008 x 528`, 532,224 launched threads, and 19,224 fringe threads.
- Accept C provisionally with grid `(16, 65)`, coverage `1024 x 520`, 532,480 launched threads, and 19,480 fringe threads.
- Preserve `gx < 1000 && gy < 513` before flattening/access, require successful launch/completion and identical result verification for each candidate, and reject “B is fastest” until a controlled measurement contract is executed.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Device legality is not the final block gate: compare each product with 768 after checking each axis against the device record.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>For positive `n` and `d`, use `1 + floor((n - 1) / d)`. Coverage is not a replacement logical extent.</details>

**Solution:** A has `32 * 32 = 1024` threads, so its axes and device aggregate pass but its function limit fails. D violates `block.x <= 1024` and has 1025 threads, so it fails before grid derivation. B has 768 threads and gives `grid = (1 + floor(999 / 48), 1 + floor(512 / 16)) = (21, 33)`. Its coverage is `1008 x 528`; checked products give 532,224 launched minus 513,000 logical equals 19,224 fringe. C has 512 threads and gives grid `(16, 65)`, coverage `1024 x 520`, 532,480 launched, and 19,480 fringe. Both remain only provisional because actual launch and completion checks still matter. Their kernel must leave the logical extent unchanged and reject fringe with both predicates before flattening. B's 256 fewer fringe threads are a geometry fact, not a runtime verdict. Only after identical inputs, successful boundaries, independent result comparison, and a declared warm-up/timing/synchronization/repetition method may B and C be measured.

**Valid alternative:** A legal `(16, 16)` candidate may be added to the ledger using the same gates and logical extent. Its legality still does not make it a preferred or faster shape.

**Common errors:** Checking only `maxThreadsPerBlock`; multiplying before checking overflow; ignoring `cudaFuncAttributes.maxThreadsPerBlock`; changing width or height to coverage; using x-only bounds; calling fewer fringe threads faster; treating browser or hand arithmetic as GPU execution.

**Source basis:** The site's original [F08 feasibility and measurement contract](/en/foundations/launch-geometry/), [F03 bounds contract](/en/foundations/multidimensional-indexing/), and [SRC-CUDA-016 record](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-016), plus NVIDIA [Compute Capabilities](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/05-appendices/compute-capabilities.html), [`cudaDeviceProp`](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/structcudaDeviceProp.html), and [`cudaFuncAttributes`](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/structcudaFuncAttributes.html).

<span id="pb-r1-013" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-013: Classify six memory objects by ownership, scope, and lifetime

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-013 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Memory-model classification; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [M01: Address spaces, ownership, scope, and lifetime](/en/memory/address-spaces/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review a static object inventory and run no CUDA.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M01](/en/memory/address-spaces/), [VIS06](/en/visuals/memory-hierarchy-lifetime/), [F04](/en/foundations/host-device-lifecycle/), and [F06](/en/foundations/compute-capability/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-27.

**Prompt:** Classify this six-object inventory: A. a host `std::vector<float> input`; B. the device allocation named by a host variable `d_input` after successful `cudaMalloc`; C. a module-scope `__constant__ float coefficients[64]`; D. one block's `__shared__ float tile[32]`; E. a compiler-created local-memory slot backing one thread's indexed `scratch[96]`; and F. one thread's scalar `sum` assigned to a register by the selected compilation. For each object, record creator/owner, accessibility and shared-instance scope, valid lifetime, and release/end action. Repair the reviewer's conclusions that “local means on-chip,” “global memory is a cache level that always bypasses caches,” and “every object needs `cudaFree`.”

**Constraints:**

- Keep the host pointer value `d_input` separate from the device allocation it names, and do not transfer ownership to a kernel merely because the pointer is passed as an argument.
- Treat address space, physical placement, cache path, capacity, and expected access cost as separate fields. Infer no fixed latency or speed order.
- Run no compiler or GPU command, invent no placement beyond the stated inventory, and assign no CUDA Evidence Status.

**Expected evidence:** A six-row ledger with owner/allocation, accessibility/scope, lifetime, and release/end columns, followed by three corrected claims about local placement, cache behavior, and release responsibility.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- A is owned by its C++ host scope and ends through language-managed destruction. B is an explicit global allocation whose release obligation belongs to host control flow; threads with a valid device pointer may access it across blocks or launches until its owner proves last use and calls `cudaFree` or reaches another documented teardown boundary.
- C is a module/context-lifetime constant symbol, read-only to kernels and updated by host symbol operations; it is not released with `cudaFree`. D is one shared instance per block, accessible by that block's threads and retired automatically when the block's execution ends.
- E and F are thread-private, compiler-managed storage for one thread invocation. Local memory may be physically in device memory, while register assignment can spill to local memory; neither case grants cross-thread access or requires `cudaFree`.
