- Reject every cache-rank or speed conclusion. An address space defines naming, sharing, and lifetime semantics; cache use and physical resource behavior require the selected architecture, compiler, and access pattern.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Start each row with the event that creates storage: C++ construction, Runtime allocation, module declaration, block launch, thread invocation, or compiler placement.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>“Local” answers who may name the object. It does not answer whether the backing bytes are physically close to the executing core.</details>

**Solution:** A's owner is the host C++ scope, which gives the CPU direct access and destroys the vector at scope exit. `d_input` is only a host handle; successful `cudaMalloc` creates B and a host-side obligation to retain it through the completion of every user, then call `cudaFree`. C belongs to its module/context interval, is visible read-only to kernel grids on the device, and is initialized or updated through symbol APIs rather than freed as an allocation. Each block creates its own D, shares that instance only among its threads, and retires it automatically at block completion. E is logically private to one thread even though its physical backing is device memory. F is also thread-private and compiler-managed; its stated register placement lasts for that invocation, but a different compilation could spill it into local memory. Thus local is not a synonym for on-chip, global is not a cache level, and only the explicit Runtime allocation in this inventory has a matching `cudaFree` obligation.

**Valid alternative:** A lifecycle diagram with six swim lanes is valid when each lane still names creator, permitted accessors, last legal use or automatic endpoint, and release authority, and separately labels unknown cache/placement facts.

**Common errors:** Calling the host pointer the allocation owner; allowing a kernel to free borrowed storage; freeing a constant symbol or shared tile; treating thread-private local storage as shared because it is physically in device memory; promising a source scalar remains in a register; ranking address spaces as cache levels.

**Source basis:** The site's original classification and release contracts in [M01](/en/memory/address-spaces/), [VIS06](/en/visuals/memory-hierarchy-lifetime/), [F04](/en/foundations/host-device-lifecycle/), and [SRC-CUDA-017](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-017), plus NVIDIA [Writing CUDA SIMT Kernels](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/02-basics/writing-cuda-kernels.html) and Runtime API [Memory Management](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/group__CUDART__MEMORY.html).

<span id="pb-r1-014" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-014: Derive aligned, offset, and stride segment sets

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-014 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Address-set derivation and claim review; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [M02: Coalescing as transaction shaping](/en/memory/coalescing-transactions/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; perform static address and segment arithmetic only.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M02](/en/memory/coalescing-transactions/), [VIS04](/en/visuals/memory-transactions/), [EX05](/en/examples/coalesced-strided-access/), and [F03](/en/foundations/multidimensional-indexing/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-27.

**Prompt:** Under M02's bounded CC 6.0+ model, one warp instruction has all 32 lanes active and each lane requests one 4-byte word. The base byte address is naturally aligned at 128. Derive the naturally aligned 32-byte segment-start set for A. `128 + 4 * lane`, B. `132 + 4 * lane`, and C. `128 + 8 * lane`, including every byte of each requested word. A reviewer then writes: “A uses four segments, B five, and C eight, so A is 1.25x faster than B and 2x faster than C; EX05 proves it.” Repair the conclusion.

**Constraints:**

- Show the lane-address formula, first and last requested byte, and `segment_start = 32 * floor(byte_address / 32)` before counting distinct segment starts.
- Keep logical source indices, requested bytes, modeled segment coverage, actual issued transactions, cache reuse, runtime, and speedup as separate facts.
- Execute neither EX05 nor a profiler. Invent no timing, throughput, cache event, or Evidence Status.

**Expected evidence:** Three explicit segment-start sets with cardinalities, one explanation of the offset and stride effects, and a corrected evidence statement for EX05.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- A requests bytes 128 through 255 and yields `{128, 160, 192, 224}`, cardinality 4.
- B requests bytes 132 through 259 and yields `{128, 160, 192, 224, 256}`, cardinality 5; the four-byte offset crosses one additional segment boundary.
- C's lane word starts run from 128 through 376 and its final byte is 379, yielding `{128, 160, 192, 224, 256, 288, 320, 352}`, cardinality 8; stride two places four requested words in each covered segment.
- Reject both speedup ratios. The sets are static coverage predictions for one warp instruction, not timings. EX05 has empty compilation evidence and Pending Hardware Verification runtime, so its source and host reference supply neither an executed GPU result nor a speed claim.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>For B, lane 31 starts at byte 256 and touches bytes 256 through 259, so the segment beginning at 256 cannot be omitted.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>For C, grouping eight-byte-spaced word starts by `floor(address / 32)` puts four lanes in each of eight segments.</details>

**Solution:** For A, lane `l` requests `[128 + 4l, 131 + 4l]`; the union spans 128 to 255 and maps to starts 128, 160, 192, and 224. B shifts every interval four bytes: `[132 + 4l, 135 + 4l]`. Lane 0 remains in the segment starting at 128, while lane 31 reaches bytes 256 to 259, so the set gains 256 and has five members. C requests `[128 + 8l, 131 + 8l]`; starts 128 through 376 cover eight aligned 32-byte segments from 128 through 352. These cardinalities explain requested-byte dispersion only. They contain no elapsed time, cache observation, issued-transaction record, or controlled comparison, so no factor may be relabeled a speedup. EX05's pure-host checks can verify indexing logic, but its compilation axis is empty and its runtime remains Pending Hardware Verification.

**Valid alternative:** A lane-to-segment table or a VIS04-style set diagram is valid when it includes all 32 lanes, preserves byte ranges rather than only word starts, and derives the same three ordered sets without performance language.

**Common errors:** Counting the offset pattern as four because it still contains 32 words; omitting the final three bytes of a word; counting one segment per lane in the stride case; replacing byte addresses with logical indices; treating a browser model, source listing, or host reference as a GPU timing.

**Source basis:** The site's original segment model in [M02](/en/memory/coalescing-transactions/), deterministic visualization in [VIS04](/en/visuals/memory-transactions/), canonical indexing cases and current evidence record in [EX05](/en/examples/coalesced-strided-access/), and [SRC-CUDA-018](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-018), plus NVIDIA CUDA C++ Best Practices Guide [Coalesced Access to Global Memory](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-c-best-practices-guide/index.html#coalesced-access-to-global-memory).

<span id="pb-r1-015" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-015: Repair an edge-tile early return and missing reuse barrier

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-015 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Shared-memory correctness debugging; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [M03: Shared-memory tiling](/en/memory/shared-memory-tiling/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review and repair source without running CUDA.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M03](/en/memory/shared-memory-tiling/), [EX06](/en/examples/shared-memory-tile-bank-padding/), [M01](/en/memory/address-spaces/), and [M02](/en/memory/coalescing-transactions/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-27.

**Prompt:** One 32-thread block loops over an input of `n = 45` and reuses one shared tile. The second iteration has only 13 valid input lanes. Repair both defects in this review draft:

```cpp
for (std::size_t tile_base = 0; tile_base < n; tile_base += blockDim.x) {
  const std::size_t input_index = tile_base + threadIdx.x;
  if (input_index >= n) {
    return;
  }
  tile[threadIdx.x] = input[input_index];
  __syncthreads();
  output[input_index] = tile[(threadIdx.x + 1) % blockDim.x];
  // The next iteration immediately overwrites tile.
}
```

**Constraints:**

- Preserve a portable C++17 synchronous `load -> __syncthreads() -> use/reuse -> __syncthreads()` baseline and the 32-thread block.
- Every thread participating in the block must reach each barrier in the same loop iteration. Bounds may guard memory access but not barrier participation.
- Use a declared neutral value for an invalid edge load. Run no build or kernel and invent no output, timing, or Evidence Status.

**Expected evidence:** A minimal source repair, a two-iteration participation ledger for valid and invalid lanes, and an explanation assigning a separate role to each barrier.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Remove the lane-dependent early return. In the edge iteration, lanes 0 through 12 load input and lanes 13 through 31 write the neutral value to their own tile slots before all 32 lanes reach the first barrier.
- Guard the output write with `input_index < n`; an invalid loader still participates in both barriers even though it performs no global input or output access.
- Keep the first barrier between tile writes and any tile read. Add a second barrier after the final tile read and before any lane can overwrite shared storage in the next iteration.
- State that the static repair creates no compilation or runtime observation. EX06's compilation evidence remains empty and runtime remains Pending Hardware Verification.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Replace “invalid lane exits” with “invalid lane contributes the neutral value,” then place the bounds condition only around global accesses.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>The first barrier protects reads from incomplete writes; the second protects current reads from next-iteration writes.</details>

**Solution:** One complete repair is:

```cpp
for (std::size_t tile_base = 0; tile_base < n; tile_base += blockDim.x) {
  const std::size_t input_index = tile_base + threadIdx.x;
  const bool valid = input_index < n;

  tile[threadIdx.x] = valid ? input[input_index] : 0.0f;
  __syncthreads();

  if (valid) {
    output[input_index] = tile[(threadIdx.x + 1) % blockDim.x];
  }
  __syncthreads();
}
```

In iteration zero, all 32 lanes load valid input. In iteration one, 13 lanes load input and 19 lanes write `0.0f`; all 32 then reach the first barrier, so every tile slot is established before any read. Only the 13 valid output owners write global output. All 32 reach the second barrier, preventing a fast lane from beginning the next load while another lane still reads the current tile. This review does not compile or execute EX06, whose compilation axis is empty and whose runtime is Pending Hardware Verification.

**Valid alternative:** If the algorithm is changed and proved to process exactly one tile per block with no later write to that shared object, the final reuse barrier may be unnecessary. That single-iteration contract is not a justification for omitting the second barrier from the loop above; the neutral edge load and first barrier remain required.

**Common errors:** Returning before a block-wide barrier; placing `__syncthreads()` inside `if (valid)`; leaving invalid tile slots uninitialized; guarding the barrier instead of the memory access; assuming lockstep warp progress replaces a block barrier; omitting the second barrier because the first protected the load.

**Source basis:** The site's original load, participation, and reuse contract in [M03](/en/memory/shared-memory-tiling/), address-space lifetime model in [M01](/en/memory/address-spaces/), canonical synchronous implementation and current evidence record in [EX06](/en/examples/shared-memory-tile-bank-padding/), and [SRC-CUDA-019](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-019), plus NVIDIA [Writing CUDA SIMT Kernels](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/02-basics/writing-cuda-kernels.html).

<span id="pb-r1-016" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-016: Distinguish broadcast from conflict and derive 32x33 padding

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-016 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Shared-memory bank and layout reasoning; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [M04: Bank conflicts and layout transforms](/en/memory/bank-conflicts-layouts/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; perform static word-address and bank mapping only.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M04](/en/memory/bank-conflicts-layouts/), [VIS05](/en/visuals/shared-memory-banks/), [EX06](/en/examples/shared-memory-tile-bank-padding/), and [M03](/en/memory/shared-memory-tiling/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-27.

**Prompt:** Use M04's selected fixture of 32 banks, successive 32-bit words, and `bank(word_index) = word_index mod 32`. Classify A. all 32 lanes read `tile[0][5]` from the same word of `float tile[32][32]`; B. lane `i` reads `tile[i][5]` from that unpadded tile; and C. lane `i` reads `tile[i][5]` after the declaration changes to `float tile[32][33]`. Derive each row stride, word-index formula, bank set, distinct-word count per bank, and classification. Repair the claim “A and B are both 32-way conflicts, while C is 32x faster.”

**Constraints:**

- Keep same-address read broadcast separate from multiple distinct words mapping to one bank.
- Derive the unpadded and padded row strides from the declarations. Preserve the logical `32 x 32` payload and account for padding bytes.
- Run no EX06 build, CUDA kernel, timer, or profiler. Infer no latency, throughput, or speedup.

**Expected evidence:** A three-row bank ledger, the modulo derivation for the padded layout, padding-capacity arithmetic, and a corrected evidence/performance statement.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- A has stride 32 in the declaration but every lane requests the identical word index 5 and bank 5. It is a same-address read broadcast, not a 32-way bank conflict.
- B uses word index `32i + 5`; `(32i + 5) mod 32 = 5`, so bank 5 receives 32 distinct words and the selected fixture classifies it as a 32-way conflict.
- C uses word index `33i + 5`; `(33i + 5) mod 32 = (i + 5) mod 32`, which visits all 32 banks exactly once and is conflict-free for this instruction. The extra column adds 32 floats, or 128 bytes, per tile while leaving the logical payload unchanged.
- Reject “32x faster.” The mapping predicts conflict classification only. EX06 has empty compilation evidence and Pending Hardware Verification runtime; neither VIS05 nor host arithmetic is timing evidence.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Count distinct requested word indices inside each bank. Thirty-two requests for one word and thirty-two requests for different words are not the same case.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Because `33 mod 32 = 1`, each successive row advances the selected column by one bank after padding.</details>

**Solution:** A sends every lane to word 5 in bank 5, but there is only one distinct requested word, so the read uses the selected model's broadcast exception. B's row stride is 32 words; lane `i` selects `32i + 5`, and all 32 distinct words reduce to bank 5, giving a 32-way conflict in this fixture. Padding changes the physical row stride to 33 while preserving 32 logical columns. C therefore maps lane `i` to `33i + 5`, whose bank is `(i + 5) mod 32`; the 32 lanes form a permutation of banks 0 through 31, so the modeled instruction is conflict-free. The physical allocation gains `32 * 1 * 4 = 128` bytes. These facts predict neither an elapsed-time ratio nor end-to-end speed: EX06 has not produced a qualifying compile record or GPU runtime observation and remains Pending Hardware Verification.

**Valid alternative:** Transpose or remap the cooperative load/use indices so the same logical values are consumed with a stride relatively prime to 32. The alternative must show its complete word-index and bank derivation, preserve bounds and synchronization, and account for any storage or indexing cost before measurement.

**Common errors:** Calling broadcast a conflict because one bank is named 32 times; counting requests instead of distinct words; keeping stride 32 after declaring 33 columns; claiming padding changes the logical matrix; forgetting the 128-byte capacity cost; converting conflict degree directly into speedup.

**Source basis:** The site's original selected bank fixture and padding derivation in [M04](/en/memory/bank-conflicts-layouts/), deterministic mapper in [VIS05](/en/visuals/shared-memory-banks/), canonical layouts and current evidence record in [EX06](/en/examples/shared-memory-tile-bank-padding/), and [SRC-CUDA-019](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-019), plus NVIDIA CUDA C++ Best Practices Guide [Shared Memory and Memory Banks](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-c-best-practices-guide/index.html#shared-memory-and-memory-banks).

<span id="pb-r1-017" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-017: Select synchronization scope from participants and communication

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-017 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Synchronization-scope selection and protocol review; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [M05: Synchronization scopes and memory visibility](/en/memory/synchronization-scopes/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review a static synchronization protocol and run no CUDA.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M05](/en/memory/synchronization-scopes/), [F02](/en/foundations/execution-hierarchy/), and [M01](/en/memory/address-spaces/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-28.

**Prompt:** Choose the narrowest candidate scope for four communication edges: A. lanes 0 through 7 in one warp exchange register values; B. 128 threads in one block rendezvous before reading a shared tile; C. one producer block writes a global payload and another block reads after seeing a completion flag; and D. a CPU reads a system-accessible allocation after GPU completion. A reviewer adds `__threadfence_system()` to all four and claims that “the largest scope supplies rendezvous, notification, visibility, and atomicity together.” Repair the review.

**Constraints:**

- Record participants, memory location, rendezvous, ordering/visibility, atomicity, and progress obligations separately for each case.
- C must not assume ordinary blocks are simultaneously resident or treat a fence as a completion notification.
- Execute no kernel and invent no output, timing, cache behavior, or Evidence Status.

**Expected evidence:** A four-row scope ledger, a producer-consumer ordering graph for C, one rejection reason for a too-narrow or too-wide choice in every case, and a corrected evidence statement.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- A uses an explicit mask naming participating lanes. B remains block-scoped and requires every required block thread to reach the same barrier phase.
- C uses a device-scope publication protocol: payload writes precede release publication, and the consumer reads only after a matching acquire observation. A standalone fence neither publishes a flag nor rendezvous with the consumer.
- D first proves CPU/GPU accessibility, then uses a relation covering both participants through a system-scope protocol or an explicit host completion boundary.
- Reject “the largest scope includes every guarantee automatically.” Scope, primitive guarantees, and Evidence Status remain separate.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Circle the farthest-apart producer and consumer in each case before choosing a primitive name.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Write C as four nodes: payload write, flag publication, flag observation, and payload read.</details>

**Solution:** A's participant set contains only lanes 0 through 7 in one warp, so a warp candidate is sufficient when its mask names those eight lanes and every named lane executes the collective. B has one shared instance per block and needs a block barrier for its 128 required threads; warp scope is too narrow, while device or system scope does not replace barrier participation. C crosses blocks, so a block operation cannot cover the consumer. One complete static repair is `write payload -> device-scope release store ready=1 -> device-scope acquire load observes 1 -> read payload`. The fence-only version lacks observable publication and consumer-side ordering. D includes a CPU participant, so system accessibility must be proved before using a documented system relation; a host API completion boundary can also establish completion before consumption. None of the four needs a system fence as a universal operation.

**Valid alternative:** C can be split into two kernels with an explicit host or stream-submission boundary that starts the consumer only after producer completion. That redesign must still preserve the global allocation lifetime and implies no performance result.

**Common errors:** Treating a fence as a barrier; using `__syncthreads()` across blocks; making a plain flag carry atomic publication; ignoring CPU accessibility; assuming wider is automatically more correct; treating a static ledger as CUDA execution.

**Source basis:** The site's original [M05 scope-selection contract](/en/memory/synchronization-scopes/), [F02 participant hierarchy](/en/foundations/execution-hierarchy/), [M01 ownership and scope model](/en/memory/address-spaces/), and [SRC-CUDA-020](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-020), plus NVIDIA [Advanced Kernel Programming](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/03-advanced/advanced-kernel-programming.html) and the [CUDA C++ Memory Model](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/05-appendices/cuda-cpp-memory-model.html).

<span id="pb-r1-018" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-018: Repair an unsafe warp collective

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-018 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Warp correctness debugging; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [M06: Divergence, reconvergence, and warp-safe reasoning](/en/memory/warp-divergence-reconvergence/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; derive predicates, masks, and synchronization edges without running CUDA.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M06](/en/memory/warp-divergence-reconvergence/), [VIS03](/en/visuals/warp-divergence/), [M05](/en/memory/synchronization-scopes/), and [F02](/en/foundations/execution-hierarchy/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-28.

**Prompt:** An algorithm names lanes 0 through 7 as participants. It branches on `lane < 4`, saves `__activemask()` separately inside each arm, then calls `__shfl_sync(saved_mask, value, lane == 0 ? 0 : lane - 1)` after the source-level join. A reviewer says the closing brace automatically reconverges all eight lanes, so the two branch-local masks are interchangeable. Repair the collective and state which schedule facts remain unknown under CC 7.0+ Independent Thread Scheduling.

**Constraints:**

- Establish the intended participant set consistently before divergence, with every named lane executing the matching collective.
- Lane 0 consumes no nonexistent predecessor. Every source lane requested by lanes 1 through 7 must be active, named, and valid.
- Rely on no implicit lockstep, do not treat VIS03 path order as hardware issue order, and invent no timing.

**Expected evidence:** An eight-row predicate and mask table, a missing-edge diagnosis, a minimal shuffle repair, one shared-memory alternative, and a list of known and unknown schedule facts.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- The original branch-local masks cover lanes 0 through 3 and 4 through 7 separately and cannot form one eight-lane collective contract; lane 4's predecessor lane 3 crosses those masks.
- The repair establishes one lane-0-through-7 mask before the outer divergence, lets all eight named lanes execute the collective in the inner branch's common region, and guards lane 0's consumption separately.
- A source-level join is not memory synchronization. The shared-memory alternative adds explicit warp synchronization over the same participant mask.
- Exact branch issue order, instruction interleaving, and hardware reconvergence mechanism remain unknown.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Write the set returned by each `__activemask()` and check whether lane 4's requested source lane 3 belongs to the same collective contract.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Put “who needs the result” and “who must execute the collective to provide it” in separate columns.</details>

**Solution:** The original program gives lanes 0 through 3 a lower mask and lanes 4 through 7 an upper mask. A closing brace provides a common source successor but does not turn those masks into one synchronization group. Before the branch, current warp participants can consistently compute `participants = ballot(lane < 8)`. Lanes 0 through 7 execute their scalar branch work, then all call `__shfl_sync(participants, value, lane == 0 ? 0 : lane - 1)` in the common region, with only lanes 1 through 7 consuming predecessor values. Source lanes 0 through 6 are named and execute the same collective; lane 0's self-source result is ignored. CC 7.0+ permits per-thread execution state and sub-warp regrouping but supplies no portable path order or instruction trace.

**Valid alternative:** The eight lanes may write shared storage, execute `__syncwarp(participants)`, and then let lanes 1 through 7 read predecessor slots. The same mask proof remains required, and synchronization must sit between the writes and peer reads.

**Common errors:** Deriving the intended group from `__activemask()` inside a branch; naming lanes that never reach the collective; reading an inactive source lane; treating a closing brace as a barrier; assuming natural lockstep within a warp; treating VIS03's deterministic drawing order as a hardware schedule.

**Source basis:** The site's original [M06 mask and warp-safe reasoning contract](/en/memory/warp-divergence-reconvergence/), [VIS03 predicate trace](/en/visuals/warp-divergence/), [M05 synchronization distinctions](/en/memory/synchronization-scopes/), and [SRC-CUDA-020](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-020), plus NVIDIA [Advanced Kernel Programming](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/03-advanced/advanced-kernel-programming.html) and [Device-Callable APIs](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/05-appendices/device-callable-apis.html).

<span id="pb-r1-019" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-019: Repair a two-stream partial order and lifetime

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-019 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Stream-order and lifetime correctness debugging; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [M07: Streams replace a global-order mental model](/en/memory/stream-ordering/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review a static stream graph and allocation lifetime.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M07](/en/memory/stream-ordering/), [VIS07](/en/visuals/stream-event-dependencies/), [F05](/en/foundations/asynchronous-errors/), and [M01](/en/memory/address-spaces/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-28.

**Prompt:** Two explicit `cudaStreamNonBlocking` streams carry `stream_prepare: H2D(h_input -> d_input) -> prepare(d_input, d_stage)` and `stream_consume: consume(d_stage, d_output) -> D2H(d_output -> h_output)`. The host submits every call in that textual order, then immediately destroys `h_input` and releases all three device allocations. A reviewer claims that “host submission order creates one global queue, and the two streams prove the kernels ran concurrently.” Repair the partial-order graph, data dependency, and every object lifetime.

**Constraints:**

- Add only enqueue-order edges within each stream automatically. Every cross-stream edge needs a documented mechanism.
- Record the last asynchronous user and release boundary for `h_input`, `h_output`, `d_input`, `d_stage`, and `d_output` separately.
- Rely on no default-stream rule and claim no observed overlap, duration, or performance.

**Expected evidence:** Two stream lanes, the complete same-stream edge set, one missing cross-stream edge, a five-row lifetime ledger, and a repaired host completion and release sequence.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- The initial graph contains only `H2D -> prepare` and `consume -> D2H`; host call order does not create `prepare -> consume`.
- Add the narrowest explicit dependency between prepare completion and consume eligibility, or put the dependent chain into one stream.
- Retain `h_input` through H2D, `d_input` through prepare, `d_stage` through consume, and `d_output` plus `h_output` through D2H and the host verification boundary.
- Do not relabel unordered work as concurrent. The static graph and lifetime repair grant no CUDA Evidence Status.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Start with two disconnected horizontal chains, then ask which consumer input lacks a predecessor edge.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>For every object, name the last asynchronous operation that may still use it instead of treating enqueue return as last use.</details>

**Solution:** The initial partial order contains only the two lane-local edges. `consume` reads `d_stage` produced by `prepare`, so the graph needs an explicit `prepare -> consume` dependency. For example, record an event after prepare in the producer stream and wait before consume in the consumer stream; M08 further constrains the event generation. This edge prevents early consumer eligibility but does not prove stream overlap. The host must retain `h_input` through H2D, `d_input` through prepare, `d_stage` through consume, and `d_output` plus `h_output` through D2H. Only after a host completion boundary covering the final D2H may it verify `h_output` and release the corresponding storage, with every release still following the actual API's documented contract.

**Valid alternative:** Put H2D, prepare, consume, and D2H in one non-default stream so per-stream order expresses the same correctness graph. A device-wide host synchronization can also establish a broader completion boundary, but it is not the only answer when one dependency edge is enough.

**Common errors:** Treating host source order as device global order; declaring concurrency from different stream names; forgetting an asynchronous copy's host-buffer lifetime; releasing `d_stage` before its consumer's last use; synchronizing only the producer stream before reading `h_output`; using undeclared default-stream behavior to supply the missing edge.

**Source basis:** The site's original [M07 stream-order contract](/en/memory/stream-ordering/), [VIS07 dependency graph](/en/visuals/stream-event-dependencies/), [M01 lifetime ledger](/en/memory/address-spaces/), [F05 asynchronous boundary](/en/foundations/asynchronous-errors/), and [SRC-CUDA-021](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-021), plus NVIDIA [Asynchronous Execution](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/02-basics/asynchronous-execution.html) and Runtime API [Stream Management](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/group__CUDART__STREAM.html).

<span id="pb-r1-020" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-020: Review event dependencies and device-time evidence

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-020 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Event-generation and timing-evidence review; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [M08: Events as dependencies and device-time measurements](/en/memory/event-dependencies-timing/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review event generations, dependencies, and timing evidence only.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M08](/en/memory/event-dependencies-timing/), [M07](/en/memory/stream-ordering/), [VIS07](/en/visuals/stream-event-dependencies/), and [O02](/en/start/evidence-status/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-28.

**Prompt:** A producer stream records handle `E` after `P1`, and the consumer then submits `W1 = wait(E)`. The producer runs `P2`, re-records the same handle, and later submits `W2`. A separate `ready` event was created with `cudaEventDisableTiming`, but a reviewer uses it as the start endpoint for elapsed time. The review also calls VIS07 browser pacing “a measured 0.8 ms with concurrent streams.” Repair the generation, dependency, timing, and Evidence Status claims.

**Constraints:**

- Use teaching labels `E1` and `E2` for two captured states of one handle; do not invent two handles.
- Distinguish non-blocking query, host-blocking synchronize, stream wait, and elapsed-time calculation.
- Supply no replacement number and treat neither browser animation, a static formula, nor owner documentation as a runtime observation.

**Expected evidence:** An API-call and generation table, two wait edges, a three-event flag table, a valid timing bracket, and one evidence-neutral review conclusion.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- `W1` remains bound to `E1` as selected at call time; later re-recording does not retarget it. `W2` uses `E2` after re-record.
- `ready` may support documented dependency, query, or synchronize operations, but disabled timing makes it invalid as a `cudaEventElapsedTime` endpoint.
- Valid timing uses timing-enabled `start` and `stop`, declares included work, waits for recorded endpoints to complete, checks API results, and reports a value only after a real run with an Environment Manifest.
- Delete the 0.8 ms and observed-concurrency claims. VIS07 and this review have no CUDA Evidence Status.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Beside every Runtime API call, write which state the handle had most recently captured at that call time.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>A creation flag is part of an event's contract. Dependency-capable does not imply timing-capable.</details>

**Solution:** The first record creates `E1 = work through P1`, so W1 makes later consumer work wait only for E1. The second record changes future selections to `E2 = work through P2`; W2 binds E2, while W1 is not retroactively retargeted. `cudaEventQuery` reports completion of the selected captured state without blocking the host, `cudaEventSynchronize` waits on the host, and `cudaStreamWaitEvent` constrains work submitted after the wait in that stream. Dependency-only `ready` remains a valid dependency object but cannot enter elapsed-time calculation. Real timing requires timing-enabled start and stop events recorded around a declared region, completed stop work, and a checked `elapsed_ms = timestamp(stop) - timestamp(start)` result. That symbolic formula supplies no value. Browser pacing controls legibility only, so both 0.8 ms and overlap claims must be removed.

**Valid alternative:** Separate handles for `E1` and `E2` can make generation ownership easier to review. A larger or smaller timing bracket is also valid when its included work, completion boundary, and measurement method are restated.

**Common errors:** Retargeting an earlier wait after re-record; treating host-side record return as event completion; treating query as a blocking wait; passing a timing-disabled event to elapsed-time calculation; copying a browser cadence as a duration; treating a dependency edge as a concurrency observation.

**Source basis:** The site's original [M08 event-generation and timing contract](/en/memory/event-dependencies-timing/), [M07 partial-order model](/en/memory/stream-ordering/), [VIS07 deterministic trace](/en/visuals/stream-event-dependencies/), [O02 Evidence Status contract](/en/start/evidence-status/), and [SRC-CUDA-021](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-021), plus NVIDIA Runtime API [Event Management](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/group__CUDART__EVENT.html) and [Stream Management](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/group__CUDART__STREAM.html).

<span id="pb-r1-021" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-021: Design an independent CPU reference, tolerances, and invariants

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-021 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Correctness-oracle design and debugging; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [Q01: CPU references, tolerances, and invariants](/en/correctness/cpu-references-tolerances-invariants/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; design and review a static correctness oracle without running CUDA.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [Q01](/en/correctness/cpu-references-tolerances-invariants/), [F04](/en/foundations/host-device-lifecycle/), and [O04](/en/start/cpp17-for-cuda/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-28.

**Prompt:** A GPU histogram emits 16 floating-point probabilities, 16 integer counts, and one `max_bin`. A reviewer copies the kernel's flattening and loop order line for line into the CPU reference, enlarges `epsilon` after seeing one mismatch, checks only the probability sum, and starts timing. Design a layered correctness oracle that can expose an indexing defect shared by the CPU and GPU paths.

**Constraints:**

- Derive the CPU reference independently from the input/output contract; reuse neither a kernel-indexing helper nor launch geometry.
- Use a predeclared absolute-plus-relative rule for floating-point fields, exact comparison for counts and `max_bin`, and separate NaN, infinity, and tie policies.
- Add at least two invariants derived from the problem specification rather than candidate output. Timing is forbidden when any gate fails.

**Expected evidence:** An oracle ledger naming the independent reference path, comparator for each field, threshold rationale, exact checks, invariants, first-failure report, and timing gate. It is a design artifact, not a run record.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- The CPU reference re-derives bin ownership and bounds; comparison occurs only after explicit GPU completion and copy-back.
- Every finite probability uses `abs(g - r) <= atol + rtol * abs(r)`, with `atol` and `rtol` fixed before results and justified by scale, precision, and an error budget or domain requirement.
- Counts, `max_bin`, and the tie rule are exact. Independent invariants such as `sum(counts) == sample_count`, non-negative counts, and normalization remain separate from reference agreement.
- Remove the universal epsilon and premature timing. A host-only oracle test grants no GPU runtime evidence.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Write the input/output contract that both paths must satisfy, then rebuild the host path with different loop nesting.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Put “the arrays agree” and “no sample was dropped or duplicated” in separate verdict columns.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The CPU implementation iterates directly over input samples, computes bins from the specification, and accumulates counts on the host without reading grid, block, or thread formulas. After GPU completion and copy-back, each probability first passes a non-finite policy and then `error <= atol + rtol * abs(reference)`; counts and `max_bin` under the declared tie rule compare exactly. Independent invariants check at least count conservation, non-negativity, probability range or normalization, and consistency between `max_bin` and the maximal count. Agreement with a failed invariant still rejects, while invariants alone cannot replace the reference. There is no valid timing input before every gate passes.

**Valid alternative:** A second independent host library path or property-based fixtures may extend coverage when every comparator, invariant, and generation seed remains explicit.

**Common errors:** Translating the kernel line for line; tuning thresholds after a mismatch; applying tolerance to integers; allowing NaN to pass accidentally; checking only a sum; relabeling a host-only pass as Runtime-Verified.

**Source basis:** The site's original [Q01 layered-oracle contract](/en/correctness/cpu-references-tolerances-invariants/) and [SRC-CUDA-022](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-022), plus NVIDIA CUDA C++ Best Practices Guide [Verification](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-c-best-practices-guide/index.html#verification) and [Floating Point and IEEE 754: Verifying GPU Results](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/floating-point/index.html#verifying-gpu-results).

**Source date:** 2026-08-28.

<span id="pb-r1-022" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-022: Audit a lane-safe memcheck plan

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-022 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Memcheck correctness debugging; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [Q03: Memcheck and invalid memory access](/en/correctness/memcheck-invalid-memory-access/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review commands, coverage, and expected report classes without running Compute Sanitizer.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [Q03](/en/correctness/memcheck-invalid-memory-access/), [Q01](/en/correctness/cpu-references-tolerances-invariants/), [F05](/en/foundations/asynchronous-errors/), [EX16](/en/examples/sanitizer-defect-suite/), and [LAB07](/en/labs/diagnose-four-sanitizer-failures/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-28.

**Prompt:** A plan says only `compute-sanitizer ./app`, loops over four inputs in one process, checks no application return, launch, or completion error, and defines no full-leak boundary. Its reviewer prewrites, “All three Toolkit Lanes should report zero errors, so the program is correct.” Repair it into a reviewable memcheck plan for the selected Lanes.

**Constraints:**

- Record the selected Toolkit Lane, actual Compute Sanitizer version, binary identity, exact command, input, and Environment Manifest. Do not assume identical defaults across Lanes.
- Keep access checking, full leak checking, application correctness, and path coverage in separate columns. Memcheck precedes every other sanitizer.
- Use a fresh process per scenario and invent no report, exit status, clean result, or Evidence Status.

**Expected evidence:** A run matrix containing an access run, a through-teardown leak run, application error and completion checks, a Q01 correctness gate, an executed-path inventory, expected report classes, and blank observation fields.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- The baseline selects memcheck explicitly and retains tool and application stdout, stderr, and exit status; the application still checks API returns, launch errors, and a completion boundary.
- A full-leak run enables full leak checking explicitly and includes CUDA context teardown in its observation boundary. An ordinary access run cannot supply its leak result.
- The input matrix exercises declared bounds and alignment branches, each isolated scenario uses a fresh process, and an independent CPU reference, exact check, or invariant runs outside the sanitizer.
- A clean report describes only the selected tool, version, binary, input, and executed path. The plan grants neither Community-Observed nor Runtime-Verified.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Separate what the tool observes from what the application checks into two tables.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>For leak observation, ask whether the process reached context destruction, not merely whether the kernel returned.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** Each Lane first records an Environment Manifest, `compute-sanitizer --version`, the binary hash, and the exact command. Every input starts a fresh process. An explicit memcheck access run retains application launch and completion checks plus the Q01 oracle verdict; another fresh process uses the Lane-supported full-leak option and completes teardown. The matrix names the intended path and expected report class, while observations, raw logs, and criteria remain blank before execution. Racecheck, initcheck, and synccheck are scheduled only after memcheck. Even a future clean memcheck result is limited to its executed paths and documented coverage.

**Valid alternative:** Access and leak runs may live in separate scripts when both preserve the same binary identity and record their own command, process boundary, and criteria.

**Common errors:** Depending on a default tool; treating sanitizer exit status as application correctness; omitting completion errors; judging leaks before context destruction; running every scenario in one process; treating a clean report as a correctness proof.

**Source basis:** The site's original [Q03 memcheck contract](/en/correctness/memcheck-invalid-memory-access/), [EX16 scenario boundary](/en/examples/sanitizer-defect-suite/), and [SRC-CUDA-023](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-023), plus NVIDIA [Compute Sanitizer memcheck tool](https://docs.nvidia.com/compute-sanitizer/ComputeSanitizer/index.html#memcheck-tool) and its [11.8 archive](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/11.8.0/compute-sanitizer/index.html).

**Source date:** 2026-08-28.

<span id="pb-r1-023" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-023: Select the right Sanitizer for four defect classes

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-023 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Sanitizer selection and claim debugging; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [Q04: Diagnose with racecheck, initcheck, and synccheck](/en/correctness/racecheck-initcheck-synccheck/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; classify static defect scenarios without running Compute Sanitizer.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [Q04](/en/correctness/racecheck-initcheck-synccheck/), [Q03](/en/correctness/memcheck-invalid-memory-access/), [EX16](/en/examples/sanitizer-defect-suite/), and [LAB07](/en/labs/diagnose-four-sanitizer-failures/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-28.

**Prompt:** Four independent executables contain a global out-of-bounds write, a shared-memory RAW hazard caused by a missing block barrier, an uninitialized device-global read, and a divergent `__syncthreads()`. A reviewer runs racecheck once and claims that “no report proves all four error classes are absent.” Select the right tools, order, and process boundaries, then repair the conclusion.

**Constraints:**

- Begin with memcheck, then select racecheck, initcheck, or synccheck for the matching scenario. One process carries only one defect or corrected case.
- Use the stable cross-Lane baseline. A current-only initcheck shared-memory option needs an explicit version gate and cannot be projected into 11.8.
- Every corrected case still needs a Q01 correctness gate. Do not turn a clean tool report, host test, or expected class into proof of GPU correctness.

**Expected evidence:** A four-row tool-selection matrix, a memcheck-first dependency, a plan for eight isolated processes, defect and corrected acceptance for each pair, and version and coverage caveats.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Memcheck owns the global bounds defect, racecheck the shared-memory data access hazard, initcheck the uninitialized device-global read, and synccheck the synchronization primitive misuse.
- Each narrower tool runs only against its matching fresh process with separate tool and application logs and statuses. A corrected variant inherits no record from its defect run.
- The shared-memory initcheck extension is used only after recording an actual supporting version; the stable baseline does not claim shared-memory coverage.
- Delete “all four classes are absent.” Every clean result covers only its documented detector, binary, input, and executed path, while numerical correctness is accepted separately.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Name the storage or primitive involved in each defect before choosing a detector.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Ask whether “this tool emitted no report” can rule out a class owned by another tool.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** Every case first passes memcheck and application error checks. The global OOB case targets memcheck; the missing shared barrier creates a shared-memory RAW hazard for racecheck; initcheck targets the uninitialized device-global input; synccheck targets the divergent block barrier. Defect and corrected binaries run in separate fresh processes with independent records. If the current initcheck shared-memory extension is used, its tool version and support are recorded first; CUDA 11.8 inherits no such conclusion. Corrected cases also pass a host reference or invariant. A clean report proves nothing about another detector, an unexecuted path, or whole-program correctness.

**Valid alternative:** Four tool-specific runners may each own one defect and corrected pair, but global orchestration must preserve the memcheck-first edge and separate process and log identities.

**Common errors:** Using racecheck for a global OOB; calling initcheck a general race detector; allowing one crash to contaminate later scenarios; projecting a current option into an archive; running only the corrected binary; treating zero reports as proof.

**Source basis:** The site's original [Q04 detector boundaries](/en/correctness/racecheck-initcheck-synccheck/), [four independent EX16 pairs](/en/examples/sanitizer-defect-suite/), [LAB07](/en/labs/diagnose-four-sanitizer-failures/), and [SRC-CUDA-023](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-023), plus NVIDIA [Compute Sanitizer User Guide](https://docs.nvidia.com/compute-sanitizer/ComputeSanitizer/index.html) and [Release Notes](https://docs.nvidia.com/compute-sanitizer/ReleaseNotes/index.html).

**Source date:** 2026-08-28.

<span id="pb-r1-024" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R1-024: Repair a one-shot asynchronous GPU timing claim

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R1-024 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Timing-evidence review; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [Q05: Time asynchronous GPU work honestly](/en/correctness/timing-asynchronous-gpu-work/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review a measurement plan, raw samples, and claim boundaries without running a benchmark.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [Q05](/en/correctness/timing-asynchronous-gpu-work/), [Q01](/en/correctness/cpu-references-tolerances-invariants/), [M08](/en/memory/event-dependencies-timing/), [O03](/en/start/environment-manifest/), [LAB04](/en/labs/observe-coalescing/), and [LAB05](/en/labs/remove-shared-memory-bank-conflicts/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-28.

**Prompt:** A reviewer places a host clock around one asynchronous kernel launch, performs no synchronization, retains the first iteration with lazy loading, checks no output, and publishes a `0.04 ms average` without raw samples, spread, exact command, or Environment Manifest. Repair this one-shot claim into an honest timing plan without supplying a replacement number.

**Constraints:**

- The correctness gate passes first. Warm-up and measured repetitions are separate, with declared work and completion boundaries for both.
- CUDA-event device time and host wall-clock time are distinct methods. Each explicitly waits for included work to finish, and their samples are never mixed.
- Declare sample retention, statistic, and spread before collection. Retain every raw repeated sample and a complete Environment Manifest.

**Expected evidence:** A measurement protocol with no prefilled value, containing a correctness verdict, warm-up exclusion, timing bracket, completion and API checks, raw-sample schema, predeclared statistic, manifest fields, and an allowed claim template.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Delete the `0.04 ms average`. Wrong or unchecked output never enters timing, and a host-only check grants no GPU runtime evidence.
- Warm-up executes and reaches completion but is excluded from samples. Measured iterations use the same frozen work and establish a completion boundary for every sample.
- The event method uses timing-enabled start and stop events, record calls, stop completion, and checked `cudaEventElapsedTime`. A host method includes explicit completion within its wall-clock bracket and reports separately.
- Publish every raw sample, a predeclared median and spread, exact source, build, and command, and a complete Environment Manifest. Limit the conclusion to recorded coordinates.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Mark launch return and GPU completion as two different events inside the original host-clock bracket.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>With one retained number, no one can inspect a distribution or tell whether warm-up was mixed in.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired protocol freezes source, build, input, and launch geometry, then completes the Q01 CPU-reference, tolerance, and invariant gate. It executes a declared warm-up to explicit completion but retains no warm-up sample. For device time, every repetition records timing-enabled events around declared stream work, waits for stop completion, checks every API result, and then stores elapsed time. A separate host-wall-clock experiment includes submission and explicit completion within its bracket and never merges with event values. All repetitions enter a raw table; median and spread are selected before collection; and the O03 GPU, driver, Toolkit and component, compiler, OS, workload, memory, permissions, command, correctness, and observation-date coordinates accompany the record. Every result field remains blank until a real run exists.

**Valid alternative:** Profiler duration is a separate method when profiler version, metric, replay, and options are frozen, the raw report is retained, and its samples stay separate from event and host values. LAB04 and LAB05 define that tool gate.

**Common errors:** Timing an asynchronous enqueue; retaining first-use work as a normal sample; skipping output validation; using a timing-disabled event; publishing only an average; discarding raw values; mixing profiler, event, and wall-clock samples; comparing machines without manifests.

**Source basis:** The site's original [Q05 measurement contract](/en/correctness/timing-asynchronous-gpu-work/), [M08 event contract](/en/memory/event-dependencies-timing/), [LAB04](/en/labs/observe-coalescing/), [LAB05](/en/labs/remove-shared-memory-bank-conflicts/), and [SRC-CUDA-024](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-024), plus NVIDIA CUDA C++ Best Practices Guide [Timing](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-c-best-practices-guide/index.html#timing), Runtime API [Event Management](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/group__CUDART__EVENT.html), and the [Nsight Compute Profiling Guide](https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html).

**Source date:** 2026-08-28.

<span id="pb-r2-001" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-001: Audit a chunked transfer-overlap claim

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-001 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Transfer-overlap evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [M09: Pinned Memory and Transfer Overlap](/en/memory/pinned-memory-transfer-overlap/).
- **Hardware gate:** No hardware for the static audit. A later correctness run requires native Linux and compute capability 7.5+, while overlap observation also requires page-locked buffers, compatible asynchronous copy/compute capability, and an exact-run device timeline.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M07](/en/memory/stream-ordering/), [M08](/en/memory/event-dependencies-timing/), [M09](/en/memory/pinned-memory-transfer-overlap/), [Q05](/en/correctness/timing-asynchronous-gpu-work/), and [EX07](/en/examples/streams-events-overlap/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-29.

**Prompt:** A two-slot pipeline obtains `h_in[2]` and `h_out[2]` with ordinary `malloc`, then submits `cudaMemcpyAsync(H2D) -> kernel -> cudaMemcpyAsync(D2H)` in two non-default streams. The host refills an input slot immediately after enqueue and reads output without waiting for D2H completion. A reviewer sees `asyncEngineCount > 0` and two streams, then publishes “42% transfer overlap and 1.8x speedup.” Repair the lifetime and dependency graph and the evidence claim without supplying replacement performance values.

**Constraints:**

- Preserve the same logical input, kernel work, tail handling, output oracle, and error checks as a serialized baseline. Pair both allocation and registration page-lock paths with their matching release actions.
- Every chunk has `host fill -> H2D -> kernel -> D2H -> output-ready` order, with completion edges protecting ring-slot overwrite, read, and release.
- Keep source eligibility, queried capability, and observed timeline in three columns. Infer no overlap, duration, bandwidth, or speedup from API spelling, stream count, an event formula, or browser layout.

**Expected evidence:** A two-slot ownership and dependency ledger, a serialized-versus-pipelined correctness-equivalence checklist, and a capability-to-observation decision table whose runtime values and timeline observation fields remain blank.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Host ranges are created by `cudaMallocHost` or `cudaHostAlloc` and released with `cudaFreeHost`, or registered by `cudaHostRegister`, unregistered after the last asynchronous use, and finally released by the original allocator.
- The host refills an input slot only after its H2D completes and reads or reuses output only after its D2H completes. Cleanup waits for final completion across every slot.
- `asyncEngineCount` and graph eligibility permit only “eligible,” “unsupported,” or “under-specified.” Without an exact-run timeline, overlap remains unobserved.
- Delete 42% and 1.8x. The serialized baseline can still pass correctness when the capability gate fails.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Treat each slot as a resource with a previous owner and a next overwrite point, rather than drawing only three device operations.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Ask whether `asyncEngineCount` says what resources may exist or whether two intervals intersected in this run.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** Create a bounded set of page-locked input and output slots before steady state. Each stream preserves same-stream H2D, kernel, and D2H order for its chunk, then records `output_ready[slot]`. Before refilling or reading a slot, the host waits for the previous generation on that slot to complete. The tail uses its actual byte count, and every API, launch, completion, and output-oracle result passes before the observation gate. The manifest retains the exact page-lock path, device query and value, transfer directions, and stream/event graph. This static artifact proves at most that the graph is correct and the configuration can be classified. Without a device timeline, report “eligible but unobserved” and no performance claim.

**Valid alternative:** One serialized stream with the same page-locked buffers is a valid fallback. A three-slot ring is also valid when every added slot has the same complete generation, completion, and reuse ledger.

**Common errors:** Treating a pageable copy as an overlap contract; mutating input after host enqueue; waiting for the kernel but not D2H; treating an event timestamp as interval overlap; inferring copy-engine use from nonzero capability; publishing speedup before correctness.

**Source basis:** The site's original [M09 chunk and capability contract](/en/memory/pinned-memory-transfer-overlap/), [M07 partial order](/en/memory/stream-ordering/), [M08 completion contract](/en/memory/event-dependencies-timing/), [Q05 measurement boundary](/en/correctness/timing-asynchronous-gpu-work/), [EX07](/en/examples/streams-events-overlap/), and [SRC-CUDA-025](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-025), plus NVIDIA [CUDA Programming Guide v13.3 Asynchronous Execution](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/02-basics/asynchronous-execution.html) and [CUDA Runtime API v13.3.1 Memory Management](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/group__CUDART__MEMORY.html).

**Source date:** 2026-08-29.

<span id="pb-r2-002" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-002: Repair an unconditional page-migration narrative

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-002 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Managed-memory evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [M10: Unified Memory and Page Migration](/en/memory/unified-memory-page-migration/).
- **Hardware gate:** No hardware for static modeling. A runtime migration claim requires native Linux, a CUDA GPU with managed-memory support, and an Environment Manifest recording the applicable memory paradigm, capability attributes, and topology.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M01](/en/memory/address-spaces/), [M02](/en/memory/coalescing-transactions/), [M10](/en/memory/unified-memory-page-migration/), [VIS08](/en/visuals/page-migration/), and [EX08](/en/examples/unified-memory-migration/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-29.

**Prompt:** A program allocates an array with `cudaMallocManaged`; the CPU writes pages A and B, the GPU reads B and C and writes C, and the CPU then reads C. It issues one prefetch but records no destination semantics, capability attributes, kernel completion, page size, fault, or residency data. From VIS08's colors, a reviewer claims, “Every first cross-processor touch migrates one 4 KiB page, and prefetch pins data permanently on the GPU.” Replace this statement with a conditional access ledger and an honest conclusion.

**Constraints:**

- Separate accessibility, residency, migration, remote or coherent access, and limited-support coarse movement. Do not choose one branch for an unknown system model.
- Preserve `CPU writes A/B -> order edge -> GPU reads B/C and writes C -> completion edge -> CPU reads C`, recording prior known state and permitted behaviors before every access.
- Prefetch and advice remain ordered requests or hints. Invent no page size, migration count, migrated bytes, duration, bandwidth, or placement history.

**Expected evidence:** A conditional ledger expanded by access episode, with a capability-query schema, correctness synchronization, possible cost terms, blank runtime observations, and the narrowest allowed claim.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- `cudaMallocManaged` is described as an ownership, access, and lifetime contract, released with `cudaFree` after every CPU and GPU user completes. A stable pointer does not imply stable physical locality.
- The ledger separately permits software-coherent fault and migration, hardware-coherent or direct remote access, and limited coarse movement. Every “migration candidate” names its premises.
- The manifest reserves at least concurrent-managed-access, pageable-memory-access, host-page-table, and direct-host-access attributes plus OS and kernel, driver, Toolkit, device and topology, access order, and synchronization.
- Delete the universal 4 KiB page and permanent-placement claims. VIS08, source order, and a prefetch call are not migration observations.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Ask first whether a processor may address the range, then where backing is local, and only then whether this system may service access by migration.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Write `cudaMemPrefetchAsync` as a stream-ordered request, not a residency lock.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired ledger starts from unknown residency. CPU writes A and B, then establishes order into the kernel. GPU access to B and C may trigger migration under a software-coherent model, may be served remotely under a hardware-coherent or direct-access model, or may be covered by movement at launch or synchronization under a limited model. CPU access to C after GPU completion uses the same conditional branches. Prefetch can move possible placement work earlier and needs correct stream or event order before consumption; advice remains a hint. Without recorded attributes, a fault or migration trace, or range queries, the only honest conclusion is “managed-access contract defined; migration behavior unobserved.”

**Valid alternative:** Retain on-demand, advised, and prefetched modes under one correctness oracle with separate observation rows. An M09 explicit-copy baseline is also valid when identified as a different data-movement contract.

**Common errors:** Treating one pointer as one memory; equating accessibility with residency; assuming every Linux GPU fault-migrates; deriving page size from M02's 32-byte segments; treating prefetch as permanent pinning; relabeling VIS08's transition count as profiler output.

**Source basis:** The site's original [M10 conditional-residency model](/en/memory/unified-memory-page-migration/), [VIS08](/en/visuals/page-migration/), [EX08](/en/examples/unified-memory-migration/), and [SRC-CUDA-026](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-026), plus NVIDIA [CUDA Programming Guide v13.3 Unified Memory](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/04-special-topics/unified-memory.html) and [CUDA Runtime API v13.3.1 Memory Management](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/group__CUDART__MEMORY.html).

**Source date:** 2026-08-29.

<span id="pb-r2-003" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-003: Repair a cross-stream allocation lifetime and pool claim

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-003 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Allocation-lifetime correctness debugging; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [M11: Stream-Ordered Allocation and Memory Pools](/en/memory/stream-ordered-allocation-memory-pools/).
- **Hardware gate:** No hardware for the static lifetime audit. A later allocator run requires a native Linux CUDA system and a checked observation that `cudaDevAttrMemoryPoolsSupported != 0`.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M07](/en/memory/stream-ordering/), [M08](/en/memory/event-dependencies-timing/), [M11](/en/memory/stream-ordered-allocation-memory-pools/), and [M14](/en/memory/cuda-graphs/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-29.

**Prompt:** The host calls `cudaMallocAsync(&p, n, s_alloc)`, then immediately submits two kernels that access `p` in `s_use_a` and `s_use_b` without allocation-completion edges. It next calls `cudaFreeAsync(p)` in `s_alloc`, relying on host submission order. The pool enables every reuse attribute and sets the release threshold to its maximum. A test requires the next allocation to return the same pointer and claims “zero allocation overhead and guaranteed speedup.” Repair the lifetime graph, pool policy, and acceptance criteria.

**Constraints:**

- Check both the memory-pool support query's API result and returned value. An enum in installed headers is not a device observation.
- Add an edge from allocation-operation completion to every first use and from every last use to free. Host-call return and submission order do not replace cross-stream dependencies.
- Keep logical pointer lifetime, pool backing-resource retention, reuse permission, pointer identity, and performance in separate columns.

**Expected evidence:** A DAG with allocation, two consumers, a join before free, pool attributes, and release policy, plus a static acceptance checklist that uses neither pointer equality nor speedup.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- A `ready` event is recorded after allocation in `s_alloc`, and both use streams wait. The last-use event from each use stream joins into the release stream, where free executes only after both complete.
- The pointer is accessible only after the allocation operation completes and before the free operation executes. Retaining pool backing resources does not extend a stale pointer's lifetime.
- Follow-event, opportunistic, and internal-dependency settings are allocator permissions. Internal dependencies may add ordering and do not prove free concurrency.
- The release threshold is neither a hard cap nor an exact retained-byte guarantee. Delete same-address, zero-overhead, and faster acceptance criteria.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Draw two lifetime boundaries: allocation completion to first use, and every last use to free.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>A pool can retain backing bytes after the logical allocation is dead. Those statements do not conflict.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** After the support gate passes, `s_alloc` submits allocation and records `ready`. Both `s_use_a` and `s_use_b` wait for `ready`, access `p`, and record `done_a` or `done_b` after their last access. A release stream waits for both event generations, then submits `cudaFreeAsync`. Pool selection separately records the default/current or explicit path. Reuse attributes describe permitted relationships only; the release threshold describes a policy for attempting to release excess backing near later synchronization points. Acceptance checks DAG reachability, API results, and the pointer-use interval, not the next address. With no allocation trace or timing records, there is no footprint, latency, or speedup conclusion.

**Valid alternative:** Place allocation, all uses, and free in one stream so same-stream order supplies the smallest valid lifetime. Explicit host or device synchronization after allocation or before the join is also valid, but it changes the concurrency contract and must be recorded.

**Common errors:** Treating `cudaMallocAsync` return as completion; joining only one consumer into free; relying on host enqueue order; assuming pool caching revives a stale pointer; requiring address equality; treating a maximum threshold as unlimited physical memory.

**Source basis:** The site's original [M11 lifetime and policy contract](/en/memory/stream-ordered-allocation-memory-pools/), [M07](/en/memory/stream-ordering/), [M08](/en/memory/event-dependencies-timing/), and [SRC-CUDA-027](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-027), plus NVIDIA [CUDA Programming Guide v13.3 Stream-Ordered Memory Allocation](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/04-special-topics/stream-ordered-memory-allocation.html) and [CUDA Runtime API v13.3.1 Memory Pools](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/group__CUDART__MEMORY__POOLS.html).

**Source date:** 2026-08-29.

<span id="pb-r2-004" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-004: Repair group-participant and cooperative-launch defects

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-004 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Cooperative-group correctness debugging; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [M12: Cooperative Groups and Composable Synchronization](/en/memory/cooperative-groups/).
- **Hardware gate:** No hardware for the static participant audit. A later block or tile run uses the native Linux CC 7.5+ baseline; grid synchronization additionally requires cooperative-launch capability, a legal resident grid, and `cudaLaunchCooperativeKernel`.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M05](/en/memory/synchronization-scopes/), [M06](/en/memory/warp-divergence-reconvergence/), [M12](/en/memory/cooperative-groups/), and [M13](/en/memory/asynchronous-copy-pipelines/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-29.

**Prompt:** In a 256-thread block, only threads with `threadIdx.x < 128` call `cg::tiled_partition<32>(block)`. Each resulting tile then lets only even ranks call `cg::reduce(tile, value, op)`. The same kernel obtains `this_grid()` and calls `grid.sync()`, but the host uses an ordinary `<<<grid, block>>>` launch without querying cooperative support or validating the resident-grid limit. Repair the group contracts without appealing to “the same warp probably runs in lockstep.”

**Constraints:**

- For every construction or collective, state the handle, membership, scope, required participants, and argument-agreement rule.
- Repair parent partition construction, tile collective, and grid collective separately. A dynamic active set cannot be renamed a permanent warp.
- The grid path explicitly queries capability, computes a legal cooperative grid, uses a cooperative host launch, and brings every grid member to the collective, or it uses a design requiring no grid barrier.

**Expected evidence:** A three-row group-contract ledger, repaired control-flow skeleton, cooperative-launch decision table, and empty runtime observations.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- The complete block obtains `block` and collectively constructs tiles before divergence. Every member of one tile reaches the same reduce instance; only per-thread `value` differs as the API permits.
- Branching that selects results occurs after the collective. Every tile or block synchronization participant set aligns with the accessible shared-storage scope.
- Grid synchronization appears only on a separate path with checked nonzero cooperative capability, an occupancy-derived legal size, and `cudaLaunchCooperativeKernel`.
- The current CUDA 13 baseline uses no archive-only multi-device Cooperative Groups API. A static repair grants no compilation or runtime evidence.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>`tiled_partition` is itself a parent-group collective, not merely a helper that creates a local object.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>`this_grid()` names a scope; the cooperative host launch makes the grid-wide synchronization contract valid.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** All 256 threads first obtain `block` and execute `tiled_partition<32>(block)`. Every tile's 32 members call the same `reduce(tile, value, cg::plus<int>())`; only a leader store occurs afterward. If only the first four tiles' results are needed, every parent member still completes partition and each selected tile follows its own collective and control contract. The host grid path checks `cudaDevAttrCooperativeLaunch`, limits the grid using active blocks per SM and SM count, uses `cudaLaunchCooperativeKernel`, and proves every grid thread reaches synchronization. If any gate fails, remove the grid barrier and use a kernel boundary or block-local algorithm.

**Valid alternative:** Split the inter-block phase into two kernels and use a kernel-completion boundary instead of `grid.sync()`. This preserves wider hardware coverage but requires a new intermediate-storage and launch-completion contract.

**Common errors:** Partitioning the parent inside a branch; allowing only leaders into a collective; treating matching call text as participation; calling `coalesced_threads()` a fixed 32-lane warp; using grid sync after an ordinary launch; projecting an archive multi-device example into CUDA 13.

**Source basis:** The site's original [M12 group contract](/en/memory/cooperative-groups/), [M05 scope model](/en/memory/synchronization-scopes/), [M06 participant reasoning](/en/memory/warp-divergence-reconvergence/), and [SRC-CUDA-028](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-028), plus NVIDIA [CUDA Programming Guide v13.3 Cooperative Groups](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/04-special-topics/cooperative-groups.html) and [CUDA Runtime API v13.3.1 Execution Control](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/group__CUDART__EXECUTION.html).

**Source date:** 2026-08-29.

<span id="pb-r2-005" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-005: Audit staged asynchronous-copy participants and evidence

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-005 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Pipeline contract and evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [M13: Asynchronous Copy and Staged Pipelines](/en/memory/asynchronous-copy-pipelines/).
- **Hardware gate:** No hardware for the static audit. A later API run requires native Linux and CC 7.0+; the hardware-accelerated global-to-shared path requires CC 8.0+, legal alignment and size, and a trivially copyable type.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M03](/en/memory/shared-memory-tiling/), [M05](/en/memory/synchronization-scopes/), [M08](/en/memory/event-dependencies-timing/), [M12](/en/memory/cooperative-groups/), and [M13](/en/memory/asynchronous-copy-pipelines/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-29.

**Prompt:** A two-stage shared pipeline lets out-of-bounds threads `return` before the first iteration, while the remaining threads call `producer_commit()` in divergent branches. A consumer reads shared values copied by other threads without group synchronization. The code asserts `cuda::aligned_size_t<16>` for a pointer offset by 4 bytes and targets CC 7.5. From source alone, a reviewer claims that the compiler emitted a hardware asynchronous-copy instruction and that copy overlapped compute. Repair the protocol and evidence table.

**Constraints:**

- Preserve a correct synchronous `load/store -> barrier -> use -> barrier-before-reuse` fallback before replacing any copy boundary.
- Declare a unified or partitioned pipeline, stage count, producer and consumer sets, each thread's role, bounds-neutral path, early-exit or quit rule, completion-before-use edge, and all-reads-before-reuse edge.
- Separate API availability, CC 8.0+ hardware-path eligibility, alignment and type proof, emitted artifact, and runtime overlap into five columns.

**Expected evidence:** A prologue, steady-state, and drain stage ledger; a full-participant control-flow repair; an alignment proof obligation; and an evidence matrix with no prefilled observations.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Bounds-invalid participants do not bypass the shared collective sequence. They submit zero useful bytes or neutral state under the selected overload, or follow documented `pipeline.quit()` before exit.
- Acquire, copy, commit, wait, peer-use synchronization, all-consumer completion, and release or reuse remain ordered. Divergent commits reconverge at the correct scope.
- Delete the false `aligned_size_t<16>` assertion or prove actual aligned ranges and byte counts. CC 7.5 meets only the applicable API gate, not the CC 8.0 accelerated-path gate.
- Source review proves at most the protocol and declared assumptions. An instruction requires a pinned build and artifact inspection; overlap or speedup requires exact-run runtime measurement.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Map M03's two barriers to “copy completes before use” and “all reads complete before stage reuse.”</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Target capability, an alignment annotation, and an API name are neither disassembly nor a timeline.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** Retain the one-buffer synchronous fallback as the correctness oracle. The asynchronous path creates a shared pipeline for complete participant sets. Every logical batch follows acquire, a legal copy or neutral path, converged commit, wait, required group synchronization, use, and release after all consumers finish. Prologue fill, steady-state rotation, and drain preserve the batch-to-stage mapping, including wait, use, and release for final batches. Offset 4 does not prove 16-byte alignment. If actual ranges, size multiples, and a trivially copyable type cannot be proved, use a runtime-checked or synchronous fallback. The CC 7.5 record permits only discussion of the API path; hardware-instruction and overlap columns remain “not proved.”
