**Valid alternative:** Partitioned producer and consumer roles may reduce participant work, but each role set needs its full collective sequence and cross-role visibility. Keeping the synchronous tile path is also valid.

**Common errors:** Returning an invalid lane; inflating a thread-local wait into block-wide peer visibility; omitting drain; committing divergently; asserting alignment falsely; relabeling CC 7.5 as CC 8.0; inferring an instruction or overlap from source.

**Source basis:** The site's original [M13 stage and participant contract](/en/memory/asynchronous-copy-pipelines/), [M03 synchronous baseline](/en/memory/shared-memory-tiling/), [M05](/en/memory/synchronization-scopes/), [M08 evidence boundary](/en/memory/event-dependencies-timing/), and [SRC-CUDA-029](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-029), plus NVIDIA [CUDA Programming Guide v13.3 Asynchronous Data Copies](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/04-special-topics/async-copies.html) and [Pipelines](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/04-special-topics/pipelines.html).

**Source date:** 2026-08-29.

<span id="pb-r2-006" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-006: Audit graph capture, replay, lifetime, and updates

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-006 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** CUDA Graph lifecycle evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [M14: CUDA Graphs and Repeated Launch Structure](/en/memory/cuda-graphs/).
- **Hardware gate:** No hardware for the static DAG and lifecycle audit. A later replay run requires native Linux, the CC 7.5+ baseline, graph support in the selected Runtime, and a complete Environment Manifest.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M07](/en/memory/stream-ordering/), [M08](/en/memory/event-dependencies-timing/), [M11](/en/memory/stream-ordered-allocation-memory-pools/), [M14](/en/memory/cuda-graphs/), and [EX09](/en/examples/graph-capture/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-29.

**Prompt:** Capture begins on `s0` and forks to `s1` through a captured event, but `s1` never rejoins `s0`. A library synchronizes a captured event during active capture, invalidating the capture. The program ignores the `cudaStreamEndCapture` error, attempts to instantiate a null graph, submits three asynchronous launches, immediately releases host and device buffers, and assumes a topology-changing update succeeded. A reviewer announces lower launch overhead from “three replays.” Write the complete repair and allowed claims.

**Constraints:**

- Draw the intended acyclic nodes and edges first, then audit the explicit Graph API and stream capture separately. Do not mix a construction mechanism with execution.
- The capture ledger includes origin stream, fork and rejoin, prohibited operations, invalidation unwind, end-capture result, and null-graph handling.
- Keep definition, instantiation, launch, completion, external-resource lifetime, template and executable destruction, and bounded updates separate. Supply no performance value.

**Expected evidence:** A corrected DAG, capture state machine, graph/executable/external-resource lifecycle table, update decision matrix, and blank compilation, runtime, and performance observations.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- `s1` joins the same capture through a captured event dependency and rejoins the origin before `cudaStreamEndCapture(s0)`. Remove prohibited query or synchronization calls during active capture.
- If an operation invalidates capture, the program still ends capture on the origin, checks the error and null graph, discards the capture, and never instantiates or launches the invalid result.
- A successful graph replays only after checked instantiation. Every launch is asynchronous stream work, and a final event or stream completion protects output consumption, resource release, and teardown.
- Check update status against topology, node, context, and parameter restrictions. An incompatible change creates a new graph and re-instantiates. Three submissions prove no overhead, concurrency, or speedup result.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>A capture failure does not erase stream state immediately; unwind still crosses the origin's end-capture boundary.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>The graph handle, executable handle, and external storage referenced by nodes have three different lifetimes.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** Define the expected DAG first and select capture as the construction path. `s0` begins capture and records a captured fork event; `s1` waits, submits supported work, and records a rejoin event; `s0` waits for that rejoin before ending capture. Any prohibited operation enters the invalidated path: end capture on `s0`, check failure and the null result, and skip instantiation. The success path checks instantiation and then replays under its contract. Immediate launch errors and completion covering final work are observed separately. Host and device buffers, callbacks, events, and other pointees remain live through the final relevant node's completion. Classify updates first; if a checked API rejects one, redefine and instantiate. This static review executes no graph, so its only conclusion is “lifecycle repaired; performance unobserved.”

**Valid alternative:** Use the explicit Graph API to construct the same DAG and avoid capture-state restrictions, or recapture and re-instantiate after topology changes. Both still require the same external-resource and completion ledger.

**Common errors:** Failing to rejoin an auxiliary stream; synchronizing a device or event during capture; ignoring end-capture failure; instantiating a null graph; treating instantiation as launch; freeing after asynchronous submission; assuming an update succeeded; inferring speedup from replay count.

**Source basis:** The site's original [M14 DAG, capture, and lifecycle contract](/en/memory/cuda-graphs/), [M07](/en/memory/stream-ordering/), [M08](/en/memory/event-dependencies-timing/), [EX09](/en/examples/graph-capture/), and [SRC-CUDA-030](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-030), plus NVIDIA [CUDA Programming Guide v13.3 CUDA Graphs](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/04-special-topics/cuda-graphs.html) and [CUDA Runtime API v13.3.1 Graph Management](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/group__CUDART__GRAPH.html).

**Source date:** 2026-08-29.

<span id="pb-r2-007" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-007: Audit an NVCC phase ledger

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-007 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Compilation-flow and evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [M15: NVCC Host/Device Compilation Flow](/en/toolchain/nvcc-compilation-flow/).
- **Hardware gate:** None. This is a static ledger only; it runs neither NVCC nor a host or GPU executable.
- **Version gate:** Every decision binds to Toolkit 11.8.0, the Toolkit 12.9.2 archive, or current NVCC v13.3 under Toolkit 13.3.1 and records that Lane's host compiler. An internal transcript or option support from one Lane cannot be projected into another.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [F04](/en/foundations/host-device-lifecycle/), [O04](/en/start/cpp17-for-cuda/), [M15](/en/toolchain/nvcc-compilation-flow/), [M18](/en/toolchain/separate-compilation-device-linking/), [EX10](/en/examples/ptx-fatbinary-inspection/), and [VIS09](/en/visuals/artifact-pipeline/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-29.

**Prompt:** A build note copies temporary files, internal tools, and subcommand order printed by `nvcc --dryrun` into a “stable API.” It claims that a `.cu` file is preprocessed once, `--ptx` produces a host-linkable object, `--compile` completes the final link, and producing an executable proves that a GPU kernel ran faster. Rewrite the note as a phase ledger and host/device trajectory reviewable across the selected Toolkit Lanes, with the narrowest evidence conclusion.

**Constraints:**

- Use only documented compilation phases, input suffixes, requested outputs, and stopping boundaries. Internal steps, temporary names, private options, and `--dryrun` order do not enter the stable build contract.
- Show device-compilation preprocessing and host-compilation preprocessing as separate paths. Trace PTX/cubin -> fatbinary on the device path and synthesized host C++ -> supported host compiler -> host object on the host path.
- Keep `--preprocess`, `--cuda`, `--ptx`, `--cubin`, `--fatbin`, `--compile`, final link, and `--run` separate. Invent no command result, artifact hash, execution, correctness, or performance observation.

**Expected evidence:** A phase ledger by selected Lane, input, phase option, primary output, and “not yet established”; a two-path artifact graph; and a claim repair whose compilation, runtime, and performance axes all remain unobserved.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- The ledger states that phase selection depends on both the input suffix and requested-output option. A documented phase is a stable interface; an internal step is not.
- For `.cu`, `--ptx`, `--cubin`, and `--fatbin` stop at device-only artifacts and discard host code. `--compile` produces a host-linkable object; no phase option enters the default compile-and-link path; `--run` remains a separate convenience phase.
- The host path retains its second preprocessing pass, fatbinary embedding, synthesized host C++, and supported host compiler. Host object, final host link, and execution are three distinct boundaries.
- Default device whole-program mode permits no unresolved cross-translation-unit device reference. The existence of a `.ptx`, `.cubin`, `.fatbin`, object, or executable grants no CUDA runtime or performance evidence.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>For each command, write what NVCC recognizes as input and at which documented output the caller requests it to stop.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Draw preprocessing once on the device path and once on the host path. Do not remove the host compiler from the NVCC trajectory.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired ledger retains only owner-documented phase boundaries. A `.cu` file is preprocessed along the device path and produces requested PTX or cubin before selected device images enter a fatbinary. The same input is preprocessed again for the host path, where NVCC synthesizes C++ for the selected supported host compiler and embeds the fatbinary; `--compile` stops no later than the host object. A separate final host link combines objects and libraries. `--run` means only that execution is attempted after building and still needs independent completion, oracle, and observation records. The default whole-program row rejects a cross-file device edge unless it adopts M18's RDC and device-link contract. Because this exercise executes neither compiler nor binary, every result, hash, and runtime field remains blank.

**Valid alternative:** Maintain three smaller ledgers for 11.8.0, 12.9.2, and 13.3.1, provided all use the same column contract, retain their own owner coordinates, and never treat an internal transcript as a phase interface.

**Common errors:** Treating an internal tool as a public phase; drawing only one preprocessing pass; calling `.ptx` a host object; calling `--compile` the final link; omitting the host compiler; asking the host linker to repair an external device reference in whole-program mode; treating executable existence as GPU execution.

**Source basis:** The site's original [M15 phase and trajectory contract](/en/toolchain/nvcc-compilation-flow/), [O04 compilation boundary](/en/start/cpp17-for-cuda/), [EX10 artifact contract](/en/examples/ptx-fatbinary-inspection/), [VIS09](/en/visuals/artifact-pipeline/), and [SRC-CUDA-031](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-031), plus NVIDIA's current [NVCC v13.3 Supported Phases](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-compiler-driver-nvcc/index.html#supported-phases), [12.9.2 archive](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/12.9.2/cuda-compiler-driver-nvcc/index.html#supported-phases), and [11.8.0 archive](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/11.8.0/cuda-compiler-driver-nvcc/index.html#supported-phases).

**Source date:** 2026-08-29.

<span id="pb-r2-008" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-008: Repair PTX, cubin, and fatbinary evidence classification

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-008 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Artifact-inspection evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [M16: PTX, Cubin, SASS, and Fatbinaries](/en/toolchain/ptx-cubin-fatbinary/).
- **Hardware gate:** None. Classification and a static inspection plan need no CUDA-capable GPU and execute no binary.
- **Version gate:** PTX ISA 7.8, 8.8, and 9.3 bind only to the Toolkit 11.8.0 archive, 12.9.2 archive, and selected Toolkit 13.3.1 current coordinate, respectively. Binary Utilities and NVCC come from the recorded Lane; current-tool output cannot retroactively characterize an archive artifact.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M15](/en/toolchain/nvcc-compilation-flow/), [M16](/en/toolchain/ptx-cubin-fatbinary/), [M17](/en/toolchain/compiler-architecture-targets/), [EX10](/en/examples/ptx-fatbinary-inspection/), and [VIS09](/en/visuals/artifact-pipeline/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-29.

**Prompt:** A report calls `.ptx` a “portable SASS binary,” calls `.cubin` a “fatbinary container,” records expected `cuobjdump --list-elf`, `--list-ptx`, and `--dump-sass` output as if observed, and infers from image inventory that the driver selected `sm_75`, JIT-compiled a fallback, executed a kernel, and gained speedup. Rebuild the artifact taxonomy, inspection record, and evidence ladder.

**Constraints:**

- For PTX, cubin, SASS, and fatbinary, separately state precise meaning, carrier, target scope, and what it cannot establish alone. Also separate standalone files from images embedded in a host object or executable.
- The PTX row retains `.version` and `.target`; the cubin row binds to a real architecture; SASS is only a machine-instruction view inspected from an architecture-specific binary; fatbinary is only a container.
- The inspection plan records exact tool and version, command, input path and hash, exit status, and complete output artifact. Inventory, selection, JIT, execution, correctness, and performance fields remain blank until real records exist.

**Expected evidence:** A four-layer artifact-classification matrix, a binary-image/PTX-JIT/no-candidate conditional tree, a `cuobjdump`/`nvdisasm` input-output ledger, and independent evidence rungs from static artifacts to runtime and performance.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- PTX is versioned virtual ISA text with `.version` and `.target`; a cubin is a CUDA ELF binary for a real target; SASS is a disassembly view of instruction encodings in a cubin; a fatbinary can carry one or more PTX or cubin images.
- Standalone `.ptx`, `.cubin`, and `.fatbin` remain separate from embedded payloads. A host executable does not become a cubin by carrying a fatbinary.
- `--list-elf` and `--list-ptx` establish inventory only; `--dump-sass` and `nvdisasm` establish an inspected instruction view only. Expected output cannot masquerade as a retained observation.
- Image presence proves no driver selection, PTX acceptance, JIT, cache behavior, module load, launch, completion, correctness, or speedup. This exercise performs no GPU execution.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Ask first whether the artifact is text, a binary, a container, or a human-readable view of a binary.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Inventory answers what is inside, not what the driver used for this launch.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired matrix records PTX as virtual ISA text, cubin as a real-architecture CUDA ELF, SASS as machine instructions inspected from a cubin, and fatbinary as a standalone or embedded multi-image container. The inspection ledger fixes the selected Toolkit Lane, tool version, hash, command, and output location for every input, but leaves observation cells blank. The conditional tree says only that an applicable binary image is a load candidate; otherwise compatible PTX can be a JIT candidate; when neither applies, a nonempty fatbinary remains non-executable. With no selection-specific runtime record, the allowed claim is only “classification and inspection plan complete; selection, JIT, and execution unobserved.”

**Valid alternative:** Inspect a standalone cubin first and use `cuobjdump` separately for embedded entries in a host binary, provided each path follows its supported input contract, retains complete output, and is never merged into a runtime trace.

**Common errors:** Treating PTX as native code; treating cubin and SASS as parallel file kinds; treating a fatbinary as one image; applying a current PTX version to an archive artifact; treating `--dump-sass` as an execution trace; inferring JIT or speedup from inventory.

**Source basis:** The site's original [M16 artifact vocabulary and evidence matrix](/en/toolchain/ptx-cubin-fatbinary/), [EX10 inspection contract](/en/examples/ptx-fatbinary-inspection/), [VIS09 static flow](/en/visuals/artifact-pipeline/), and [SRC-CUDA-032](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-032), plus NVIDIA's [current PTX ISA 9.3](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/parallel-thread-execution/index.html), [PTX ISA 8.8 archive](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/12.9.2/parallel-thread-execution/index.html), [PTX ISA 7.8 archive](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/11.8.0/parallel-thread-execution/index.html), and [current CUDA Binary Utilities 13.3](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-binary-utilities/index.html).

**Source date:** 2026-08-29.

<span id="pb-r2-009" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-009: Audit a SASS-plus-PTX target plan and `a`/`f` scope

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-009 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Compiler-target compatibility review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [M17: Choosing Compiler Architecture Targets](/en/toolchain/compiler-architecture-targets/).
- **Hardware gate:** None. This reviews command intent and a target ledger only and runs neither NVCC, a driver, nor a GPU.
- **Version gate:** Use only selected rows reviewed by F06. Baseline 7.5 appears in 11.8.0, 12.9.2, and 13.3.1; `90a`, `100f`, `100a`, `120f`, and `120a` enter only the reviewed 12.9.2 and 13.3.1 rows that accept them. Current 13.3 target documentation is not projected backward into the 11.8.0 archive.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [F06](/en/foundations/compute-capability/), [M16](/en/toolchain/ptx-cubin-fatbinary/), [M17](/en/toolchain/compiler-architecture-targets/), [EX10](/en/examples/ptx-fatbinary-inspection/), and [VIS09](/en/visuals/artifact-pipeline/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-29.

**Prompt:** A release packet substitutes `-arch=sm_120a` for a complete target plan and claims, “120 is greater than 100, so exact `120a` PTX covers `100a`, `100f`, and every future GPU; 11.8 accepts suffixes from current documentation; PTX in a fatbinary guarantees older-driver deployment.” Write named SASS-image plus same-scope PTX-fallback clauses for baseline 7.5, exact 9.0, and family 10.0 source requirements, then repair the Lane, suffix, and deployment conclusions.

**Constraints:**

- Each row begins with the source feature requirement, exact Toolkit Lane, virtual target, real target, PTX fallback target, and planned verification. Do not start from a GPU product name, shorthand, or capability-number ordering.
- Use complete, repeatable `--generate-code=arch=...,code=...` clauses. Every real target implements its paired virtual assumptions, and the PTX fallback preserves the same suffix scope.
- Separate command review, successful Lane build, artifact inspection, driver and deployment compatibility, runtime selection, and performance into six gates. This exercise fills no observation in the last five.

**Expected evidence:** A three-source-requirement by three-Toolkit-Lane plan ledger, a SASS/PTX clause pair for every eligible row, `a`/`f` scope-rejection notes, and a compatibility/evidence decision table.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- The baseline row uses `arch=compute_75,code=sm_75` and `arch=compute_75,code=compute_75`, recording the three Lanes only as owner-page-accepted plans rather than successful builds.
- The exact 9.0 row uses `compute_90a`/`sm_90a` plus `compute_90a` PTX. The family 10.0 row uses `compute_100f`/`sm_100f` plus `compute_100f` PTX. Both enter only selected 12.9.2 and 13.3.1 rows.
- `a` covers one exact compute capability and `f` covers only the same family named by the current owner table. Unsuffixed baseline, `a`, and `f` are not interchangeable by number, and same-scope PTX does not broaden the source contract.
- SASS plus PTX is an artifact strategy only. Accepted spelling, PTX presence, or fatbinary presence proves no embedded entry, minor or forward compatibility, driver selection, JIT, execution, or speedup.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>For every source requirement, write one `code=sm_...` clause and one `code=compute_...` clause without changing `arch=` for the PTX clause.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>`a` and `f` are feature scopes, not decimal comparison operators.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired packet separates the three requirements. Baseline 7.5 requests a `compute_75 -> sm_75` SASS image and `compute_75` PTX in all three Lanes. Exact 9.0 requests `compute_90a -> sm_90a` and `compute_90a` PTX in 12.9.2 and 13.3.1. Family 10.0 requests `compute_100f -> sm_100f` and `compute_100f` PTX in those same two Lanes. Qualified 11.8.0 rows say “absent from the selected reviewed dataset” rather than guessing acceptance. Independent build, inspection, compatibility, and runtime cells follow every plan; Minor and Forward Compatibility receive later review against exact driver, package, and feature contracts. The present conclusion only classifies target intent in selected owner rows and generates or selects no image.

**Valid alternative:** Produce separate artifacts for each deployment policy instead of placing every eligible clause in one fatbinary. Each artifact still needs its own source requirement, same-scope SASS/PTX pair, Lane build, and inspection record.

**Common errors:** Hiding two outputs behind `-arch` shorthand; treating `120a` as a superset of `100f`; pairing a real target weaker than virtual assumptions; using PTX with a different suffix as fallback; projecting a current accepted name into 11.8; inferring an older-driver path or future-GPU execution from PTX.

**Source basis:** The site's original [M17 target-plan contract](/en/toolchain/compiler-architecture-targets/), [F06 selected compiler rows](/en/foundations/compute-capability/), [M16 artifact contract](/en/toolchain/ptx-cubin-fatbinary/), [VIS09](/en/visuals/artifact-pipeline/), and [SRC-CUDA-033](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-033), plus NVIDIA's current [Feature Set Compiler Targets](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/05-appendices/compute-capabilities.html#feature-set-compiler-targets), [NVCC 13.3](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-compiler-driver-nvcc/index.html), [12.9.2 archive](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/12.9.2/cuda-compiler-driver-nvcc/index.html), [11.8.0 archive](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/11.8.0/cuda-compiler-driver-nvcc/index.html), and [CUDA Compatibility](https://docs.nvidia.com/deploy/cuda-compatibility/why-cuda-compatibility.html).

**Source date:** 2026-08-29.

<span id="pb-r2-010" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-010: Repair a separate-compilation device-link graph

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-010 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Device-link correctness debugging; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [M18: Separate Compilation and Device Linking](/en/toolchain/separate-compilation-device-linking/).
- **Hardware gate:** None. Repair only the source, object, and link graph; execute neither the final host artifact nor GPU code.
- **Version gate:** Whole-program, RDC, device-link, library, and object-compatibility decisions bind separately to current NVCC v13.3 under Toolkit 13.3.1, the 12.9.2 archive with page label v12.9, or the 11.8.0 archive. The device-linker version cannot predate the input objects' Toolkit version.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M15](/en/toolchain/nvcc-compilation-flow/), [M16](/en/toolchain/ptx-cubin-fatbinary/), [M17](/en/toolchain/compiler-architecture-targets/), [M18](/en/toolchain/separate-compilation-device-linking/), and [EX10](/en/examples/ptx-fatbinary-inspection/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-29.

**Prompt:** A kernel in `caller.cu` calls an external `__device__` function defined only in `device_math.cu`. The build applies `-dc` only to the caller, compiles the definition with ordinary `--compile`, stores the only device definition in a `.so`, skips explicit device link, and goes directly to host link. The two objects also give a same-named template different semantics through different `__CUDA_ARCH__` branches. From final-artifact existence, the author claims that device symbols resolved and the kernel can run. Repair the complete graph and allowed claims.

**Constraints:**

- List the declaration, definition, and cross-translation-unit device edge first, then apply `-dc = --relocatable-device-code=true --compile` to every participating CUDA source. RDC mode in one object cannot retroactively repair another whole-program object.
- Record complete inputs and outputs separately for RDC compile, explicit `--device-link`, the device-link output, and final host link. Keep static and shared libraries in separate device-linker and host-linker columns.
- Review device-code ABI, pointer size, link-compatible target, linker version, and header-defined weak or template `__CUDA_ARCH__` semantics. A hash, symbol list, or successful link is not a runtime observation.

**Expected evidence:** A `sources -> RDC objects -> device_link.o -> final host artifact` DAG, phase-command ledger, library-consumer matrix, object-compatibility and semantic checklist, and blank runtime and performance rows.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Both `caller.cu` and `device_math.cu` produce host objects containing relocatable device code. A declaration is neither a definition nor an observed resolved symbol.
- Device link receives every RDC object, eligible static archive, and the complete target set, resolves external device symbols, and produces a host object. Final host link still receives the original CUDA objects, `device_link.o`, and required host and CUDA libraries.
- The device linker can consider `.a` or `.lib` members containing RDC but ignores `.so` or `.dll`. A device definition present only in a shared library cannot satisfy this external device reference.
- Inputs pass ABI, pointer-size, link-compatible-SM, and linker-version gates and either share one compute target or remove `__CUDA_ARCH__`-dependent shared-header semantics. A successful link still proves no load, launch, correctness, or performance result.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>The device-link output is an object for the host linker, not the final executable.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Ask separately which objects and libraries the device linker and host linker see. One `-lfoo` does not make both consume the same content.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired source graph names the `caller.cu -> ex10_device_scale()` external edge and the definition in `device_math.cu`. Both compile with `-dc` under the same dialect, pointer size, and compatible target clauses, producing `caller.o` and `device_math.o`. Explicit `--device-link` consumes those objects and any static archive containing RDC, fixes the complete target set, and names `device_link.o`. Final link uses `--no-device-link` and receives both original objects, the device-link object, and required libraries so the two boundaries remain auditable. The `.so` remains a host-link input only and cannot supply the device definition. A header ledger either removes differing `__CUDA_ARCH__` behavior or unifies the compute target. With no qualifying command, log, or hash record and no executable execution, the only conclusion is “link graph repaired; build and runtime unobserved.”

**Valid alternative:** If no cross-file device edge is needed, place the required device definition inside the caller's whole-program visibility boundary and remove the RDC/device-link path. The definition can also enter an RDC-containing static archive when archive, target, and compatibility inputs are fully recorded.

**Common errors:** Applying `-dc` to one side only; treating an `extern` declaration as a resolved definition; asking the host linker to resolve a device symbol; treating a shared library as device-link input; dropping original CUDA objects and their host registration code; ignoring linker version or a `__CUDA_ARCH__` semantic mismatch; inferring execution from the final artifact.

**Source basis:** The site's original [M18 object and link contract](/en/toolchain/separate-compilation-device-linking/), [M15 phase boundary](/en/toolchain/nvcc-compilation-flow/), [M16 artifact vocabulary](/en/toolchain/ptx-cubin-fatbinary/), [EX10 device-link contract](/en/examples/ptx-fatbinary-inspection/), and [SRC-CUDA-034](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-034), plus NVIDIA's [current NVCC v13.3 Using Separate Compilation in CUDA](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-compiler-driver-nvcc/index.html#using-separate-compilation-in-cuda), [12.9.2 archive](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/12.9.2/cuda-compiler-driver-nvcc/index.html#using-separate-compilation-in-cuda), and [11.8.0 archive](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/11.8.0/cuda-compiler-driver-nvcc/index.html#using-separate-compilation-in-cuda).

**Source date:** 2026-08-29.

<span id="pb-r2-011" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-011: Audit a C++ dialect/toolchain gate and narrow probe

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-011 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Language-mode evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [M19: CUDA C++17, C++20, and C++23 Dialect Boundaries](/en/toolchain/cpp-dialect-boundaries/).
- **Hardware gate:** None. Matrix review and a qualifying probe are compile- and artifact-only, forbid host or GPU executable execution, and use Runtime-Not-Applicable.
- **Version gate:** The ordinary EX10 matrix is fixed at 11.8.0/C++17, 12.9.2/C++17+C++20, and 13.3.1/C++17+C++20. C++23 enters only a separate EX10 + Toolkit 13.3.1 + exact NVCC + supported GCC 14 narrow probe and cannot be projected to another Lane, host compiler, source, or phase.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [O04](/en/start/cpp17-for-cuda/), [M15](/en/toolchain/nvcc-compilation-flow/), [M19](/en/toolchain/cpp-dialect-boundaries/), [EX02](/en/examples/vector-addition/), and [EX10](/en/examples/ptx-fatbinary-inspection/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-29.

**Prompt:** A reviewer sees C++23 in the current CUDA 13.3 Programming Guide and Linux guide, then sees the retained EX10 GCC 14.2 probe pass and marks all three Toolkit Lanes C++23 Compile-Checked. They rely on the host compiler's default, propose GCC 13.3 with `--allow-unsupported-compiler`, and prepare to delete EX02's historical unsupported record. Repair the teaching matrix, documentation boundary, and retained-probe claim scope.

**Constraints:**

- Separate WG21 standard provenance, the CUDA language-support page, Linux host-compiler policy, the NVCC `--std` option reference, and an actual retained build record into five facts. None substitutes for another.
- Every row explicitly records Toolkit, NVCC, host compiler family and major, dialect option, source commit and range, checked phase, diagnostics, exit status, and artifact identity. Do not rely on a default dialect or unsupported-host bypass.
- Preserve the immutable `unsupported` history for CUDA 13.3.1 + NVCC 13.3.73 + GCC 13.3.0. Separately state the GCC 14.2 result from run 33275734951 as an exact retained `C++23-Dialect-Probe` pass, not ordinary C++23 support.

**Expected evidence:** A three-Lane ordinary/probe matrix, owner-documentation discrepancy ledger, immutable-history note, and a field-by-field scope audit of the retained EX10 GCC 14.2 successful-probe record.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Ordinary rows return to 11.8.0/C++17, 12.9.2/C++17+C++20, and 13.3.1/C++17+C++20. C++23 does not enter the ordinary matrix.
- Preserve the current Programming Guide and Linux guide C++23 declaration and GCC 14 minimum while also preserving that the current NVCC `--std` accepted-value list stops at C++20. This discrepancy proves neither a broad pass nor broad failure.
- The historical GCC 13.3 record still proves only that the exact CUDA 13.3.1/NVCC 13.3.73/GCC 13.3.0 probe does not support requested C++23. Do not delete or rewrite it or grant status to EX10.
- The retained EX10 record from run 33275734951 includes source commit `904c6da03800ed3012baacb861494377c0fa01f2` and the `cxx23-probe` range, an Ubuntu 24.04.4 container, Toolkit 13.3.1, NVCC 13.3.73, GCC 14.2.0, the exact command, a passing language guard, object hash and inspection, no executable execution, and Runtime-Not-Applicable. It therefore supports only the narrow recorded `C++23-Dialect-Probe` pass.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>What the standard is, what the owner declares supported, and whether this exact toolchain and source passed are three different questions.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>A successful exit is insufficient. The probe proves that the option was not ignored and retains a reviewable artifact.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired matrix retains only the three ordinary contracts and isolates C++23 in EX10's `cxx23-probe`. The five ordinary records are Compile-Checked in run 33275734951; the C++23 record establishes a narrow pass only at source commit `904c6da03800ed3012baacb861494377c0fa01f2`, Toolkit 13.3.1, NVCC 13.3.73, GCC 14.2.0, and Ubuntu 24.04.4 LTS. The documentation ledger still preserves that the current NVCC option page ends at C++20, and EX02's GCC 13.3 `unsupported` record remains unchanged. No host or GPU executable ran, and no ordinary C++23 support, other compiler or platform, runtime, or performance claim follows.

**Valid alternative:** Continue placing only C++20 in the ordinary 13.3.1 row. Clang 18+ or `nvc++` 24.3+ can form a different, equally narrow retained probe but cannot be inferred from the existing GCC 14.2 record.

**Common errors:** Using a WG21 draft to prove CUDA acceptance; treating documentation eligibility as compile evidence; relying on the host default; checking only exit status; using `--allow-unsupported-compiler`; deleting unsupported history; inferring probe success from a workflow or site build; generalizing one source's result to all C++23 or runtime behavior.

**Source basis:** The site's original [M19 dialect matrix and publication gate](/en/toolchain/cpp-dialect-boundaries/), [EX10 `cxx23-probe` contract](/en/examples/ptx-fatbinary-inspection/), [EX02 retained history](/en/examples/vector-addition/), and [SRC-CUDA-035](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-035), plus NVIDIA's current [CUDA C++ Language Support](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/05-appendices/cpp-language-support.html), [Linux Host Compiler Support Policy](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-installation-guide-linux/index.html#host-compiler-support-policy), [NVCC `--std`](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-compiler-driver-nvcc/index.html#std-c-03-c-11-c-14-c-17-c-20-std), [12.9.2 archive](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/12.9.2/cuda-compiler-driver-nvcc/index.html#std-c-03-c-11-c-14-c-17-c-20-std), [11.8.0 archive](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/11.8.0/cuda-compiler-driver-nvcc/index.html#options-for-altering-compiler-linker-behavior-std), and WG21 [N4950](https://www.open-std.org/jtc1/sc22/wg21/docs/papers/2023/n4950.pdf)/[N4951](https://www.open-std.org/jtc1/sc22/wg21/docs/papers/2023/n4951.html).

**Source date:** 2026-08-29.

<span id="pb-r2-012" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-012: Repair elementwise-map ownership and data-movement accounting

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-012 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Ownership and memory-movement contract review; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [A01: Elementwise Map and One Element per Owner](/en/algorithms/elementwise-map/).
- **Hardware gate:** None. This is static indexing, ownership, and traffic reasoning only; it neither compiles nor runs EX02 and executes no GPU.
- **Version gate:** Kernel, thread-hierarchy, and memory-management decisions bind separately to the current CUDA Programming Guide v13.3 and Runtime API v13.3.1, the 12.9.1 owner guide, or the 11.8.0 archive. No source coordinate constitutes build, runtime, or performance evidence.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [F03](/en/foundations/multidimensional-indexing/), [F04](/en/foundations/host-device-lifecycle/), [M02](/en/memory/coalescing-transactions/), [A01](/en/algorithms/elementwise-map/), and [EX02](/en/examples/vector-addition/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-30.

**Prompt:** A binary-map design makes each thread write `output[i]` and `output[i + 1]`, removes the `i < n` guard from a ceiling-divided grid, treats input/output aliasing as automatically safe, counts only one addition, and infers coalesced speedup directly from neighboring addresses. Repair it as a unique-owner contract, bounds-safe launch, and layered memory-movement ledger for `output[i] = transform(left[i], right[i])`.

**Constraints:**

- Declare the logical domain, read and write sets for every `i`, `owner(i)`, and the alias policy. Do not use a barrier or atomic to conceal overlapping writes.
- Separate device-global loads and stores per valid element from application-level H2D and D2H movement. Resident input or a downstream device consumer is a named lifecycle variant only.
- Record expected request counts and hypotheses to measure, not invented transactions, bandwidth, duration, correctness, or speedup observations.

**Expected evidence:** An index-to-owner table, write-set-disjointness and partial-block proof, kernel and transfer ledger in terms of `n` and `sizeof(T)`, and a repaired claim whose compilation, runtime, and performance axes remain unobserved.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Every `i` in `[0,n)` has exactly one owner whose global index is `i`, and that thread writes only `output[i]`. Write sets for distinct valid indices are disjoint.
- Ceiling division and `if (i < n)` are both retained. Extra threads in the final block issue no global load or store, and the launch policy for `n = 0` is stated separately.
- A binary map records two semantic device-load requests and one device-store request per valid element. H2D, kernel movement, and D2H occupy separate ledger rows, and aliasing is either forbidden or proven safe.
- Consecutive addresses establish only M02 expected-access reasoning. Without an Environment Manifest, actual output, and measurement record, no transaction, bandwidth, or speedup conclusion is published.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Draw one output index first: who reads which locations, and who may write this location? Then compare the write sets of two distinct indices.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Split the ledger into host-to-device, in-kernel loads and stores, and device-to-host. Do not relabel value-request counts as hardware-transaction counts.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired design computes `i = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x` and reads `left[i]` and `right[i]` and writes `output[i]` only when `i < n`. Coverage follows from the rounded-up grid, uniqueness from the global-index mapping, and isolation from the single output write; an ordinary out-of-place map needs neither an output atomic nor a block barrier. Aliasing is forbidden by default. If in-place operation is allowed, a separate proof shows that each thread overwrites only an element it has already read and no other thread still needs. The ledger separates resident and nonresident inputs, two device loads, one device store, and optional D2H movement. Only the static contract is complete, so all observed fields remain blank.

**Valid alternative:** Use a grid-stride loop, provided every iteration remains in bounds, each output still has one owner, and the ledger counts every processed element without recounting H2D or D2H per iteration.

**Common errors:** Letting neighboring threads write overlapping outputs; assuming ceiling division supplies bounds automatically; assuming alias safety; merging device loads/stores with PCIe movement; calling request counts transaction counts; inferring speedup from consecutive addresses or little arithmetic.

**Source basis:** The site's original [A01 ownership and memory-movement contract](/en/algorithms/elementwise-map/), [F04 lifecycle boundary](/en/foundations/host-device-lifecycle/), [M02 transaction reasoning](/en/memory/coalescing-transactions/), and [SRC-CUDA-036](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-036), plus NVIDIA's [CUDA Programming Guide v13.3 Thread Hierarchy](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/02-basics/writing-cuda-kernels.html#thread-hierarchy) and [CUDA Runtime API v13.3.1 Memory Management](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/group__CUDART__MEMORY.html).

**Source date:** 2026-08-30.

<span id="pb-r2-013" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-013: Repair reduction stages, barriers, and operation order

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-013 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Parallel-stage correctness debugging; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [A02: Multi-stage Reduction, Barriers, and Operation Order](/en/algorithms/multi-stage-reduction/).
- **Hardware gate:** None. This reviews a reduction tree and phase ledger only; it neither compiles nor runs EX11 and executes no GPU.
- **Version gate:** Block synchronization and floating-point order bind separately to the current CUDA Programming Guide v13.3, 12.9.1 owner-guide archives, or 11.8.0 archives. The CUB production comparison is pinned to CCCL v3.4.2 tagged source.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M03](/en/memory/shared-memory-tiling/), [M05](/en/memory/synchronization-scopes/), [M06](/en/memory/warp-divergence-reconvergence/), [A02](/en/algorithms/multi-stage-reduction/), [Q02](/en/correctness/floating-point-order-reproducibility/), [EX11](/en/examples/multi-stage-reduction/), and [VIS10](/en/visuals/reduction-stages/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-30.

**Prompt:** A reduction kernel lets edge lanes return before writing identity, places `__syncthreads()` inside `if (tid < stride)`, attempts to make other blocks' partials visible with one block barrier, and requires a floating-point tree to match a CPU serial left fold bit for bit. Repair the block tree, inactive-lane participation, multi-kernel stages, and numerical claim.

**Constraints:**

- Declare `combine`, identity, input domain, and exact tree edges before listing each stage's partial owner, active lanes, inactive lanes, and shared slots.
- Every block phase follows `write -> barrier reached by all participants -> next read`. Ordinary `__syncthreads()` cannot serve as a grid-wide rendezvous.
- Separate tree order, serial-reference order, problem-specific tolerance, determinism, and bitwise reproducibility. CUB `DeviceReduce` is only the CCCL v3.4.2 production baseline.

**Expected evidence:** A reduction tree with operand edges, an active/inactive participant ledger, an `input -> P0 -> P1 -> scalar` kernel-stage DAG, and an operation-order and claim matrix.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Every valid input enters the declared tree exactly once. Invalid slots in a partial block receive identity and cannot return before a barrier they still must reach.
- Active lanes perform the declared combine; inactive lanes do not combine but reach the same block barrier. A data predicate does not enclose the barrier.
- Every block writes one partial to global memory. The next same-stream kernel launch consumes the complete partial array, so a kernel boundary rather than a block barrier supplies the cross-block stage boundary.
- A floating-point tree may differ from a serial fold. The report records type, tree, launch shape, compiler flags, reference, and a pre-justified tolerance; it does not automatically call a mismatch a race or infer speedup from CUB's presence.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>At each stage, write who combines and who must participate in the barrier as two separate sets. They need not be identical.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>End the kernel after storing block partials, then begin the next level. Afterward draw the tree order beside the CPU left fold.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** Every repaired block first has all lanes store a valid input or identity, reaches one uniform barrier, and then reduces through a fixed-stride tree. Only lanes with `tid < stride` update partials in each round, but the complete block reaches the next barrier until lane 0 stores the block partial. The host repeatedly launches the stage kernel in one stream so the partial count shrinks level by level; no step uses `__syncthreads()` to synchronize an ordinary grid. The numerical ledger identifies the GPU tree and CPU left fold as distinct operation sequences and delegates tolerance and reproducibility to Q02. No build or run occurred, so only the phase proof is published, not bits, timing, or a CUB comparison result.

**Valid alternative:** Replace the block-local tree with Cooperative Groups or warp collectives that have equivalent participant and mask proofs, or adopt CUB `DeviceReduce` directly. Every option still records cross-block completion and numerical order independently.

**Common errors:** Early return by an invalid lane; a conditional barrier; treating inactive as exited; using a block barrier for a grid; omitting identity in the final partial block; assuming parallel regrouping equals serial bits; treating a library baseline as an observed winner.

**Source basis:** The site's original [A02 stage, barrier, and order contract](/en/algorithms/multi-stage-reduction/), [Q02 numerical contract](/en/correctness/floating-point-order-reproducibility/), [VIS10](/en/visuals/reduction-stages/), and [SRC-CUDA-037](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-037), plus NVIDIA's [CUDA Programming Guide v13.3 Thread Block Synchronization](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/02-basics/writing-cuda-kernels.html#thread-block-synchronization) and CCCL v3.4.2 tagged [`device_reduce.cuh`](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cccl/blob/v3.4.2/cub/cub/device/device_reduce.cuh).

**Source date:** 2026-08-30.

<span id="pb-r2-014" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-014: Repair scan dependencies, block sums, and offset propagation

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-014 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Prefix-dependency and hierarchy debugging; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [A03: Inclusive and Exclusive Scan](/en/algorithms/inclusive-exclusive-scan/).
- **Hardware gate:** None. This derives recurrences, stages, and offsets only; it neither compiles nor runs EX12 and executes no GPU.
- **Version gate:** Thread cooperation, block barriers, and scan context bind separately to the current CUDA Programming Guide v13.3, the 12.9.1 owner guide, or the 11.8.0 archive. The production baseline is pinned to CCCL v3.4.2 tagged `DeviceScan` source.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [A02](/en/algorithms/multi-stage-reduction/), [M05](/en/memory/synchronization-scopes/), [A03](/en/algorithms/inclusive-exclusive-scan/), and [EX12](/en/examples/inclusive-exclusive-scan/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-30.

**Prompt:** A purported parallel scan makes lane `i` read `output[i - 1]` while that array is being updated in place, mixes inclusive and exclusive recurrences, uses the last physical lane as a partial block sum without stating an identity-padding or full-width-scan proof, and treats inclusive block totals as block offsets. It then tries to complete whole-grid propagation in one ordinary kernel with `__syncthreads()`. Rebuild the correct dependency graph.

**Constraints:**

- State `op`, identity, input order, inclusive and exclusive contracts, and the empty and partial-block policies. Do not reorder operands of a noncommutative associative operation.
- Every parallel stage reads only a stable prior-stage snapshot. Unless an in-place implementation has a complete read/write proof, use ping-pong shared buffers and uniform barriers.
- Separate local prefixes, block sums, exclusive block offsets, and propagation into independently verifiable stages connected by kernel boundaries.

**Expected evidence:** A comparison of the sequential recurrence and staged dependency graph, a partial-block snapshot and barrier ledger, a block-sum-to-offset-to-propagation DAG, and invariants for every level.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- For `x = [3,1,4,2]` with addition identity 0, inclusive is `[3,4,8,10]` and exclusive is `[0,3,4,8]`. Every generalized formula preserves original input order.
- Each stage at distance `1,2,4,...` reads only the prior snapshot. All participating lanes reach the barrier, and an edge lane supplies identity instead of returning early.
- The block sum equals the valid slice total. Canonical EX12's identity-padded full-width scan may take it from the physical last lane; another implementation reads the logical last valid prefix or proves an equivalent path. For totals `[10,7,5]`, exclusive block offsets are `[0,10,17]`; `[10,17,22]` is not used as the offsets.
- Completed kernel stages connect local scan, block-sum scan, and offset propagation. Formulas and CCCL source provide no EX12 runtime, timing, or speedup evidence.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Write the sequential inclusive and exclusive recurrences first, then ask which prior-time snapshot every parallel stage requires.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>A block sum answers how much this block contains; a block offset answers how much all earlier blocks contain. Exclusive-scan the sums first.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired design first stores each valid input or identity in a shared snapshot, then processes distances 1, 2, and 4 by reading the prior buffer and writing the next buffer, with a uniform block barrier between rounds. Every block retains local prefixes. Canonical EX12 scans its identity-padded full-width snapshot, so the physical last lane produces the valid slice's block sum; another implementation may read the logical last valid prefix. A second stage exclusive-scans the sums in block order, and a third kernel combines the owning block offset into each local prefix with `op`. Kernel boundaries supply grid-wide completion. Inclusive and exclusive relations, partial-block totals, and the final recurrence are statically reviewable; there is no EX12 execution or performance observation.

**Valid alternative:** Recursively use the same scan primitive for block sums, or adopt CCCL v3.4.2 CUB `DeviceScan` in production. Both retain original order, identity, temporary-storage and stream lifetimes, and an independent evidence boundary.

**Common errors:** Concurrent reads and writes of one snapshot; exchanging noncommutative operands; omitting identity at exclusive lane 0; reading the physical last lane without an identity-padding/full-width proof; treating a block sum as a block offset; using a block barrier for a grid; inferring speedup from lower dependency depth.

**Source basis:** The site's original [A03 dependency and offset contract](/en/algorithms/inclusive-exclusive-scan/), [A02 stage model](/en/algorithms/multi-stage-reduction/), and [SRC-HIST-003](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-hist-003)/[SRC-CUDA-038](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-038), plus Blelloch's original historical source [Prefix Sums and Their Applications](https://www.cs.cmu.edu/afs/cs.cmu.edu/project/scandal/public/papers/CMU-CS-90-190.html) and CCCL v3.4.2 tagged [`device_scan.cuh`](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cccl/blob/v3.4.2/cub/cub/device/device_scan.cuh). Blelloch is used for history only, not as current CUDA interface authority.

**Source date:** 2026-08-30.

<span id="pb-r2-015" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-015: Separate histogram atomic correctness, contention, and privatization

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-015 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Atomic and privatization correctness review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [A04: Privatized Histogram](/en/algorithms/privatized-histogram/).
- **Hardware gate:** None. This reviews exact-count and phase contracts only; it neither compiles nor runs EX13 and executes no GPU.
- **Version gate:** Atomic, shared-memory, and barrier semantics bind separately to the current CUDA Programming Guide v13.3, the 12.9.1 owner guide, or the 11.8.0 archive. The CUB comparison is pinned to CCCL v3.4.2 tagged `DeviceHistogram` source.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M03](/en/memory/shared-memory-tiling/), [M05](/en/memory/synchronization-scopes/), [A04](/en/algorithms/privatized-histogram/), and [EX13](/en/examples/privatized-histogram/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-30.

**Prompt:** A histogram optimization calls plain `bins[b] += 1` a safe update and then claims global `atomicAdd` eliminates contention. It has only one thread clear one shared bin, lets bounds-invalid threads return before both barriers, performs a non-atomic global merge, and infers speedup from “fewer global atomics.” Repair the correctness and evidence contracts.

**Constraints:**

- Declare the `bin_of` range, out-of-range policy, counter-overflow boundary, exact CPU counts, and sum-of-bins invariant.
- Separate global-atomic correctness, same-address contention, per-block shared privatization, and cross-block merge. Shared updates can still need atomics after privatization.
- Prove the complete `zero -> barrier -> update -> barrier -> merge` sequence, and treat the input distribution only as a contention hypothesis to measure.

**Expected evidence:** An exact histogram oracle, global/shared update-address map, five-stage participant ledger, per-bin merge proof, and a repaired claim with no timing or speedup observation.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Every valid sample contributes exactly once and the sum of all bins equals the valid sample count. Bin bounds, discard/clamp/reject policy, and counter capacity are explicit.
- The direct baseline uses `atomicAdd` for concurrent global updates to one destination to prevent lost updates. Atomic correctness does not mean hot-bin contention vanished or performance is known.
- Threads clear every shared bin with a stride, and all participants reach the initialization and update barriers. An invalid sample skips only its update and cannot leave the synchronization path early.
- Merging per-block counts into shared global bins retains an atomic cross-block update or uses another proven merge stage. Only an exact match, Environment Manifest, and measurement record can support a performance conclusion.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>First ask what a plain read-modify-write loses when two threads hit one bin, then place correctness and waiting cost in separate columns.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Check whether every shared bin is zeroed, who reaches both barriers, and whether multiple blocks ultimately write the same global address.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired global baseline completes bin initialization before each valid sample atomically increments one valid global bin. In the privatized variant, all block threads stride over and zero shared bins, reach one uniform barrier, perform shared atomic updates to block-private bins, reach a second uniform barrier, and stride over the merge. Because blocks still share global outputs, the merge uses global atomic increments unless a separate merge kernel is proven. The CPU oracle supplies exact per-bin counts and the total-count invariant. Skewed and uniform distributions enter only the future-input ledger; without a runtime record there is no contention-cost or speedup claim.

**Valid alternative:** Retain the simple global-atomic baseline when the bin count is small or shared storage is unsuitable. Per-block partial histograms may instead live in global memory and be reduced by a separate kernel when storage, completion, and the exact merge proof are complete.

**Common errors:** A plain-increment race; equating atomic correctness with no contention; clearing only `blockDim.x` bins; early return by an invalid thread; omitting the post-update barrier; a non-atomic cross-block merge; declaring acceleration directly from fewer global atomic opportunities.

**Source basis:** The site's original [A04 histogram and privatization contract](/en/algorithms/privatized-histogram/), [M05 synchronization boundary](/en/memory/synchronization-scopes/), and [SRC-CUDA-039](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-039), plus NVIDIA's [CUDA Programming Guide v13.3 Writing SIMT Kernels](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/02-basics/writing-cuda-kernels.html) and CCCL v3.4.2 tagged [`device_histogram.cuh`](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cccl/blob/v3.4.2/cub/cub/device/device_histogram.cuh).

**Source date:** 2026-08-30.

<span id="pb-r2-016" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-016: Repair numerical-order, FMA, tolerance, and reproducibility claims

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-016 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Numerical-contract and reproducibility review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [Q02: Floating-point Order, Determinism, and Bitwise Reproducibility](/en/correctness/floating-point-order-reproducibility/).
- **Hardware gate:** None. This is a static operation-graph and claim review only; it does not compile EX11, produce observed bits, or execute a GPU.
- **Version gate:** Associativity, FMA, compiler-option, and deterministic-result decisions bind separately to current CUDA v13.3, the 12.9.1 archives, or the 11.8.0 archives. Product-specific determinism binds only to the CCCL v3.4.2 tagged policy and is not projected onto a custom kernel.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [Q01](/en/correctness/cpu-references-tolerances-invariants/), [A02](/en/algorithms/multi-stage-reduction/), [Q02](/en/correctness/floating-point-order-reproducibility/), and [EX11](/en/examples/multi-stage-reduction/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-30.

**Prompt:** A report sees the final few bits differ between a GPU reduction and CPU serial reference and declares a race. It describes FMA as identical to separate multiply and add with two roundings, chooses one universal epsilon after seeing the candidate, and labels a single tolerance pass both deterministic and bitwise reproducible across GPUs. Rebuild the numerical and evidence contracts.

**Constraints:**

- Draw exact operation graphs for the CPU fold and GPU tree, recording type, rounding, and operand order at every node. Diagnose nonassociativity separately from a race.
- Separate one-rounding FMA `rn(a*b+c)`, the two-rounding sequence `rn(rn(a*b)+c)`, contraction policy, explicit intrinsics, and compiler flags.
- Justify problem-specific `atol` and `rtol` before inspecting the candidate, then issue three independent verdicts for numerical acceptance, same-scope determinism, and bitwise reproducibility over a named scope.

**Expected evidence:** Two operation graphs annotated with rounding, a compiler and environment ledger, an FMA and contraction decision table, a tolerance rationale, and a blank evidence plan for repeated and cross-scope bit comparisons.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- `(1e20f + -1e20f) + 3.14f` and `1e20f + (-1e20f + 3.14f)` illustrate binary32 nonassociativity. Their difference alone proves neither a race nor that either result satisfies the application contract.
- Rounding counts for FMA and separate multiply/add, implicit contraction, and explicit FMA are recorded separately. `--use_fast_math` is not abbreviated as “turn on FMA only.”
- Finite comparison uses `abs(g-r) <= atol + rtol*abs(r)`. Thresholds are chosen in advance from scale, precision, reduction length and tree, conditioning, and error budget, with separate NaN, infinity, and signed-zero policy.
- A tolerance pass grants numerical acceptance only. Determinism fixes same-input, build, device, launch, library, run scope, and equality predicate; bitwise reproducibility separately names exact bits, environment, and comparison scope and is not projected automatically across Toolkits or GPUs.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Draw the parentheses around every addition and multiply-add first. The same inputs do not imply the same floating-point operation sequence.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Write one predicate and artifact set for each of “numerically acceptable,” “same over repeated runs,” and “bit-identical across the declared environments.”</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired report preserves the differing pairings in the CPU left fold and GPU tree and records rounding at every step; it reports a race only if a separate memory-order defect exists. The FMA row records one rounding and the separate row records product and sum roundings while preserving `--fmad`, `--use_fast_math`, target, and explicit-intrinsic fields. The comparator fixes its abs-plus-rel rationale and non-finite policy before the candidate. The final table separates one numerical pass, repeated-run bit equality for one executable, and bitwise comparison across named builds and devices. No repeated output or cross-environment artifact exists, so the last two remain unobserved.

**Valid alternative:** If requirements need only numerical correctness, stop at the problem-specific tolerance verdict. If a narrower bitwise claim is required, fix one operation graph, explicit FMA policy, exact build, device, input, and bit comparator, then verify that scope with retained repeated-run records.

**Common errors:** Calling nonassociativity a race; assuming a parallel tree equals a serial fold; treating FMA as two roundings; ignoring compiler defaults; tuning epsilon afterward; treating tolerance acceptance as determinism; projecting one-device repetition across architectures; applying a CCCL policy to any kernel.

**Source basis:** The site's original [Q02 order, FMA, and reproducibility contract](/en/correctness/floating-point-order-reproducibility/), [Q01 comparator contract](/en/correctness/cpu-references-tolerances-invariants/), [A02 tree-order model](/en/algorithms/multi-stage-reduction/), and [SRC-CUDA-040](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-040), plus NVIDIA's [Floating Point and IEEE 754 v13.3 Operations and Accuracy](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/floating-point/index.html#operations-and-accuracy) and CCCL v3.4.2 tagged [`DeviceReduce` determinism contract](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cccl/blob/v3.4.2/cub/cub/device/device_reduce.cuh).

**Source date:** 2026-08-30.

<span id="pb-r2-017" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-017: Prove rectangular-transpose tile and padding contracts

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-017 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Transpose correctness debugging; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [A05: Matrix Transpose, Coalescing, and Shared-Memory Layout](/en/algorithms/matrix-transpose-layout/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review static mapping, phase, bank, and evidence ledgers without compiling or running CUDA.
- **Version gate:** CUDA Programming Guide v13.3 and the 11.8.0/12.9.1 owner guides; EX14 C++17 lanes for Toolkit 11.8.0, 12.9.2, and 13.3.1.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M02](/en/memory/coalescing-transactions/), [M03](/en/memory/shared-memory-tiling/), [M04](/en/memory/bank-conflicts-layouts/), [A05](/en/algorithms/matrix-transpose-layout/), [EX14](/en/examples/tiled-transpose/), and [VIS11](/en/visuals/tiled-transpose/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-30.

**Prompt:** A transpose review tests only a `32x32` square input, uses input columns as the output leading dimension, lets edge threads return before the barrier, and treats the padding column in `tile[32][33]` as a 33rd logical output column. It also claims from source layout that bank conflicts disappeared and the kernel is faster. Repair the complete correctness, layout, and evidence contract.

**Constraints:**

- For row-major `5x7` and `33x35` fixtures, state `output[col*rows+row] = input[row*columns+col]`, output shape, block/tile coordinates, and independent load/store guards.
- For each phase, list the global input owner, shared write/read coordinates, barrier participants, and global output owner. An invalid data owner cannot exit the rendezvous.
- Keep logical `32x32` separate from physical `32x33` in the selected 32-bank/32-bit-word model. Fill only expected bank formulas; measured fields remain blank.

**Expected evidence:** Two rectangular mapping and edge tables, a `load -> all threads barrier -> transposed store` ledger, a logical/physical layout comparison, bank-index derivation, and an expected/observed claim matrix.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Output extents are `7x5` and `35x33`, with exactly one owner per input and output index; any leading dimension that works only for squares is rejected.
- The 256 one-dimensional threads of a partial block each own four local slots at `threadIdx.x + k*blockDim.x` and uniformly reach the one barrier; every legal store reads a slot defined by a legal load.
- Padding changes physical row stride from 32 to 33 and changes no logical coordinate, global allocation, or output value.
- Source arithmetic supports only expected access and bank reasoning. Without a retained GPU record, it publishes no transaction, conflict counter, timing, bandwidth, or speedup.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Use inverse mapping `(out_row,out_col) -> (in_row=out_col,in_col=out_row)` to prove the rectangular bijection.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Separate data predicates from the barrier participant set, then use `(lane*stride+column) mod 32`.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired oracle allocates a `columns x rows` output for a `rows x columns` input and exact-compares the declared mapping. The kernel ledger gives each of 256 one-dimensional threads four guarded load slots at `threadIdx.x + k*blockDim.x`, all threads reach an unconditional barrier, and the store phase independently linearizes output-local coordinates before reading the exchanged shared coordinate. Inverse coordinates prove that every legal output reads a source slot satisfying input bounds. The logical tile remains `32x32`; the physical +1 slot changes only row stride. In the selected bank model, stride 32 maps a fixed column to one bank while stride 33 visits all banks, but that is neither an actual conflict observation nor speed evidence. EX14 remains Pending Hardware Verification.

**Valid alternative:** A different conflict-avoiding swizzle or rectangular tile is valid if it re-declares physical mapping, capacity, guards, and the logical oracle. An unpadded correctness baseline is also a valid measurement comparator.

**Common errors:** Testing only square matrices; mixing rows and columns in leading dimensions; exchanging only thread coordinates; returning early at an edge; treating padding as logical data; inferring a profiler counter or speedup from a bank formula.

**Source basis:** The site's original [A05](/en/algorithms/matrix-transpose-layout/), [EX14](/en/examples/tiled-transpose/), [VIS11](/en/visuals/tiled-transpose/), and [SRC-CUDA-041](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-041), plus NVIDIA [CUDA Programming Guide v13.3 Writing SIMT Kernels](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/02-basics/writing-cuda-kernels.html).

**Source date:** 2026-08-30.

<span id="pb-r2-018" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-018: Repair a stencil boundary, halo, and barrier ledger

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-018 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Stencil correctness debugging; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [A06: Stencil Neighborhoods, Halos, and Cooperative Reuse](/en/algorithms/stencil-neighborhood-reuse/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review static boundary, halo-coverage, and participant ledgers without running CUDA.
- **Version gate:** CUDA Programming Guide v13.3 and the 11.8.0/12.9.1 shared-memory, stencil-context, and synchronization owner guides.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M03](/en/memory/shared-memory-tiling/), [M04](/en/memory/bank-conflicts-layouts/), [M05](/en/memory/synchronization-scopes/), and [A06](/en/algorithms/stencil-neighborhood-reuse/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-30.

**Prompt:** A radius-1 square 2D stencil mixes clamp and zero policies at edges, loads the center and four side strips but misses corner halos, and returns threads without outputs before cooperative loading. Its report divides direct logical requests by staged slots and calls the ratio speedup. Rebuild a complete, reviewable baseline.

**Constraints:**

- Fix one boundary policy for the CPU oracle, global loader, and shared consumer. State the output domain and negative/out-of-range coordinate handling.
- Derive the complete `8x10` staged rectangle for a `6x8` output tile, label center, side halos, and four corners, and prove coverage and uniqueness under block-stride assignment.
- Every block participant reaches the barrier after its load loop. Keep reuse arithmetic separate from observed performance.

**Expected evidence:** A boundary table, 80-slot owner map, corner coordinates, participant ledger, direct/staged request equations, and a blank runtime/performance record.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- The selected policy is the one mathematical contract and issues no out-of-bounds read; corner coordinates apply the policy on both axes.
- Forty-eight center slots, 28 non-corner side-halo slots, and four corners cover 80 slots exactly once.
- A thread without an output may still own a halo slot and reaches the barrier; the compute predicate does not enclose the rendezvous.
- Direct `6*8*9=432` logical references versus 80 staged positions is only a reuse opportunity, not a transaction or speedup claim.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>The rectangle corners are `(0,0)`, `(0,9)`, `(7,0)`, and `(7,9)`.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Assign a writer to every staged linear index before mapping the coordinate to a global input or boundary value.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired baseline selects symmetric zero policy: an out-of-domain coordinate produces 0 and issues no global read. A `6x8` output and radius 1 require an `8x10` staged rectangle; a block-stride loop partitions all 80 linear slots and naturally includes the corners. Every thread completes the loop and reaches the uniform barrier regardless of load count or output validity. Valid owners then read their 3x3 neighborhoods. The static ledger shows that 432 logical references can use 80 staged slots, but it contains no cache, transaction, barrier-cost, or environment observation, so performance remains `unrecorded`.

**Valid alternative:** Clamp, periodic, or valid policy is acceptable with a fully recomputed output domain and reference. A cross-shaped stencil may omit corners after replacing the square-footprint proof.

**Common errors:** Mixing boundary policies; omitting corner halos; returning a no-output thread early; duplicating or missing staged writers; treating a logical-request ratio as hardware traffic or elapsed-time ratio.

**Source basis:** The site's original [A06](/en/algorithms/stencil-neighborhood-reuse/) and [SRC-CUDA-042](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-042), plus NVIDIA [CUDA Programming Guide v13.3 Asynchronous Data Copies stencil context](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/04-special-topics/async-copies.html). The A06 baseline remains synchronous; the owner example is not source code for this site.

**Source date:** 2026-08-30.

<span id="pb-r2-019" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-019: Audit direct convolution and a production-library decision

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-019 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Convolution and library evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [A07: Direct 2D Convolution, Neighborhood Reuse, and Layout Contracts](/en/algorithms/convolution-reuse-layout/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review static tensor, operation, reuse, and library contracts without executing CUDA or cuDNN.
- **Version gate:** Retains the 2026-08-30 CUDA/cuDNN source review; L10 supplies a separately dated graph/plan continuation without refreshing old facts or execution evidence.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [A06](/en/algorithms/stencil-neighborhood-reuse/), [M03](/en/memory/shared-memory-tiling/), [A07](/en/algorithms/convolution-reuse-layout/), and optional [L10](/en/libraries/cudnn-graphs-and-plans/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-30.

**Prompt:** A custom direct-convolution proposal states no NCHW strides, filter orientation, stride, dilation, padding, or accumulation policy and tests only a symmetric filter on square input. It treats one cuDNN heuristic candidate as a selected plan and claims both “the library is optimized” and “shared reuse” prove their respective path is faster. Repair its semantics, correctness, and production-decision packet.

**Constraints:**

- Declare dense addresses for `X[N,C,H,W]`, `F[K,C,R,S]`, and `Y[N,K,P,Q]`, use unflipped cross-correlation, and state the output-shape formula.
- Include asymmetric-filter, rectangular-input, edge-padding, stride/dilation, and multi-channel fixtures, plus predeclared accumulation, tolerance, and non-finite policy.
- Record future cuDNN component pin, graph validation/build, heuristic candidates, selected plan, workspace, correctness, and determinism separately. [L10](/en/libraries/cudnn-graphs-and-plans/) is the published continuation; this task still runs no comparison and adds no prerequisite.

**Expected evidence:** A tensor/operation contract, asymmetric-filter oracle, staged-patch ledger, ordered production-gate table, failure taxonomy, and blank measurement template.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- CPU, custom, and future-library paths share shape, layout, cross-correlation, stride, dilation, padding, data/compute types, and numerical acceptance.
- An asymmetric fixture distinguishes unflipped correlation from flipped mathematical convolution; every out-of-domain tap generates 0 without an out-of-bounds read.
- Graph validation, build/support, candidate filtering, plan selection, and workspace allocation/lifetime are independent gates; a failure is not disguised as performance.
- L10's independent version references do not establish a complete local component matrix. Without correctness-qualified retained measurements, there is no winner.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Use one asymmetric `2x2` filter to write separate correlation and flipped-convolution outputs.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Give separate verdicts to “API accepts graph,” “candidate exists,” “workspace is available,” “numerics pass,” and “timing result.”</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired packet first fixes dense NCHW/KCRS/NKPQ addresses, the unflipped cross-correlation equation, output extents, and a zero-padding accessor, then gives CPU, custom, and future cuDNN paths the same semantics. The fixture matrix covers asymmetry, rectangular extents, edges, stride, dilation, and channels; the floating-point comparator fixes accumulation, `atol/rtol`, and non-finite policy before output. L10 now supplies separately dated component and graph/plan reading; the actual production table still needs a complete local component matrix before graph validation/build, heuristics, selected plan, workspace, and repeated correctness/determinism. A07 executes no cuDNN, every plan/output/timing field is `unrecorded`, and neither path has a performance verdict. This continuation link does not refresh the entry's 2026-08-30 facts.

**Valid alternative:** A framework-managed cuDNN backend can be the production baseline if its component and algorithm selection can be recorded. An integer fixture may exact-compare but does not replace floating-point application acceptance.

**Common errors:** Saying only “convolution” without a flip convention; testing only a symmetric filter; omitting tensor strides or output formulas; implementing zero padding as an out-of-bounds read; treating a candidate as a plan; treating library reputation or shared reuse as observed speedup.

**Source basis:** The site's original [A07](/en/algorithms/convolution-reuse-layout/) and [SRC-CUDA-043](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-043), plus NVIDIA [cuDNN Convolutions](https://docs.nvidia.com/deeplearning/cudnn/latest/operations/Convolutions.html), [Developer Overview](https://docs.nvidia.com/deeplearning/cudnn/latest/developer/overview.html), and the [cuDNN Frontend v1.27.0 release](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/releases/tag/v1.27.0).

**Source date:** 2026-08-30.

<span id="pb-r2-020" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-020: Prove tiled-GEMM partial-tile and numerical contracts

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-020 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** GEMM correctness and debugging; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [A08: From Correct Naive GEMM to Shared-Memory Tiling](/en/algorithms/tiled-gemm-correctness/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review static GEMM, tile, barrier, tolerance, and evidence ledgers without running CUDA.
- **Version gate:** CUDA Programming Guide and Best Practices Guide v13.3 plus the 11.8.0/12.9.1 archives; EX15 C++17 lanes use Toolkit 11.8.0, 12.9.2, and 13.3.1.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [A02](/en/algorithms/multi-stage-reduction/), [A05](/en/algorithms/matrix-transpose-layout/), [M03](/en/memory/shared-memory-tiling/), [M04](/en/memory/bank-conflicts-layouts/), [A08](/en/algorithms/tiled-gemm-correctness/), [EX15](/en/examples/tiled-gemm/), and [VIS12](/en/visuals/gemm-tiling-hierarchy/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-31.

**Prompt:** A `M=18,K=19,N=17`, `16x16x16` tiled-GEMM proposal puts K slices in grid.z so multiple blocks write one C element. Edge threads return before a barrier, K tails are not zero-filled, correctness says only “looks close,” and shared reuse is treated as proof of speed. Repair the packet.

**Constraints:** Preserve row-major `C=alpha*A*B+beta*C`; let the output grid cover only M/N; have each block loop through two K slices; give each slice guarded zero-fill and two 256-participant barriers; declare a double reference, finite policy, `atol=1e-4`, and `rtol=2e-5`; keep evidence axes separate.

**Expected evidence:** A hand-computable oracle, `2x2` output-grid and K-slice ledger, bottom-right valid-output table, first-mismatch schema, portable-fallback statement, and blank performance record.
