**Acceptance criteria:** The bottom-right block has two output owners; the second K slice has three valid K positions; every shared slot receives a valid load or zero in every iteration; no thread returns before a barrier; host reference is not called Compile-Checked; no timing or speed winner appears.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>K is the reduction extent, not an output-ownership dimension.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Write M/N output validity and K-tail load validity as three predicates.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired grid is `ceil(17/16) x ceil(18/16)=2x2`, and every block handles K slices `[0,15]` and `[16,18]`. Bottom-right origin `(16,16)` has only rows 16/17 and column 16 as valid outputs, but all 256 threads execute `guarded load or zero -> barrier -> 16 products -> barrier` in every slice before two owners store. The independent double oracle rejects non-finite values before applying `atol+rtol*abs(reference)` and reports mismatch coordinates and errors. EX15's single FP32 path is the portable fallback with no architecture variant. Compilation, runtime, and performance remain unobserved.

**Valid alternative:** A different tile or tolerance is valid after restating resource, guard, numerical, and acceptance contracts before observation. An integer hand fixture may compare exactly.

**Common errors:** Making a K slice a second writer dimension; guarding only the final store; returning an edge thread before a barrier; stale shared K-tail data; widening tolerance after failure; treating source-level reuse as measured speedup.

**Source basis:** The site's original [A08](/en/algorithms/tiled-gemm-correctness/), [EX15](/en/examples/tiled-gemm/), [VIS12](/en/visuals/gemm-tiling-hierarchy/), and [SRC-CUDA-044](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-044), plus current and archived NVIDIA owner guides.

**Source date:** 2026-08-31.

<span id="pb-r2-021" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R2-021: Compose stable selection, compaction, and bounded-key sorting

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R2-021 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Concepts and implementation decision; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [A09: Compose Sorting, Selection, and Compaction from Scan, Histograms, and Movement](/en/algorithms/sorting-selection-compaction/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; derive flags, scans, histograms, ranks, scatter, and a production decision without running CUDA or CCCL.
- **Version gate:** CCCL v3.4.2 immutable release; CCCL 3.x covers latest-patch Toolkit 12.x/13.x and excludes 11.8; exact CUB `DeviceRadixSort`/`DeviceSelect` and Thrust `sort`/`copy` owner sources.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [A03](/en/algorithms/inclusive-exclusive-scan/), [A04](/en/algorithms/privatized-histogram/), and [A09](/en/algorithms/sorting-selection-compaction/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-31.

**Prompt:** A proposal atomically appends even values and calls the result stable compaction. For keys `[2,0,1,2,1,0]`, it computes only a histogram and starts before declaring stable sorting complete. It then ranks custom code above CUB and Thrust because custom code exposes more phases. Repair the composition and production decision.

**Constraints:** Use flags, exclusive positions, and scatter for selection; give sorting counts, bin starts, stable within-bin ranks, and destinations; separate atomic uniqueness from stability; put CCCL v3.4.2 only in 12.9.2/13.3.1 rows and use bundled libraries or a separately reviewed 2.x coordinate for 11.8; then compare CUB, Thrust, and custom semantics, storage, policy, maintenance, and measurement.

**Expected evidence:** A compaction table, six-item stable movement ledger, three-path decision matrix, blank measurement cells, and source/version pin.

**Acceptance criteria:** The even fixture preserves original order; sorting destinations are `[4,0,2,5,3,1]`; the rank missing from histogram plus starts is supplied; the atomic path no longer claims stability; no path becomes a performance winner before correctness-qualified measurement.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>A stable destination is the number of earlier selected or equal items plus the corresponding base.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Eliminate paths by semantics before comparing maintenance and measurement; do not rank abstraction levels.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** Exclusive-scanning even flags makes selected positions unique and increasing, so scatter produces stable compaction; atomic append remains unordered. The key fixture's counts and starts still need ranks to produce destinations `[4,0,2,5,3,1]`. The decision matrix limits CCCL v3.4.2 to 12.9.2/13.3.1; 11.8 uses bundled CUB/Thrust or a separately reviewed CCCL 2.x coordinate. Every timing cell remains `unrecorded`.

**Valid alternative:** Compact indices, comparison sorting, or a framework-managed primitive is valid when predicate or key, stability, payload, and component/backend contracts are explicit.

**Common errors:** Using inclusive scan directly as a zero-based destination; dropping the final flag; treating bin boundaries as per-item ranks; inferring order from atomic uniqueness; inferring speed from a temporary-storage query or library reputation.

**Source basis:** The site's original [A09](/en/algorithms/sorting-selection-compaction/) and [SRC-CUDA-045](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-045), plus CCCL v3.4.2 tagged `device_radix_sort.cuh`, `device_select.cuh`, and Thrust `sort.h` and `copy.h`.

**Source date:** 2026-08-31.

<span id="pb-r3-001" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R3-001: Repair an APOD record with no baseline or hypothesis

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R3-001 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [Q06: Use APOD as an Optimization Loop](/en/correctness/apod-optimization-loop/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; repair a static APOD evidence ledger without running or measuring CUDA.
- **Version gate:** CUDA C++ Best Practices Guide v13.3 APOD and the 11.8 archive.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [Q05](/en/correctness/timing-asynchronous-gpu-work/) and [Q06](/en/correctness/apod-optimization-loop/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-31.

**Prompt:** An optimization proposal has no correctness-qualified recorded baseline. It says only that one path “looks slow” and plans to change both buffer lifetime and compiler flags. It names no representative workload, metric endpoints, Environment Manifest, falsifiable hypothesis, or before/after comparability rule, yet intends to deploy if the next result is “better.” Repair it as one reviewable APOD pass.

**Goal:** Establish baseline qualification, one controlled change, an explicit falsifiable hypothesis, a comparable-evidence contract, and deployment/regression gates while leaving every observation and disposition field empty.

**Constraints:**

- Mark the baseline `baseline missing` until an actual correctness verdict, raw artifact, and complete manifest exist. Declare workload representativeness and exclusions, the metric, endpoints, and completion boundary.
- Select either the buffer-lifetime change or compiler-flag change, not both. The hypothesis names its mechanism, predicted direction, and falsifier; the other change moves to a future pass.
- List held-constant workload, input, correctness criteria, metric, measurement protocol, build policy, and environment coordinates. Fill in no timing, speedup, keep, deploy, or regression observation.

**Expected evidence:** A repaired APOD ledger containing a baseline-missing inventory, one hypothesis row, a confounder and held-constant table, before/after artifact schema, predeclared decision rule, and deploy gate.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- The proposal no longer calls an impression, screenshot, or unexecuted template a recorded baseline. A missing baseline gate stops the pass before Assess.
- The hypothesis isolates one independent variable and can be falsified or left inconclusive by correctness failure, absent mechanism evidence, an unmet comparison rule, or incomparable coordinates.
- The candidate must pass the same correctness contract again, and before/after records must share workload, metric endpoints, measurement policy, and comparable manifests.
- Disposition remains `pending observation`. Without actual comparable evidence and regression and operational records, no performance conclusion or Deploy entry is permitted.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Ask whether a traceable baseline really exists before asking what should change.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Put the two proposed edits in separate passes so only one mechanism carries the falsifiable explanation.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired record marks the available material `baseline missing` and leaves fields for workload rationale, correctness verdict, the Q05 protocol, manifest, and artifact pointers. The first pass retains only the buffer-lifetime change and holds compiler flags fixed. Its hypothesis template says that if the change removes the named repeated-movement mechanism while all other comparison coordinates remain fixed, the predeclared end-to-end metric should move in the named direction; correctness failure, an unchanged mechanism artifact, an unmet decision rule, or incomparable evidence rejects it or leaves it inconclusive. The before/after schema, source diff, commands, raw evidence, and deployment gates define fields without results. The only current verdict is “APOD record repaired; observation pending.”

**Valid alternative:** Isolate the compiler-flag change first, provided buffer lifetime and every other coordinate remain fixed and the mechanism and falsifier are rewritten for that change. Two changes cannot share one attribution.

**Common errors:** Calling an unqualified old run a baseline; inferring cause from a hotspot ranking; changing multiple variables; comparing performance before correctness; putting a prediction in an observation field; relabeling inconclusive as improvement; entering Deploy without a rollback owner.

**Source basis:** The site's original [Q06 APOD contract](/en/correctness/apod-optimization-loop/) and [Q05 measurement contract](/en/correctness/timing-asynchronous-gpu-work/), plus NVIDIA's [CUDA C++ Best Practices Guide v13.3 APOD](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-c-best-practices-guide/index.html#assess-parallelize-optimize-deploy), [Application Profiling](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-c-best-practices-guide/index.html#application-profiling), and [11.8 APOD archive](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/11.8.0/cuda-c-best-practices-guide/index.html#assess-parallelize-optimize-deploy).

**Source date:** 2026-08-31.

<span id="pb-r3-002" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R3-002: Choose timeline-first evidence from application symptoms

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R3-002 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [Q07: Read the Application Timeline First with Nsight Systems](/en/correctness/timeline-first-nsight-systems/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review a static symptom, timeline, and report-custody plan.
- **Version gate:** Nsight Systems 2026.4/2026.4.1 and selected Lane versions 2022.4.2.1, 2025.1.3.140, and 2026.1.3.425.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M07](/en/memory/stream-ordering/), [M09](/en/memory/pinned-memory-transfer-overlap/), [Q05](/en/correctness/timing-asynchronous-gpu-work/), [Q07](/en/correctness/timeline-first-nsight-systems/), [LAB06](/en/labs/build-overlapped-pipeline/), and [VIS14](/en/visuals/nsight-systems-versus-nsight-compute/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-31.

**Prompt:** An incident note says only that “batches pause between submissions and copies do not overlap kernels,” then labels a selected kernel the bottleneck and asks to collect every Nsight Compute metric immediately. The record has no representative workload, correctness verdict, `nsys --version`, collection method, timeline, profiler diagnostics, or `.nsys-rep` custody. Repair the symptom diagnosis as a timeline-first decision record.

**Goal:** Route the application-level symptom to Nsight Systems first, design a reviewable collection and custody plan, and permit kernel evidence only after an exact kernel instance and specific question exist.

**Constraints:**

- Before collection, fix the workload, input, representative-path rationale, Q05 correctness and completion contract, capture scope, trace domains, Environment Manifest, and observed CLI and help gates.
- Keep CPU gap, CUDA API duration, submission gap, copies, kernels, streams, dependencies, and overlap as separate fields awaiting observation. Blank space, multiple streams, and overlap eligibility are not diagnoses.
- Treat the original `.nsys-rep` as the primary artifact. Stats and exports retain the source hash, exact command, tool version, and derivative hash. Prefill no interval, cause, bottleneck, timing, or overlap result.

**Expected evidence:** A symptom-to-tool decision table, pre-observation collection contract, timeline interval and dependency ledger, report-custody inventory, and Systems-to-Compute handoff gate.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- The question still concerns application pauses, submission, copies, and dependencies, so Nsight Systems comes first. VIS14 helps choose an evidence source but supplies no observation.
- Selected Lane component numbers are planning coordinates only. Saved `nsys --version` and help output govern the actual command and interpretation.
- Future overlap wording is permitted only after named copy and kernel intervals intersect in the same retained report, and it does not automatically establish cause, generality, or performance effect.
- LAB06 inherits collection and correctness gates, not a prefilled result. Without a complete method, diagnostics, manifest, and report custody, there is no timeline conclusion or Q08 handoff.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Locate the symptom within an application interval first. Kernel metrics cannot reconstruct a timeline that does not exist.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Separate “what is visible,” “why it might happen,” and “which evidence comes next.” The latter two columns cannot impersonate the first.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired record preserves the incident note as an unverified symptom and selects Q07 and Nsight Systems as the first evidence source. Its collection template fixes a representative correct workload, capture and completion boundaries, requested trace domains, actual CLI/version/help, the CUPTI instrumentation boundary, diagnostics, and immutable report custody; every interval cell remains `pending observation`. The interpretation ledger separates host gap, API duration, submission, copy and kernel placement, stream and dependency, and overlap, rejecting causal inferences from blank space or eligibility. Only a future report that stably identifies process, phase, context or stream, kernel occurrence, and one kernel-level question may enter Q08. No Compute metric is currently requested.

**Valid alternative:** Review an existing retained Systems report without recollection if it satisfies the same representative-workload, correctness, version, method, and custody gates. A detached screenshot or summary table is not a substitute.

**Common errors:** Guessing `nsys` version from the Toolkit; sending an application symptom directly to Nsight Compute; naming blank space a CPU cause; treating separate lanes as concurrency; treating overlap eligibility as an observation; replacing `.nsys-rep` with a screenshot; choosing every metric before handoff.

**Source basis:** The site's original [Q07 timeline-first contract](/en/correctness/timeline-first-nsight-systems/), [LAB06 collection boundary](/en/labs/build-overlapped-pipeline/), and [VIS14 decision model](/en/visuals/nsight-systems-versus-nsight-compute/), plus NVIDIA's [Nsight Systems User Guide v2026.4](https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-systems/UserGuide/index.html), [2026.4.1 Release Notes](https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-systems/ReleaseNotes/index.html#nsight-systems-2026-4-1-highlights), and [CUPTI Usage](https://docs.nvidia.com/cupti/main/main.html).

**Source date:** 2026-08-31.

<span id="pb-r3-003" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R3-003: Design a minimal metric and replay plan for a selected kernel

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R3-003 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [Q08: Ask One Selected Kernel Question with Nsight Compute](/en/correctness/kernel-first-nsight-compute/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; review a static kernel, question, and metric plan; real collection requires authorized performance counters.
- **Version gate:** Nsight Compute 2026.2.1 and selected Lane versions 2022.3.0.22, 2025.2.1.3, and 2026.2.1.5.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M02](/en/memory/coalescing-transactions/), [M03](/en/memory/shared-memory-tiling/), [Q07](/en/correctness/timeline-first-nsight-systems/), [Q08](/en/correctness/kernel-first-nsight-compute/), [LAB08](/en/labs/profile-full-application-before-kernel/), and [VIS14](/en/visuals/nsight-systems-versus-nsight-compute/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-08-31.

**Prompt:** A Q07 handoff names only a repeated `normalize_rows` kernel family and application phase, with no exact occurrence or reproducible filter. The next plan requests every Nsight Compute section, records permission denial as zero, assumes all counters come from one untouched launch, and intends to call any “large” value the bottleneck and repair direction. Repair it as a minimal selected-kernel metric and replay plan.

**Goal:** Complete exact-kernel selection and one predeclared mechanism question before bounding collection and interpretation with target-specific availability, permission, minimal evidence, replay disclosure, and `.ncu-rep` custody.

**Constraints:**

- The selection ledger links the source `.nsys-rep` identity, process, phase, context or stream when available, full kernel name, matching occurrence, workload and input, and separate-run equivalence rule. Return to Q07 if mapping cannot be made stable.
- On the exact target, retain `ncu --version`, GPU, driver and Toolkit, `--list-sections`, `--query-metrics`, and the performance-counter permission result. A denial or unavailable item stops the plan and never becomes zero.
- Select one queried section or a short metric list that directly answers one M02 or M03 question. Retain each exact name, definition, unit, denominator, and scope. Replay mode and pass information and every value remain pending observation.

**Expected evidence:** A selected-instance identity card, question-to-evidence map, version, permission, and availability gate, symbolic minimal command, replay and perturbation disclosure, two-report custody ledger, and bounded-claim template.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Metric selection follows the exact occurrence, specific question, and decision rule. A name-only match or `--launch-count 1` cannot establish selection by itself.
- Every requested section or metric serves the same declared mechanism. The plan contains no all-sections dump and compares no values with different definitions or units.
- The `.nsys-rep` and future `.ncu-rep` are separate profiler executions. Replay may re-execute, perturb, or serialize work and is not described as attaching counters to the old Systems event.
- There is currently no availability, permission, pass count, metric value, bottleneck, duration, throughput, or speedup verdict. LAB08 collects only after the gates are actually complete.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Write which occurrence, which mechanism, and which decision the answer changes before opening the metric catalog.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Collecting less can narrow replay surface, but it cannot prove that replay or profiler perturbation is absent.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired plan first requires the `normalize_rows` source-report hash, process and phase, context or stream, named matching occurrence, and fixed workload, then labels the Compute target a corresponding occurrence. It predeclares only one M02 global-memory request-shape question. Until actual query output exists, section name, metric name, availability, and value remain blank. The gate order is exact tool and GPU coordinates, permission, query availability, filter match, one minimal section or short list, declared replay mode, and immutable export and custody. A denial, ambiguous match, or divergent workload stops the plan or returns it to Systems. A future report can answer only the narrow question; a causal performance conclusion still requires a separate correctness-qualified controlled comparison.

**Valid alternative:** Ask one M03 shared-memory staging or reuse question and select its minimum queried evidence instead. Do not merge global and shared mechanisms into one unbounded question.

**Common errors:** Dumping metrics before writing the question; selecting an instance by kernel name alone; guessing availability; recording permission denial as zero; hiding replay; mixing units or definitions; calling a “large” value the bottleneck; discarding original reports or omitting hashes, commands, and filters.

**Source basis:** The site's original [Q08 selected-kernel contract](/en/correctness/kernel-first-nsight-compute/), [LAB08 decision trail](/en/labs/profile-full-application-before-kernel/), and [VIS14 tool gate](/en/visuals/nsight-systems-versus-nsight-compute/), plus NVIDIA's [Nsight Compute Profiling Guide 2026.2.1](https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html), [Nsight Compute CLI 2026.2.1](https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/NsightComputeCli/index.html), [2025.2 CLI archive](https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/2025.2/NsightComputeCli/index.html), and [2022.3 CLI archive](https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/2022.3/NsightComputeCli/index.html).

**Source date:** 2026-08-31.

<span id="pb-r3-004" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R3-004: Audit an occupancy, stall, and throughput report claim

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R3-004 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [Q09: Interpret Latency Hiding with Occupancy, Stalls, and Throughput](/en/correctness/occupancy-stalls-throughput/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; audit a static report claim and its missing exact-GPU contract without running CUDA or a profiler.
- **Version gate:** CUDA Programming and Best Practices Guides v13.3 and Nsight Compute 2026.2.1; metric definition, unit, scope, and availability bind to the exact GPU and report.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [Q08](/en/correctness/kernel-first-nsight-compute/), [F08](/en/foundations/launch-geometry/), [Q09](/en/correctness/occupancy-stalls-throughput/), [LAB09](/en/labs/build-original-roofline/), and [VIS13](/en/visuals/roofline/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-01.

**Prompt:** A summary with no attached `.ncu-rep` says that occupancy is high, the largest stall category is large, and throughput remains below a ceiling, so the kernel is memory-bound and raising occupancy will recover the remaining speedup. It identifies neither theoretical nor achieved occupancy, metric names, units, denominators, sampling scope, exact GPU, tool version, filter, replay, workload, nor correctness. Audit and repair the claim.

**Goal:** Repair a causal conclusion assembled from three prominent labels as an exact-GPU, query-first, falsifiable evidence-review record that remains pending observation.

**Constraints:** Separate residency, eligible and issued scheduler state, stall sampling, and named-resource throughput. Inventory exact report coordinates and missing evidence. Do not invent metric spellings, values, a bottleneck, speedup, or an observation.

**Expected evidence:** A claim-audit table, missing-coordinate inventory, permitted narrow statements, at least two competing explanations, and a next-experiment contract that changes one primary mechanism.

**Acceptance criteria:** The memory-bound and occupancy-speedup conclusion is marked unsupported; theoretical and achieved occupancy are not interchanged; a stall category is not automatically a root cause; throughput gap is not relabeled recoverable speedup; every observation cell remains empty.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Ask whether each quantity describes residency, issue eligibility, or a resource rate before checking whether they share a workload and scope.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>“Largest category” and “distance below a ceiling” are rankings or ratios, not standalone causal explanations.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired record preserves the summary as an unverified claim. Its first column requires exact GPU and CC, binary, launch, workload, correctness, `ncu --version`, filter, replay, and report hash. Its second column separately requires theoretical-model inputs, achieved sampling and denominator, scheduler issue context, stall definition and normalization, and the named throughput ceiling. The only current statement is that the summary lacks the contract needed for interpretation. A next step may isolate one register or shared-memory limiter or one access-layout mechanism, hold other coordinates fixed, and predeclare support and rejection rules. Without qualifying before/after evidence, the verdict remains `pending observation`.

**Valid alternative:** Audit only the occupancy numerator and denominator first, or only whether the scheduler lacks eligible warps, provided the local audit is not expanded into a whole-kernel cause.

**Common errors:** Treating theoretical as achieved occupancy; treating active as eligible; declaring a bottleneck from the largest stall share; comparing same-named metrics across GPUs; recording permission denial as zero; calling throughput headroom speedup; replacing report custody with a screenshot.

**Source basis:** The site's original [Q09 interpretation contract](/en/correctness/occupancy-stalls-throughput/), [Q08 query and report contract](/en/correctness/kernel-first-nsight-compute/), and [LAB09 evidence boundary](/en/labs/build-original-roofline/), plus NVIDIA's [CUDA Best Practices occupancy](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-c-best-practices-guide/index.html#occupancy), [CUDA Programming Guide](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/index.html), and [Nsight Compute Profiling Guide 2026.2.1](https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html).

**Source date:** 2026-09-01.

<span id="pb-r3-005" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R3-005: Analyze a synthetic Nsight Roofline report with a unit, boundary, or provenance mismatch

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R3-005 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [Q10: Build an Auditable Roofline from Arithmetic Intensity](/en/correctness/roofline-arithmetic-intensity/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; analyze an original synthetic Nsight report packet and audit its static model contract without running CUDA or a profiler.
- **Version gate:** CUDA Best Practices Guide v13.3, Nsight Compute 2026.2.1, and the original 2009 Roofline model; current tool semantics are not projected backward from the paper.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [Q05](/en/correctness/timing-asynchronous-gpu-work/), [A14](/en/algorithms/algorithm-choice-arithmetic-intensity/), [Q09](/en/correctness/occupancy-stalls-throughput/), [Q10](/en/correctness/roofline-arithmetic-intensity/), [LAB09](/en/labs/build-original-roofline/), and [VIS13](/en/visuals/roofline/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-01.

**Prompt:** An original synthetic Nsight Compute Roofline report-analysis packet, explicitly not a captured `.ncu-rep`, supplies `W=6.0e9 FLOP`, `T=3.0e9 byte`, `P_compute=12.0e12 FLOP/s`, `B=2.0e12 byte/s`, and `P_workload=5.0e12 FLOP/s`. Its field provenance says that `T` is an L2 traffic estimate from report A, `B` is a measured DRAM ceiling from report B, the compute ceiling comes from a specification, and the workload rate comes from report C under another scope. Complete the formal arithmetic, then audit why this cross-report point and roof cannot be interpreted.

**Goal:** Analyze the report fields and their provenance, show that dimensionally closed arithmetic is insufficient for a valid Roofline, then turn memory-domain, workload-identity, and ceiling-provenance mismatches into an executable rebuild plan.

**Constraints:** Calculate intensity, ridge, and roof. Never relabel the synthetic values as GPU observations. Propose separate theoretical, calibrated or measured, and tool-reported rebuilds without silent mixing.

**Expected evidence:** A report-field-to-source matrix, unit-bearing calculation table, above-roof mismatch flag, domain and provenance audit, three rebuild packets, and an explicit no-interpretation verdict.

**Acceptance criteria:** The result includes `I=2.0 FLOP/byte`, `I_ridge=6.0 FLOP/byte`, and formal roof `4.0e12 FLOP/s`. The `5.0e12` point is above the declared roof. L2/DRAM, scope, and provenance mismatches prevent any bottleneck or speedup conclusion.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Close the dimensions first, then ask whether the denominator bytes belong to the same path as the bandwidth ceiling.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>An above-roof point is not an observation of exceptional speed. It first signals an audit of units, scope, run identity, and provenance.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The report matrix first maps the five fields to report A, report B, the specification, and report C, proving that they share no run identity. The formal arithmetic is `I=6.0e9/3.0e9=2.0 FLOP/byte`, `I_ridge=12.0e12/2.0e12=6.0 FLOP/byte`, and a bandwidth-side roof of `2.0*2.0e12=4.0e12 FLOP/s`, so the supplied point exceeds that roof. The verdict is `reject interpretation`: x uses an L2 estimate, the sloped line uses measured DRAM bandwidth, the horizontal line comes from a specification, and y does not share the scope. A theoretical rebuild aligns specification ceilings with theoretical traffic; a calibrated rebuild measures compatible compute and path ceilings under one manifest and method; a tool-reported rebuild uses documented fields from one exact report. Each rebuild recomputes the point instead of moving the roof to fit it.

**Valid alternative:** Rebuild only a DRAM roof or only an L2 roof. Three provenance families may also be displayed side by side if each line and point is labeled independently and no unaligned denominator is shared.

**Common errors:** Declaring a model valid because units close; pairing L2 bytes with DRAM bandwidth; confusing GB and GiB; joining x and y from different runs; moving a ceiling to contain a point; calling a modeled region an observed bottleneck; presenting synthetic values as profiler output.

**Source basis:** The site's original [Q10 Roofline contract](/en/correctness/roofline-arithmetic-intensity/), [A14 accounting contract](/en/algorithms/algorithm-choice-arithmetic-intensity/), and [VIS13 mismatch state](/en/visuals/roofline/), plus NVIDIA's [Best Practices bandwidth](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-c-best-practices-guide/index.html#bandwidth), [Nsight Compute Roofline Charts](https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#roofline-charts), and the [original Roofline paper](https://doi.org/10.1145/1498765.1498785).

**Source date:** 2026-09-01.

<span id="pb-r3-006" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R3-006: Compare traffic estimates and write a falsifiable optimization choice

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R3-006 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Concepts and implementation; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [A14: Choose Falsifiable Algorithm Optimizations with Arithmetic Intensity](/en/algorithms/algorithm-choice-arithmetic-intensity/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; compare static operation and traffic ledgers and design a falsifiable experiment without running CUDA.
- **Version gate:** CUDA Programming and Best Practices Guides v13.3 and the original 2009 Roofline model; every traffic value is an explicitly labeled estimate.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [A01](/en/algorithms/elementwise-map/), [A02](/en/algorithms/multi-stage-reduction/), [A05](/en/algorithms/matrix-transpose-layout/), [A08](/en/algorithms/tiled-gemm-correctness/), [A14](/en/algorithms/algorithm-choice-arithmetic-intensity/), [Q10](/en/correctness/roofline-arithmetic-intensity/), [EX02](/en/examples/vector-addition/), [EX11](/en/examples/multi-stage-reduction/), [EX14](/en/examples/tiled-transpose/), [EX15](/en/examples/tiled-gemm/), and [VIS13](/en/visuals/roofline/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-01.

**Prompt:** For the synthetic elementwise pipeline `tmp=a+b; z=g(tmp)`, count `g` as 5 FLOP per element. The materialized estimate is `6N FLOP` and `20N byte`; the fused estimate is `6N FLOP` and `12N byte`. Compare traffic and intensity, then write one first optimization choice that correctness, traffic, or timing evidence could falsify.

**Goal:** Turn two boundary-explicit traffic estimates into a narrow, attributable, rejectable experiment rather than declaring a winner from an arithmetic-intensity ranking.

**Constraints:** Hold workload, precision, operation convention, and output contract fixed. List register pressure, occupancy, cache behavior, and launch overhead as competing explanations. Actual traffic and timing slots remain empty.

**Expected evidence:** A baseline and candidate ledger, `0.3` and `0.5 FLOP/byte` calculations, predicted traffic ratio, correctness gate, one primary mechanism, support and rejection rules, and rollback condition.

**Acceptance criteria:** The fused candidate only removes the materialized intermediate's write and read. Mechanism and performance claims each remain rejectable. Higher modeled intensity is not called observed speedup. Failure and inconclusive results are valid outcomes.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>The unfused intermediate adds one 4N-byte write and one 4N-byte read.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Write a rejection rule for whether traffic falls before writing a separate rule for whether elapsed time falls.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** Both versions perform `6N FLOP`. Materialized intensity is `6N/20N=0.3 FLOP/byte`, fused intensity is `6N/12N=0.5 FLOP/byte`, and predicted logical-traffic ratio is `12/20=0.6`. The first experiment enables fusion only while fixing source inputs, build, GPU and software, warm-up, correctness, and timing and traffic definitions. If traffic at the exact declared boundary does not fall, reject the removed-intermediate mechanism. Even if traffic falls, reject the performance claim if correct-workload time does not, then inspect register pressure, occupancy, cache behavior, and launch overhead. Current disposition is `candidate selected; observation pending`.

**Valid alternative:** Select reduction partial fusion or tiled transpose first if it has corresponding before/after traffic formulas, a same-workload correctness contract, and one primary mechanism. The largest estimated reduction need not come first.

**Common errors:** Calling an estimate measured traffic; comparing intensity alone; changing precision, layout, and algorithm together; omitting the intermediate read; ignoring resource cost; treating a modeled Roofline region as the winner; filling speedup before observation.

**Source basis:** The site's original [A14 traffic ledger](/en/algorithms/algorithm-choice-arithmetic-intensity/), [Q10 model boundary](/en/correctness/roofline-arithmetic-intensity/), and EX02, EX11, EX14, and EX15 correctness contracts, plus NVIDIA's [CUDA Programming Guide v13.3](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/index.html), [Best Practices bandwidth](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-c-best-practices-guide/index.html#bandwidth), and the [original Roofline paper](https://doi.org/10.1145/1498765.1498785).

**Source date:** 2026-09-01.

<span id="pb-r3-007" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R3-007: Analyze a four-variable transpose report and repair it as staged comparisons

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R3-007 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [Q11: Optimize the Canonical Transpose with Controlled Evidence](/en/correctness/transpose-optimization-case-study/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; analyze a synthetic Nsight report packet and repair a static transpose comparison design without running CUDA or a profiler.
- **Version gate:** CUDA Programming Guide v13.3 and the 12.9.1/11.8.0 archives; Nsight Compute 2026.2.1 and selected Lane versions 2022.3.0.22/2025.2.1.3/2026.2.1.5; Q11/EX14/VIS11 facts; no measured claim.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [A05](/en/algorithms/matrix-transpose-layout/), [Q06](/en/correctness/apod-optimization-loop/), [Q08](/en/correctness/kernel-first-nsight-compute/), [Q10](/en/correctness/roofline-arithmetic-intensity/), [EX14](/en/examples/tiled-transpose/), [VIS11](/en/visuals/tiled-transpose/), and [LAB10](/en/labs/optimize-canonical-transpose/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-02.

**Prompt:** An original synthetic Nsight Compute report-analysis packet, explicitly not a captured `.ncu-rep`, has only `B0` and `candidate` rows. It reduces unnamed duration, throughput, and bank-conflict fields to “candidate is faster” but has no report hash, exact GPU or tool, metric name or unit, filter, replay, permission, raw attempts, or Environment Manifest. Its linked transpose proposal also changes lane-to-global-address direction, logical tile extent, thread-to-slot ownership, and physical shared-row padding together. Reject the report's attribution first, then repair the proposal as a staged comparison plan.

**Goal:** Audit the report packet's identity, metric, and custody gaps, then split the four entangled knobs into reviewable adjacent stages so each comparison has one primary variable and permits support, rejection, inconclusive, and rollback outcomes.

**Constraints:**

- Qualify `B0` first with EX14's output shape, complete row-major mapping, and exact `5x7`, `33x35`, and `64x32` fixtures. Declare a separate fixed performance workload, but fill no observation.
- Build a `B0 -> C1 -> E2 -> O3 -> P4` ledger that changes only global coalescing direction, logical tile extent, thread ownership, and physical stride `T -> T+1`, respectively. In `E2`, hold launch policy and every independent launch knob constant, and deterministically recompute and record the grid dimensions needed to cover the same fixed workload. Record those grid dimensions, edge fraction, and shared-resource footprint as derived consequences and confounders of tile extent, not separately tuned variables.
- At every stage, hold source parent, value type, workload, oracle, compiler and flags, selected GPU and tool coordinates, launch policy, every independent launch knob, warm-up, completion, sample policy, metric query, filter, replay, and report custody fixed. Stop when a prior stage is unqualified; it cannot silently become the next baseline.

**Expected evidence:** A report-field and custody rejection table, five-node variant graph, four adjacent one-variable diff ledgers, per-stage held-constant, derived-consequence, and confounder tables, an unfilled correctness and measurement artifact schema, and predeclared support, reject, inconclusive, and rollback rules.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- The synthetic report packet and original four-variable comparison are both marked confounded and grant no attribution.
- `C1` changes only the named global address direction, `E2` only logical tile extent, `O3` only thread-to-slot ownership, and `P4` only physical shared-row stride.
- Every candidate must pass the same correctness contract again. Apart from the named primary variable and its declared derived consequences, any additional source, workload, tool, or collection difference, or any unexplained or independently tuned launch-policy or launch-knob difference, makes that pair unattributable. The deterministic grid-dimension recomputation required for `E2` to cover the fixed workload is a necessary derived change, not grounds for rejection.
- Tile extent, ownership, and padding retain separate hypotheses and competing explanations. A later stage cannot retroactively fill evidence missing from an earlier stage.
- Every result, metric, timing, tile-winner, and speedup cell remains `pending observation`; failure and inconclusive are valid outcomes.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Draw the variant graph first, then check whether each edge's source diff has exactly one named primary variable.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Passing correctness only admits a stage to evidence collection; it does not make that stage an optimization winner.</details>

**Report audit:** Every unnamed field is `uninterpretable`. The “faster” conclusion must be rejected when report identity, exact environment, query, replay, permission, samples, and custody are missing.

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired ledger first labels the original proposal `confounded comparison; no attributable result`. `B0` exists only after the EX14 oracle, three fixtures, and fixed-workload contract are complete. `C1` changes lane and global direction alone. `E2` changes tile extent alone while keeping launch policy and every independent launch knob fixed; it deterministically recomputes and records the grid dimensions needed to cover the same fixed workload, then lists grid dimensions, edge fraction, and shared-resource footprint as extent-derived consequences and confounders. `O3` changes slot ownership alone under `E2`'s extent, derived grid, and the same launch-policy and independent-knob contract. `P4` finally changes only physical stride from `T` to `T+1`. Every edge preserves source and binary identity, held constants, the derivation of launch and resource consequences, correctness schema, query, replay, and report schema, and an independent decision rule; all observation fields remain empty. The only current verdict is “the design is isolated; no measured winner exists.”

**Valid alternative:** Branch four candidates from one correctness-qualified shared-memory baseline instead of inheriting stages sequentially, provided every branch still changes one variable and any dependency that cannot be isolated returns to a new experiment.

**Common errors:** Splitting one four-variable diff into four prose sections while running only one candidate; independently tuning ownership, padding, or another launch knob with tile extent; treating the grid recomputation needed for full coverage as a second tuned variable, or retaining a stale grid that no longer covers the fixed workload; letting a failed stage become the next baseline; inferring an observed conflict from VIS11 bank arithmetic; using a profiler label to replace a source diff; naming a winner before comparison.

**Source basis:** The site's original [Q11 controlled-comparison contract](/en/correctness/transpose-optimization-case-study/), [A05 transpose model](/en/algorithms/matrix-transpose-layout/), [EX14 correctness contract](/en/examples/tiled-transpose/), [VIS11 evidence-neutral arithmetic](/en/visuals/tiled-transpose/), and [SRC-CUDA-053](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-053).

**Source date:** 2026-09-02.

<span id="pb-r3-008" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R3-008: Audit fixed-tile and speedup claims in an uncustodied profiler summary

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R3-008 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [Q11: Optimize the Canonical Transpose with Controlled Evidence](/en/correctness/transpose-optimization-case-study/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; audit a static profiler summary and missing evidence custody without running CUDA, Nsight Compute, or LAB10.
- **Version gate:** Toolkit 11.8.0/12.9.2/13.3.1; Nsight Compute 2022.3.0.22/2025.2.1.3/2026.2.1.5; CUPTI 11.8.87/12.9.79/13.3.75; exact GPU, tool, metric, replay, report, and manifest gates.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [A05](/en/algorithms/matrix-transpose-layout/), [Q06](/en/correctness/apod-optimization-loop/), [Q08](/en/correctness/kernel-first-nsight-compute/), [Q10](/en/correctness/roofline-arithmetic-intensity/), [EX14](/en/examples/tiled-transpose/), [VIS11](/en/visuals/tiled-transpose/), and [LAB10](/en/labs/optimize-canonical-transpose/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-02.

**Prompt:** A profiler summary detached from its original artifacts claims that one fixed tile extent is universally optimal and that the candidate has a speedup over the baseline. It has no exact GPU, driver or Toolkit, Nsight Compute or CUPTI version, metric query, permission, kernel filter, replay record, correctness record, raw timing, `.ncu-rep`, hash, Environment Manifest, or custody history. Audit and repair the claim.

**Goal:** Downgrade the fixed-tile and speedup conclusions to unsupported claims, inventory the exact environment, collection, measurement, and custody packet needed for a reviewable comparison, and retain competing explanations.

**Constraints:**

- The inventory includes exact GPU identity, device ordinal, compute capability, driver, Toolkit Lane, `ncu --version`, Nsight Compute and CUPTI components, source and binary hashes, launch, workload, EX14 correctness, and permission result. A nearby version or another GPU cannot substitute silently.
- For each profiler item, require the exact queried section or metric name, definition, unit, scope, availability, kernel, filter and launch selection, replay mode and pass count, save and restore behavior, cache and clock controls, command, stdout and stderr, `.ncu-rep` path and hash, and observer and transfer history.
- A speedup needs independent matched unprofiled timing records with fixed warm-up exclusion, explicit synchronization, attempt count, raw-sample retention, statistic, and run order. Do not supply a tile value, metric value, elapsed time, ratio, or winner.
- Retain at least two competing explanations among resource footprint and occupancy, edge fraction, cache state, instruction mapping, replay perturbation, clock and thermal state, competing load, and correctness or workload drift.

**Expected evidence:** A claim-to-missing-evidence matrix, exact GPU, tool, metric, replay, report, and manifest checklist, timing-versus-profiler boundary, artifact-custody ledger, competing-explanation table, and bounded replacement statement.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- The fixed tile is only a claimed configuration in an uncustodied summary and cannot be projected to another workload, GPU, Toolkit Lane, or launch contract.
- The speedup claim is unsupported. Even a profiler summary containing a metric cannot replace matched correctness-qualified raw timing.
- Permission denial, an unavailable metric, ambiguous filtering, unmatched replay, a missing report or hash, or an incomplete manifest yields a blocker or no answer, not zero or a best-effort winner.
- The repaired record publishes no observed metric, timing, tile winner, bottleneck, causal attribution, or speedup.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Ask first where the original `.ncu-rep`, expanded command, query output, and hash are. A label in a screenshot cannot establish artifact identity.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Split “is the candidate faster?” from “why might it be faster?” into independent timing and mechanism evidence chains.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The audit marks both conclusions `unsupported from uncustodied summary`. A recovery packet first binds source and binary, exact workload, EX14 verdict, GPU, driver, Toolkit, matching Nsight Compute and CUPTI, approved non-admin permission, and a complete manifest. It then retains exact queries, metric definitions, filtering, replay, pass and save/restore behavior, commands and logs, the original `.ncu-rep`, and SHA-256 custody. Speedup separately requires unprofiled baseline and candidate raw attempts on the same workload, excluded warm-up, explicit completion, a predeclared statistic, and complete sample retention. Resource footprint, edge behavior, cache, instruction mapping, replay, and system state remain competing explanations. Because those artifacts are absent, no tile winner, metric direction, or speedup can be named.

**Valid alternative:** If the private original report, commands, queries, manifest, and custody can be recovered, review them and create a sanitized derivative with a hash mapping. Otherwise begin a new comparison under the LAB10 contract; do not attach new records retroactively to the old summary.

**Common errors:** Guessing GPU or tool version from a filename; guessing a metric from a section label; recording permission denial as zero; hiding replay; substituting profiled duration for matched timing; retaining only a ratio and not samples; universalizing one workload's tile claim; replacing report hashes and a manifest with a screenshot.

**Source basis:** The site's original [Q11 profiler and custody boundary](/en/correctness/transpose-optimization-case-study/), [Q08 exact-GPU query contract](/en/correctness/kernel-first-nsight-compute/), [Q10 model boundary](/en/correctness/roofline-arithmetic-intensity/), [LAB10 acquisition contract](/en/labs/optimize-canonical-transpose/), and [SRC-CUDA-054](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-054).

**Source date:** 2026-09-02.

<span id="pb-r3-009" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R3-009: Analyze a confounded reduction report and rewrite it as a controlled stage graph

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R3-009 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [Q12: Optimize the Canonical Reduction with Controlled Evidence](/en/correctness/reduction-optimization-case-study/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; analyze a synthetic Nsight report packet and repair static reduction source, operation-tree, and evidence plans without running CUDA or a profiler.
- **Version gate:** CUDA Programming, Floating Point, and Best Practices Guides v13.3 plus Nsight Compute 2026.2.1; no measured claim.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [A02](/en/algorithms/multi-stage-reduction/), [Q02](/en/correctness/floating-point-order-reproducibility/), [Q06](/en/correctness/apod-optimization-loop/), [Q08](/en/correctness/kernel-first-nsight-compute/), [Q12](/en/correctness/reduction-optimization-case-study/), [EX11](/en/examples/multi-stage-reduction/), and [VIS10](/en/visuals/reduction-stages/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-02.

**Prompt:** An original synthetic Nsight Compute report-analysis packet, explicitly not a captured `.ncu-rep`, retains only a final scalar and the sentence “the profiler shows fewer stalls.” It has no exact report identity, metric name, unit, or scope, GPU or tool, filter, replay, permission, raw attempts, correctness record, or Environment Manifest. Its linked reduction candidate also changes the final warp to shuffles, deletes block barriers, replaces the down tree with an XOR tree, and changes inputs per thread from two to four. Audit and reject the report claim first, then rewrite the candidate as a rejectable stage graph.

**Goal:** Separate the report-field, correctness, and custody gaps, then build a four-node graph from an immutable EX11-shaped baseline so the control and synchronization bundle, numerical order, and partial staging each own a correctness gate, hypothesis, coupling disclosure, and decision rule.

**Constraints:**

- Every node retains the EX11 double CPU reference, finite absolute-plus-relative comparator, neutral zero, host-driven stage boundaries, and `1/3/511/512/513/4099` edge fixtures.
- Fix `canonical-shared-tree -> warp-tail-control -> reassociated-warp-order -> four-load-staging`. The first edge discloses branch, barrier, shared, and instruction coupling; the second supplies an operand-edge diff; the third supplies stage-size and logical partial-traffic ledgers.
- Every measured stage predeclares one workload, warm-up, checked completion, raw attempts, median and min/max, exact-GPU profiler query, permission, Environment Manifest, correctness result, and bounded interpretation. Result cells stay empty.

**Expected evidence:** A report-field and custody rejection table, four-node graph, three adjacent diffs, operand and stage-size ledgers, a per-stage measurement schema, support, reject, no-answer, and rollback rules, and a competing-explanation matrix.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- The synthetic report packet and original four-variable comparison are both marked confounded and grant no attribution.
- Each edge independently passes the same CPU-reference and tolerance gate, and disclosure does not disguise unavoidable coupling as a held constant.
- Source-derived fewer partials are not called measured traffic, tolerance acceptance is not called bitwise reproducibility, and no stage has a prefilled winner.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Compare source and operand graphs before deciding whether a change is the primary intervention or disclosed coupling.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>If one edge cannot isolate divergence from synchronization, name it as a bundle and narrow the claim.</details>

**Report audit:** `stalls` has no metric identity, unit, denominator, scope, or availability, and the final scalar has no correctness custody. Neither supports optimization attribution.

**Reviewed solution:** The repaired plan rejects the original comparison. `canonical-shared-tree` freezes an EX11-shaped two-load tree. `warp-tail-control` is only a control and synchronization bundle, with branch encoding, barrier count, shared accesses, and instruction mix listed as coupling. `reassociated-warp-order` changes the down versus XOR operand and active-lane schedule while retaining five shuffles and 31 effective additions. `four-load-staging` changes to four inputs while disclosing local-addition order and separately records `16777219 -> 32769 -> 65 -> 1` and `16777219 -> 16385 -> 17 -> 1`. Every edge owns independent correctness, query, measurement, manifest, and rollback schemas. Current disposition is `design isolated; observations pending`.

**Valid alternative:** Branch all three candidates from one canonical baseline if every branch has an independent source diff and evidence record that borrows no result from another branch.

**Common errors:** Splitting one confounded candidate into three prose sections; hiding coupled instruction or order changes; using VIS10 instead of a warp-participant proof; retaining only the final scalar; inferring an elapsed winner directly from fewer partials; promoting a failed stage to the next baseline.

**Source basis:** The site's original [Q12 controlled reduction contract](/en/correctness/reduction-optimization-case-study/), [A02 stage model](/en/algorithms/multi-stage-reduction/), [Q02 numerical contract](/en/correctness/floating-point-order-reproducibility/), [EX11 correctness contract](/en/examples/multi-stage-reduction/), [VIS10 evidence-neutral tree](/en/visuals/reduction-stages/), and [SRC-CUDA-055](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-055).

**Source date:** 2026-09-02.

<span id="pb-r3-010" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R3-010: Audit an uncustodied reduction profiler and bitwise claim

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R3-010 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [Q12: Optimize the Canonical Reduction with Controlled Evidence](/en/correctness/reduction-optimization-case-study/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; audit a static fixture and claim without running CUDA, Nsight Compute, or LAB11.
- **Version gate:** Toolkit 11.8.0/12.9.2/13.3.1 and Nsight Compute 2022.3.0.22/2025.2.1.3/2026.2.1.5; exact GPU, tool, report, and manifest gates.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [A02](/en/algorithms/multi-stage-reduction/), [Q02](/en/correctness/floating-point-order-reproducibility/), [Q06](/en/correctness/apod-optimization-loop/), [Q08](/en/correctness/kernel-first-nsight-compute/), [Q12](/en/correctness/reduction-optimization-case-study/), [EX11](/en/examples/multi-stage-reduction/), and [VIS10](/en/visuals/reduction-stages/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-02.

**Prompt:** A summary detached from original artifacts says a four-load reduction is “bitwise equal to the CPU, eliminates divergence, reduces DRAM traffic, and is always fastest.” It has only a screenshot, with no EX11 revision, source or binary hashes, raw attempts, metric query, permission, filter, replay, `.ncu-rep`, Environment Manifest, or comparator record. It ends with one unversioned CUB-like API name. Audit the summary.

**Goal:** Downgrade unsupported universal claims to unanswered questions and establish four independent evidence chains for numerical acceptance, unprofiled elapsed evidence, profiler mechanism evidence, and a future production comparison.

**Constraints:**

- The numerical chain requires exact input bytes, CPU reference, actual, absolute and allowed error, compiler and launch, and an operation-tree ledger. A tolerance pass does not become bitwise or determinism evidence.
- The elapsed chain requires one workload, three excluded warm-ups, checked completion, ten raw attempts, a predeclared median and min/max, and no deletion. The profiler chain separately requires exact GPU and tool, availability query, permission, filtering, replay, report and hash, and manifest.
- The production chain treats published [L03](/en/libraries/cub-device-primitives/) and [LAB11](/en/labs/compare-custom-reduction-with-cub/) as separate follow-ons. A route, name, or static API contract cannot establish build support, runtime behavior, a determinism observation, or a result.

**Expected evidence:** A claim-to-evidence matrix, four independent custody chains, complete but unfilled per-stage records, blocker and no-answer rules, competing explanations, and a bounded replacement statement.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Reject the bitwise, divergence, traffic, and fastest claims as unsupported by the supplied evidence.
- Permission denial, an unavailable metric, missing report or hash, incomplete manifest, or failed correctness produces a blocker rather than zero.
- The repaired record fills no timing, metric value, speedup, winner, CUB call, or runtime result.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Ask first for the primary `.ncu-rep` and exact command behind the screenshot. Metric identity cannot be recovered without them.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Separate “within tolerance,” “same bits,” “why a mechanism changed,” and “whether elapsed work changed.”</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The audit labels all four conclusions `unsupported from uncustodied summary`. A numerical record needs the EX11 reference and comparator plus an operand tree. An elapsed record needs matched unprofiled attempts. A mechanism record needs exact queries, permission, filtering and replay, and a hashed report. Production comparison uses the separate LAB11 contract and cannot backfill the old summary. No qualifying artifact exists, so no metric, bits, traffic, winner, or CUB verdict can be supplied. An acceptable replacement is: “This source proposes four-load staging and a changed operation tree; correctness and performance observations remain pending.”

**Valid alternative:** If the private original reports and commands can be recovered, verify custody and create a sanitized derivative. Otherwise begin a new comparison; never attach new evidence retroactively to the old screenshot.

**Common errors:** Guessing GPU or version from a filename; encoding denied permission as zero; substituting profiled duration for unprofiled attempts; claiming bitwise identity from tolerance acceptance; calling a logical partial estimate a DRAM observation; inventing a CUB contract from an API-like name.

**Source basis:** The site's original [Q12 evidence boundary](/en/correctness/reduction-optimization-case-study/), [Q02 numerical contract](/en/correctness/floating-point-order-reproducibility/), [Q08 query and report custody](/en/correctness/kernel-first-nsight-compute/), [EX11](/en/examples/multi-stage-reduction/), and [SRC-CUDA-055](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-055).

**Source date:** 2026-09-02.

<span id="pb-r3-011" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R3-011: Analyze a memorized-tile GEMM report and derive a stage graph

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R3-011 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [Q13: Optimize the Canonical GEMM with Controlled Evidence](/en/correctness/gemm-optimization-case-study/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; analyze a synthetic Nsight report packet, derive static ledgers, and design a falsifiable comparison without running CUDA or a profiler.
- **Version gate:** CUDA Programming and Best Practices Guides v13.3, Toolkits 11.8.0/12.9.2/13.3.1, and Nsight Compute 2026.2.1; no measured claim.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [A08](/en/algorithms/tiled-gemm-correctness/), [Q06](/en/correctness/apod-optimization-loop/), [Q08](/en/correctness/kernel-first-nsight-compute/), [Q10](/en/correctness/roofline-arithmetic-intensity/), [Q13](/en/correctness/gemm-optimization-case-study/), [EX15](/en/examples/tiled-gemm/), and [VIS12](/en/visuals/gemm-tiling-hierarchy/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-03.

**Prompt:** An original synthetic Nsight Compute report-analysis packet, explicitly not a captured `.ncu-rep`, asks to “use the common 32-by-32 tile from the web” and calls it best from an unnamed occupancy screenshot and one timing ratio. It lacks the exact GPU, tool, and report hash, metric definition, compiler resources, permission, replay, raw attempts, correctness result, and Environment Manifest. Reject that report claim first. Then, for `A[1024,1024] * B[1024,1024] -> C[1024,1024]`, use Q13's four stages to derive full-K-slice A/B loads, reuse per value, outputs, source shared bytes, source accumulators, K slices, and barriers, and design three rejectable adjacent hypotheses.

**Goal:** Audit the report, metric, compiler, and custody gaps, then produce a tile, reuse, and resource ledger from the immutable EX15 correctness baseline so workload, ownership, and mechanism rather than a memorized size drive every candidate.

**Constraints:**

- Retain the EX15 row-major equation, three edge fixtures with each fixture's published alpha/beta, FP32 device accumulation, double CPU reference, finite rejection, and absolute-plus-relative tolerance. The measurement shape alone uses `alpha=0.75` and `beta=0.25`; restore each case's initial C before every attempt.
- For `canonical-16x16x16`, `k-tile-16x16x8`, `rectangular-32x8x8`, and `coarsened-32x16x8`, label `TM/TN/TK`, block, outputs per thread, edge guards, and cooperative-load ownership. Source arithmetic cannot masquerade as compiled registers, occupancy, or observed traffic.
- Predeclare support, rejection, no answer, rollback, normalization, and at least two competing explanations for each edge. Name no winner.

**Expected evidence:** A report-field and custody rejection table, four-row tile, reuse, and resource table, three adjacent hypothesis ledgers, a correctness checklist, source-versus-compiler-versus-profiler boundary, and a reject/no-answer matrix.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Reject the synthetic report's occupancy and timing claims for missing identity, definitions, samples, correctness, and custody.
- Reject the 32-by-32 suggestion for missing a labeled K depth, block legality, workload, resource, and evidence contract rather than replacing it with another memorized number.
- Separate the direct effects and coupling of `TK`, rectangular shape, and two-output ownership. Matched evidence can falsify every hypothesis.
- Publish no metric value, timing, speedup, occupancy target, universal tile, or production claim.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Full-slice inputs are `TM*TK + TK*TN`, while outputs are `TM*TN`. Compare that ratio before discussing cache or DRAM.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Source shared bytes are directly calculable. Register allocation waits for exact compiler output, and resident blocks additionally require exact device limits.</details>

**Report audit:** The unnamed occupancy screenshot has no metric denominator or exact-device limits, and the lone ratio has no raw attempts, matched timing method, or correctness gate. Neither can establish a tile winner.

**Reviewed solution:** The ledger records 512 loads/256 outputs, 2,048 shared bytes, and one accumulator for canonical; 256/256, 1,024 bytes, and one accumulator for K-tile, with twice the K slices and barriers and with cooperative-load ownership and active instruction groups changing from all 256 threads to the participating half-block per shared array; 320/256, 1,280 bytes, one accumulator, and A/B reuse of 8/32 for rectangular; and 384/512, 1,536 bytes, two source accumulators, and reuse of 16/32 for coarsened. The three hypotheses predict static shared/barrier coordinates with disclosed load-group coupling, normalized requested inputs, and a source-accumulator/resource question. Each includes rejection and no answer and predicts no elapsed winner. Disposition: `tile rationale derived; observations pending`.

**Valid alternative:** Branch another labeled tile coordinate from the canonical baseline if ownership, edge waste, reuse, resources, and the comparison contract are recomputed and no result is inherited from another workload.

**Common errors:** Writing `TM x TN` as “tile size” without `TK`; using an illegal 32-by-32 block without checking thread count; counting shared bytes but not barriers; calling a reuse ratio observed DRAM traffic; calling two source accumulators an exact register count; maximizing occupancy; prefilling a winner.

**Source basis:** The site's original [Q13 controlled GEMM contract](/en/correctness/gemm-optimization-case-study/), [A08 correctness and tiling model](/en/algorithms/tiled-gemm-correctness/), [Q10 traffic boundary](/en/correctness/roofline-arithmetic-intensity/), [EX15](/en/examples/tiled-gemm/), [VIS12](/en/visuals/gemm-tiling-hierarchy/), and [SRC-CUDA-056](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-056).

**Source date:** 2026-09-03.

<span id="pb-r3-012" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R3-012: Audit occupancy and production claims in an uncustodied GEMM report

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R3-012 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [Q13: Optimize the Canonical GEMM with Controlled Evidence](/en/correctness/gemm-optimization-case-study/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; audit a static summary and expected-only fixture without running CUDA, Nsight Compute, or LAB12.
- **Version gate:** Toolkits 11.8.0/12.9.2/13.3.1, Nsight Compute 2022.3.0.22/2025.2.1.3/2026.2.1.5, and exact GPU, compiler, profiler, and manifest gates.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [A08](/en/algorithms/tiled-gemm-correctness/), [Q06](/en/correctness/apod-optimization-loop/), [Q08](/en/correctness/kernel-first-nsight-compute/), [Q10](/en/correctness/roofline-arithmetic-intensity/), [Q13](/en/correctness/gemm-optimization-case-study/), [EX15](/en/examples/tiled-gemm/), and [VIS12](/en/visuals/gemm-tiling-hierarchy/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-03.

**Prompt:** A detached summary contains only an “occupancy 100%” screenshot and a “32x16 is fastest” conclusion. It omits source and binary hashes, matrix shape, precision, compiler resources, exact GPU and compute capability, query, permission, filtering, replay, `.ncu-rep`, Environment Manifest, raw attempts, and the EX15 comparator record. It also claims Tensor Core use and says the kernel directly replaces cuBLAS. Audit it.

**Goal:** Split occupancy, traffic, elapsed, architecture, and production statements into independent evidence chains, then turn unsupported conclusions into bounded unanswered questions.

**Constraints:**

- Repeat matrix shapes, A/B/C and accumulation types, compute capability, workload and C restoration, warm-up, checked synchronization, ten retained attempts with median and min/max, profiler method and permission, complete manifest, correctness result, and bounded interpretation for every stage.
- Separate source shared and accumulator estimates, compiler registers and local memory, theoretical occupancy, achieved occupancy, queried path traffic, and unprofiled elapsed samples. None substitutes for another.
- The source has no Tensor Core path, and this R3 scenario has no cuBLAS comparison evidence. Rolling R4 publication of L06/LAB12 does not backfill an API, comparison, result, or production recommendation into it.

**Expected evidence:** A claim-to-evidence matrix, five custody chains, missing-coordinate register, exact-GPU query-first repair plan, four unfilled stage records, blocker/no-answer rules, and a bounded replacement statement.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Reject the 100% occupancy, fastest, Tensor Core, and production-replacement claims as unsupported by the supplied evidence.
- Permission denial, unavailable metrics, missing compiler output, report or hash, incomplete manifest, or failed correctness yields a blocker rather than zero.
- Fill no metric, register count, occupancy, traffic, timing, speedup, winner, cuBLAS call, or runtime result.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>Ask whether the screenshot shows theoretical or achieved occupancy, what the exact definition and scope are, and where the primary report and hash reside.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Source-level multiply-add does not prove emitted FMA or MMA. Likewise, a library name supplies no layout, precision, workspace, stream, or algorithm contract.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** The audit labels all four conclusions `unsupported from uncustodied summary`. The resource chain needs the exact template instantiation, NVCC, flags, targets, compiler registers/shared/local memory, and binary hash. The occupancy chain adds exact device limits or a queried metric definition and report. The traffic chain needs a matched path and normalization. The elapsed chain needs correctness-qualified unprofiled raw attempts. The architecture and production chain notes that the source contains no Tensor Core primitive or supported production-replacement conclusion. [L06](/en/libraries/cublas-gemm/) and [LAB12](/en/labs/compare-gemm-with-cublas/) are now published, but neither backfills evidence into this summary; LAB12 still has empty compilation evidence and recorded observations and Pending Hardware Verification runtime. A new, conditional comparison requires completing LAB12's prerequisites and separately collecting matched correctness and measurement records. An acceptable replacement is: “This source proposes a two-output FP32 shared-memory candidate; correctness, compiled resources, occupancy, traffic, elapsed behavior, and production comparison remain pending.”

**Valid alternative:** If original reports, compiler outputs, commands, and the manifest can be recovered, verify custody and create a sanitized derivative. Otherwise start a new comparison from the Q13 fixture; never attach new evidence retroactively to the screenshot.

**Common errors:** Guessing an occupancy definition from a percentage; guessing registers from source accumulators; treating achieved occupancy as speed; inferring DRAM behavior from a tile-load estimate; inferring Tensor Core use from source `*` and `+`; inventing a cuBLAS API and result from its name; replacing a report, hash, and manifest with a screenshot.

**Source basis:** The site's original [Q13 resource and evidence boundary](/en/correctness/gemm-optimization-case-study/), [Q08 query and report custody](/en/correctness/kernel-first-nsight-compute/), [Q10 model boundary](/en/correctness/roofline-arithmetic-intensity/), [EX15](/en/examples/tiled-gemm/), [VIS12](/en/visuals/gemm-tiling-hierarchy/), and [SRC-CUDA-056](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-056).

**Source date:** 2026-09-03.

<span id="pb-r3-013" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R3-013: Audit numerical and traffic ledgers for stable and online softmax

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R3-013 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Concepts and implementation; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [A10: Numerically Stable Softmax, Online Normalization, and Fusion Boundaries](/en/algorithms/numerically-stable-softmax/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; calculate a finite host reference and static logical traffic without running CUDA.
- **Version gate:** Milakov and Gimelshein 2018, Blanchard, Higham, and Higham 2019, and CUDA Programming and Best Practices Guides v13.3; no backend or measured claim.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [A02](/en/algorithms/multi-stage-reduction/), [M02](/en/memory/coalescing-transactions/), [M03](/en/memory/shared-memory-tiling/), [A10](/en/algorithms/numerically-stable-softmax/), and [A11](/en/algorithms/attention-as-an-io-problem/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-03.

**Prompt:** A proposal evaluates exponentials directly for `x=[1000,1001,1002,1003]`, then claims “online softmax reads only once, and fusion must be faster.” Repair the numerical path and traffic claims: provide stable probabilities, every online `(m,l)` state, and logical bytes for stable three-pass and online-plus-output schedules on an FP32 row with `n=4096`.

**Goal:** Deliver an independently auditable numerical worksheet and pass ledger, bounding “one fewer logical read” by explicit boundary and residency assumptions.

**Constraints:** Inputs are finite; scalar state remains in fast storage; no exponentials are stored; emitting probabilities rereads logits; cache lines, transactions, and spills are excluded. State underflow, rounding, and bitwise boundaries. Fill no timing or speedup.

**Expected evidence:** A four-row stable-softmax table, four online states, two pass ledgers, an assumption and exclusion list, and a static-analysis replacement statement.

**Acceptance criteria:** Maximum is 1003, final probability is about `0.64391426`, and final `l` is about `1.55300179`. Stable counts `4*4096*4=65536 B`; online plus output counts `3*4096*4=49152 B`; difference is `16384 B`. The conclusion claims neither actual DRAM traffic nor performance.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>After shifting, logits are `[-3,-2,-1,0]`. Each increase of one in the maximum rescales the old denominator by `exp(-1)`.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>The online normalizer merges max and sum passes, but emitting every probability still rereads input when logits are not resident.</details>

**Reviewed solution:** Shifted exponentials are about `[0.04978707,0.13533528,0.36787944,1]`; division by `1.55300179` gives `[0.03205860,0.08714432,0.23688282,0.64391426]`. Online states are `(1000,1)`, `(1001,1.36787944)`, `(1002,1.50321472)`, and `(1003,1.55300179)`. Stable max, sum, and output reads plus one write total `4n` elements; online normalizer and output reads plus one write total `3n`, giving 65,536 B and 49,152 B. The 16,384 B difference is static at the declared logical boundary. Actual transactions, cache, spills, resource pressure, and elapsed time remain unknown.

**Valid alternative:** Use another finite row with a large common offset, or audit the same invariant with tile-wise merges, provided the reference, pass count, boundary, and exclusions are restated.

**Common errors:** Evaluating `exp(1003)` directly; equating overflow avoidance with no underflow; failing to rescale old `l`; calling one-pass normalization one-read output; inferring speedup from logical bytes.

**Source basis:** The site's original [A10 stable and online contract](/en/algorithms/numerically-stable-softmax/), [A02 reduction model](/en/algorithms/multi-stage-reduction/), and [SRC-CUDA-057](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-057), plus the [Online normalizer paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/1805.02867), [Accurate log-sum-exp and softmax paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/1909.03469), and NVIDIA [Best Practices bandwidth](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-c-best-practices-guide/index.html#bandwidth).

**Source date:** 2026-09-03.

<span id="pb-r3-014" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R3-014: Derive row state and an IO ledger for exact tiled attention

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R3-014 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Concepts and implementation; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [A11: Attention as an IO Problem](/en/algorithms/attention-as-an-io-problem/).
- **Hardware gate:** None; audit a real-arithmetic recurrence and static logical IO without running CUDA.
- **Version gate:** Vaswani et al. 2017, Milakov and Gimelshein 2018, Dao et al. 2022, and CUDA Best Practices Guide v13.3; papers supply no current backend or dtype claim.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [A08](/en/algorithms/tiled-gemm-correctness/), [A10](/en/algorithms/numerically-stable-softmax/), [A11](/en/algorithms/attention-as-an-io-problem/), and [VIS18](/en/visuals/attention-memory-traffic/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-03.

**Prompt:** For unmasked FP32 self-attention with `N=16,d=8`, compare a stable materialized schedule with a query-outer tiled schedule. Use `Br=Bc=4` and `Br=Bc=8`, calculating tile counts, per-stage transfers, and total bytes, then write the recurrence that merges two tile-local `(m_t,l_t,a_t)` states. A reviewer sees fewer logical bytes at larger Br and declares it the fastest tile and a production backend's choice.

**Goal:** Establish shape, row-state, residency, and traffic contracts while separating a static schedule comparison from backend and runtime claims.

**Constraints:** Q/K/V already exist and O is separate. Full S/P reads S three times and writes P. Tiled Q, state, and accumulator remain in fast storage. Exclude projection, mask, dropout, backward, cache lines, transactions, resource capacity, and spills. Exact means real-arithmetic equivalence only.

**Expected evidence:** A three-stage decomposition, merge recurrence, two-row tile-count and traffic table, full S/P materialization ledger, assumption list, and bounded verdict.

**Acceptance criteria:** Materialized total is `4Nd+6N^2=2048 elements=8192 B`. `4x4` has 16 score tiles and totals `1280 elements=5120 B`. `8x8` has four score tiles and totals `768 elements=3072 B`. Record that larger Br reduces K/V replay in this model, but reject fastest and backend claims.

<details><summary>Hint 1</summary>`T_r=ceil(N/Br)` and tiled total is `2Nd+2T_rNd`. Bc changes score-tile count but not total K/V logical elements in this query-outer ledger.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2</summary>Merge under one new maximum: `l'=exp(m-m')l+exp(m_t-m')l_t`; `a'` uses the same two scale factors.</details>
