## PB-R4-010: Rebuild hierarchy, layout, and stage-reuse proofs for an uneven GEMM

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R4-010 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Concepts and implementation; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [L09](/en/libraries/cutlass-cpp-gemm-structure/), with exact prerequisite set `[L09]`.
- **Hardware gate:** None; design review without CUTLASS instantiation, compilation, or execution. The ordinary SM75 path needs no `a` suffix.
- **Version gate:** CUTLASS C++ v4.7.0 commit `dcf215af68a2d08d305076c152a06f201728cd53`; retained 2.x-style API, selected two-stage path, and bounded 3.x comparison, without Python DSL.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [A08](/en/algorithms/tiled-gemm-correctness/), [L06](/en/libraries/cublas-gemm/), [M17](/en/toolchain/compiler-architecture-targets/), [L09](/en/libraries/cutlass-cpp-gemm-structure/), and [VIS12](/en/visuals/gemm-tiling-hierarchy/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-07.

**Prompt:** An unimplemented design computes `D=0.5*A*B+C` with `M=130,N=70,K=33`. A/B are FP16; C/D, accumulation, and epilogue computation are FP32. All four global matrices use RowMajor storage, with stride 40 for A and 80 for B/C/D; allocations cover complete physical rows. Candidate geometry uses threadblock tiles `64x32x16`, warp tiles `32x32x16`, and the SM75 `16x8x8` instruction-wrapper layer as a reading comparison. Geometry is only a derivation, not an existing valid template instantiation.

The author specifies `3*3*3=27` grid blocks, each handling one K slice and independently overwriting D; uses `row*70+col` for every RowMajor address; and exits edge threads before synchronization. A two-stage shared-memory pipeline overwrites stage 0 with the third K slice before the last consumer of its first use finishes reading. The author renames a 2.x warp to a 3.x collective, treats host-facing `device::Gemm` as a GPU-thread call, and claims `can_implement` success proves allocation extent, all dimensions being multiples of 16, native acceleration, and completion. There are no actual call results.

**Goal:** Reconstruct output ownership, complete K reduction, three distinct layout layers, and two-stage reuse proofs. Separate source-visible contracts from future instantiation and execution obligations.

**Constraints:** Preserve the ordinary non-split-K mapping, equation, and logical dimensions; geometry alone does not prove template support. Do not copy owner tests, introduce a DSL, or relabel VIS12's scalar slot as MMA/SASS.

**Expected evidence:** Block/warp counts and edge extents; global offset proofs; a stage-ownership timeline for three K slices; a Device/Kernel/Threadblock/Warp/Instruction responsibility table; a 3.x comparison and future evidence checklist.

**Acceptance criteria:** One block owns each output and applies `0.5*acc+C` once after all three K slices. Global layout is distinct from shared/register organization. Publication precedes consumption, and completed consumption precedes overwrite. Model counts, source wrapper operations, and checking-function returns are not measured results.

<details><summary>Hint 1: Establish D ownership first</summary>M/N determine output-block count; K slices belong to each block's internal reduction. For the bottom-right block, write valid row/column extents before independently checking the final D element's physical offset.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2: Mark each stage's last reader</summary>Three K slices share only two slots. The next write to stage 0 must follow the last read by every consumer of its first batch. Data being ready solves initial consumption, not reuse.</details>

<details>
<summary>Separate reviewed solution: Open after your attempt</summary>

**Reviewed solution:** The output grid contains `ceil(130/64)*ceil(70/32)=3*3=9` blocks. Each block internally visits `ceil(33/16)=3` K slices; multiplying these counts does not produce independent output blocks. The declared geometry has `2*1=2` output warp tiles per block. The bottom-right origin `(128,64)` has only `2x6` valid outputs, and the last K slice contains only `k=32`. None of this proves actual template instantiation, emitted instruction count, or thread scheduling.

Global A/B/C/D offsets are respectively `i*40+k`, `k*80+j`, `i*80+j`, and `i*80+j`. D's last logical element has offset `129*80+69=10389`, below the complete `130*80=10400`-element allocation. The proposed `row*70+col` discards padding. Shared storage can have its own layout, while register fragments use another thread/value organization; the global formula cannot describe all three. Fixed-size `cutlass::Matrix` and `cutlass::layout::RowMajor` are different utilities. The former's known non-square defect does not invalidate the latter's stride formula.

The stage ledger loads `k=0..15` into stage 0, completes and publishes it before consumption, then similarly uses stage 1 for `k=16..31`. Only after all first-batch consumers finish may stage 0 receive `k=32` and explicit zero padding. Bounds-aware staging or explicit padding supplies valid edge inputs; threads without data still reach required synchronization. Apply epilogue `0.5*acc+C` once after the final K slice and store only valid D elements. Do not add C separately for every slice. This timeline establishes dependencies, not copy/compute overlap.

Device is the host-facing typed handle for arguments, workspace, initialization, launch, and status. Kernel composes the block mainloop and epilogue. Threadblock traverses K and coordinates shared-storage reuse. Warp partitions tiles into register fragments and operation sequences. Instruction wrappers request concrete operations. The selected SM75 wrapper contains inline PTX `mma.sync.aligned.m16n8k8.row.col.f32.f16.f16.f32`, not C++ WMMA `16x16x16` and not observed SASS. Retain an all-FP32 `OpClassSimt` comparison baseline; its `1x1x1` shape is neither a Tensor Core operation nor an exact real-arithmetic reference.

The 3.x reading path is Device, Kernel, Collective, Tiled MMA/Copy, Atom. A collective coordinates cooperating threads and data dependencies; it is not a word-for-word replacement for a 2.x warp. CuTe C++ is not CuTe Python DSL. VIS12's instruction panel remains a source-level scalar `1x1x1` slot. Selected `MmaPipelined` inherits a two-stage base; that does not make every CUTLASS kernel double-buffered or establish use of L05's `cuda::pipeline` API.

At the pinned source, `can_implement` checks layout-dependent tensor-reference alignment, not universal M/N/K divisibility, allocation extent, numerical correctness, native acceleration, or completion. Future work must first establish the exact template's applicable layout/alignment/tail policy, then retain source SHA, toolchain, targets, commands, artifact hashes, diagnostics, and exit status. Actual instruction claims additionally need artifact inspection. Completion and independent reference checks are separate prerequisites for runtime evidence. Selecting ordinary `sm_75` yields no build result from a source `Sm75` tag alone; `90a` PTX is not a general future fallback.

**Valid alternatives and common errors:** Explicit padded storage or individually justified predicated iterators can preserve the same equation. Split-K needs a new merge and exactly-once-C proof and does not satisfy this task's primary constraint. Slice count is not block count; stage count is not synchronization; a type alias is not correctness; source presence is not performance.

</details>

**Source basis:** Original derivations; [L09](/en/libraries/cutlass-cpp-gemm-structure/), [TERM-190](/en/glossary/#term-190), and the pinned headers, tests, and license ledger in [SRC-CUDA-070](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-070). All owner material is reference-only; existing EX/Lab evidence is unchanged.

**Source date:** 2026-09-07.

## PB-R4-011: Audit engine candidates, total workspace, and a cache-policy change

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R4-011 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [L10](/en/libraries/cudnn-graphs-and-plans/), exactly `[L10]`.
- **Hardware gate:** None; an original hypothetical table, not a query, build, or execution.
- **Version gate:** Independent cuDNN backend 9.24.0 and frontend 1.27.0 commit `f77fbc3d21be3f24cd0286b9b368105f7c518b8a`.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [A07](/en/algorithms/convolution-reuse-layout/), [L01](/en/libraries/library-primitive-dsl-custom-kernel/), [Q05](/en/correctness/timing-asynchronous-gpu-work/), [L10](/en/libraries/cudnn-graphs-and-plans/), and [L11](/en/libraries/attention-backend-dispatch/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-07.

**Prompt:** A deployment review changes its scratch budget to 6 MiB and excludes `NONDETERMINISTIC` and `DOWN_CONVERT_INPUTS`. A hypothetical heuristic list is ordered as below. All sizes and outcomes are invented task inputs, not library results. The application sets the backend workspace filter to 6 MiB, declares all remaining rows supported after one successful support check, and caches the first list entry using only `graph->key()`.

| Ordered candidate | Numerical notes | Backend workspace | Frontend-node workspace | Hypothetical later build |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P | NONDETERMINISTIC | 1 MiB | 1 MiB | Not attempted |
| Q | DOWN_CONVERT_INPUTS | 2 MiB | 1 MiB | Not attempted |
| R | Neither excluded note | 6 MiB | 1 MiB | Succeeds |
| S | Neither excluded note | 3 MiB | 1 MiB | Fails |
| T | Neither excluded note | 4 MiB | 1 MiB | Succeeds |

**Goal:** Produce a policy/build/allocation decision ledger and repair reuse after the budget change without identifying a measured winner.

**Constraints:** Keep discovery order, distinguish backend filtering from final total scratch, and preserve a separate numerical-tolerance contract. A prior cache entry was built under a 12 MiB budget with down-conversion allowed. Inputs, outputs, and kernel-cache allocations are outside this scratch budget and still need their own memory accounting.

**Expected evidence:** A five-row rejection ledger, a build-policy comparison, a final workspace/lifetime checklist, and a cache-revalidation decision.

**Acceptance criteria:** Reject P/Q on policy, R on total scratch, and S on the given build failure. T is only the provisional admissible row under the supplied facts; 5 MiB is arithmetic from the hypothetical table, not a queried size. Neither `ALL` nor support success proves a fastest or numerically correct plan.

<details><summary>Hint 1: Add at the correct boundary</summary>The backend filter sees 6 MiB for R, but the application's total scratch condition sees 7 MiB. Filtering alone cannot implement the application budget.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2: Separate identity from policy</summary>A graph key does not encode a complete machine identity or every selection policy. Does the old policy permit something the new policy forbids?</details>

<details>
<summary>Separate reviewed solution: Open after your attempt</summary>

**Reviewed solution:** P/Q fail the numerical policy before support/build. R passes the backend-only cap but `6+1=7 MiB` exceeds total scratch. S fits `3+1=4 MiB` but the supplied later build fails. T fits `4+1=5 MiB` and is the sole provisional admissible row. Default `HEURISTICS_CHOICE` can stop at R's first successful build before the application's final-total check rejects it; do not assume the library automatically continues to T after that application rejection. Explicitly resume candidate handling or use an `ALL` policy and then apply final acceptance to the successfully built plans. `ALL` only attempts builds, not timing. `check_support` stops at an acceptable configuration and does not certify every row or guarantee later build success.

Invalidate or revalidate the old cache entry against the new budget and numerical exclusions. Namespace/recheck exact backend/frontend/build, device, graph semantics, dtype/layout/shape/alignment, and policy; a graph hash alone is insufficient. Query final workspace through the error-returning overload, allocate only after success, bind correct tensor UIDs, and retain exclusive scratch until completion. Separate graph JSON, selected-plan serialization, and backend CUBIN caching; none grants permanent cross-device validity. Excluding nondeterminism does not prove FP32-reference accuracy or cross-architecture bits. Local queries, builds, output, timing, and selected engine remain unrecorded.

**Valid alternatives and common errors:** A stricter preliminary cap can reduce candidates, but still needs the final total and may exclude useful plans. Do not silently relax numerical policy, reuse a rejected plan, allocate from an unchecked size query, or relabel the hypothetical T row as a real cuDNN engine.

</details>

**Source basis:** Original scenario, labels, table, and solution; [L10](/en/libraries/cudnn-graphs-and-plans/), [SRC-CUDA-071](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-071), and the immutable graph/plans source and tests in [SRC-CUDA-072](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-072). No owner question, code, asset, or result is copied.

**Source date:** 2026-09-07.

## PB-R4-012: Separate attention representation, router order, and strict-pin failure

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R4-012 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [L11](/en/libraries/attention-backend-dispatch/), exactly `[L11]`.
- **Hardware gate:** None; hypothetical dispatch review only. Future narrow SDPA execution needs native Linux, natively listed SM80+ hardware, and per-feature gates.
- **Version gate:** Independent cuDNN backend 9.24.0 and frontend 1.27.0 commit `f77fbc3d21be3f24cd0286b9b368105f7c518b8a`; narrow FP16/BF16 forward scope.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [A11](/en/algorithms/attention-as-an-io-problem/), [L10](/en/libraries/cudnn-graphs-and-plans/), [L08](/en/libraries/tensor-core-precision-contracts/), [L11](/en/libraries/attention-backend-dispatch/), and [VIS18](/en/visuals/attention-memory-traffic/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-07.

**Prompt:** Review four independent, hypothetical planning records for dense forward attention: equal Q/K/V heads, BHSD contiguous storage, equal sequence lengths greater than one, head dimension 64, all Q/K/V/O BF16, FP32 intermediate/compute, scale `1/sqrt(64)`, `generate_stats=false`, and no mask/dropout/bias/backward/paging. No row describes a GPU run.

| Record | Supplied planning fact | Proposed conclusion |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | Frontend AUTO chooses UNIFIED representation | A framework has selected the fastest fused kernel |
| B | Python ranked list `[proposal X, backend Y]`; unpinned X raises `NotImplementedError` during build | Any runtime exception may fall through to Y |
| C | Same list, explicit `select_plan(0)`; X declines | Strict pin silently selects Y |
| D | Two Python proposals build far enough to report 9 MiB and 3 MiB; cap is 4 MiB | Position zero proves engine identity and wins before checking workspace |

**Goal:** Repair each conclusion and specify what a future dispatch record must capture.

**Constraints:** Keep frontend representation, Python router ranking, backend engine configuration, and framework dispatch separate. Do not infer real engine IDs, emitted instructions, dtype accuracy, or timing from list positions, graph names, or owner test definitions.

**Expected evidence:** A four-row verdict, strict versus unpinned failure rules, final workspace acceptance, and a missing-evidence checklist.

**Acceptance criteria:** A proves only representation choice. B can continue on the named planning decline, not arbitrary execution failures. C raises instead of falling through. D rejects 9 MiB under the cap; 3 MiB only passes that one hypothetical condition. No actual engine or framework selection is recorded.

<details><summary>Hint 1: Name the layer</summary>A graph's AUTO implementation choice happens before ordinary backend heuristic discovery. A Python plan index is neither an engine ID nor an observation of framework dispatch.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2: Read the failure contract</summary>Unpinned planning handles specific decline exceptions. An explicit pin deliberately removes that fallback; neither is a recovery promise for arbitrary GPU faults.</details>

<details>
<summary>Separate reviewed solution: Open after your attempt</summary>

**Reviewed solution:** A records frontend feature-support selection of UNIFIED before backend candidate discovery/build. It identifies no final engine, framework choice, or performance. B may try Y after X's supplied `NotImplementedError`; the pinned source also handles `cudnnGraphNotSupportedError` during unpinned planning, not arbitrary runtime errors. C must report the pinned decline, not conceal a switch. D must check the Python compiled plan's reported workspace against 4 MiB: 9 MiB fails and 3 MiB passes only the budget test. Ranking and engine identity require separate fields, and correctness still needs independent acceptance.

Record exact frontend SHA/backend/build identity, device and software environment, graph semantics and tensor strides/types, requested policy, representation, ranked entries, selected identity, decline/build diagnostics, final workspace, binding and completion, independent numerical checks, and separately scoped measurements. Until those observations exist, leave outcome fields unrecorded. FP32 acceptance in COMPOSITE/AUTO does not authorize FP32 UNIFIED ports; it also does not prove a backend plan exists. The BF16 teaching restriction is a chosen narrow scope, not a blanket rejection of all other cuDNN types. VIS18's logical IO cannot fill a dispatch field. No framework dispatcher is independently pinned by this exercise.

**Valid alternatives and common errors:** Reject a failed strict pin and ask for a new explicit policy, or use an unpinned policy from the start. Do not reinterpret a pin as a preference, conflate source tests with their execution, or use a GPU family label to authorize the separate exact-SM100 C++ open-source path.

</details>

**Source basis:** Original records and solution; [L11](/en/libraries/attention-backend-dispatch/), [SRC-CUDA-071](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-071), and pinned `router.py`, `_pygraph.py`, support-surface and owner tests in [SRC-CUDA-072](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-072). No upstream code, question, asset, or result is copied or executed.

**Source date:** 2026-09-07.

## PB-R4-013: Repair row padding and typed distances in a batched real FFT

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R4-013 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Correctness debugging; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [L12](/en/libraries/cufft-plans-layouts-startup/), exactly `[L12]`.
- **Hardware gate:** None; static address and numerical reasoning, without CUDA execution.
- **Version gate:** Toolkit 11.8.0/12.9.2/13.3.1 with independent cuFFT 10.9.0.58/11.4.1.4/12.3.0.29; ordinary FP32, no callbacks, low precision, or multi-GPU.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [L12](/en/libraries/cufft-plans-layouts-startup/) and [EX19](/en/examples/cufft-batched-transform/). EX19 is C2C only and does not implement this real-transform audit.
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-08.

**Prompt:** Two independent FP32 images each contain `3x10` logical real values. A proposed in-place, rank-two R2C plan uses `n=[3,10]`, `batch=2`, unit strides, NULL embeddings, and `idist=odist=30`. The allocation has 60 floats, and the author puts any padding at the very end. For an inverse they propose an out-of-place C2R call, promise to preserve its spectral input, and divide each output by 60. Repair the layout and inverse contract without running either transform.

**Goal:** Produce a typed address ledger, a safe inverse-input policy, and the correct normalization factor.

**Constraints:** Each float is 4 bytes and each FP32 complex value is 8 bytes. Use the ordinary contiguous packed layout with padding on every innermost row, an adequately complex-aligned allocation base, and non-NULL embeddings. Keep logical shape separate from physical storage. The two batches are independent transforms, not one `2x3x10` transform.

**Expected evidence:** Values for K, both embeddings, both distances, allocation bytes, two checked offsets, and a Hermitian-input/normalization checklist. These are original paper calculations, not GPU outputs.

**Acceptance criteria:** Derive K=6, 12 real slots per row, 36 real slots or 18 complex slots per batch, and 288 total bytes. Check real `(batch=1,row=2,column=9)` at byte 276 and stored complex `(batch=1,row=2,k=5)` at byte 280. Explain why NULL embeddings defeat the proposed advanced layout, why a C2R input copy may be required even out-of-place, and why round-trip scaling is 30 rather than 60.

<details><summary>Hint 1: Give every distance a type</summary>The real and complex views share bytes, not element counts. Start with `K=floor(10/2)+1` and compare `idist*4` with `odist*8`.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2: Reverse every frequency coordinate</summary>For a two-dimensional real input, Hermitian symmetry conjugates the frequency at both negated coordinates. It does not make every value in an entire boundary column purely real.</details>

<details>
<summary>Separate reviewed solution: Open after your attempt</summary>

**Reviewed solution:** `K=floor(10/2)+1=6`; each innermost row needs `2K=12` real slots, not 10. Choose `inembed=[3,12]`, `onembed=[3,6]`, `istride=ostride=1`, `idist=36` real elements, and `odist=18` complex elements. The allocation is `2*3*12=72` floats, or `72*4=288` bytes, rather than `60*4=240` bytes. Padding belongs after each ten-value row. Keep both embedding pointers non-NULL so cuFFT honors the advanced layout parameters rather than assuming the default layout.

The real element offset is `1*36+2*12+9=69`, hence `69*4=276` bytes. The stored complex element offset is `1*18+2*6+5=35`, hence `35*8=280` bytes; its final byte is 287, inside the allocation. Row starts advance by 48 bytes in either view, and batch starts by 144 bytes. Both remain aligned when the base meets the complex-type alignment; a one-float subview shift must not be assumed safe.

For the inverse, describe complex input embedding/distance and real output embedding/distance separately, reversing the forward roles only when retaining the same padded geometry. Out-of-place C2R can overwrite its complex input, so preserve a needed spectrum with a separate copy before execution. The full spectrum obeys `X[r,k]=conj(X[(-r) mod 3,(-k) mod 10])`. Only coordinates self-conjugate in every dimension must have zero imaginary part; here `(0,0)` and `(0,5)`. A shape-correct but non-Hermitian input has no valid real-inverse guarantee. cuFFT normalizes neither direction: divide the round trip by `3*10=30`, not by the two-batch count times 30. All-one input would have DC=30 per image as a mathematical expectation, not an observed result.

**Valid alternatives and common errors:** A separately allocated out-of-place R2C design can use unpadded real input with a newly specified layout; it cannot retain the in-place allocation argument. Do not substitute bytes for element distances, append one global padding region, require every Nyquist-column value to be real, or infer real-transform validation from EX19's C2C path.

</details>

**Source basis:** Original dimensions, erroneous proposal, address ledger, and solution; [L12](/en/libraries/cufft-plans-layouts-startup/) and the archived/current layout, data-format, and plan contracts in [SRC-CUDA-073](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-073). No owner example, question, table, or result is copied.

**Source date:** 2026-09-08.
