## PB-R4-014: Audit cuFFT workspace ownership and cold-start amortization

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R4-014 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [L12](/en/libraries/cufft-plans-layouts-startup/), exactly `[L12]`.
- **Hardware gate:** None; hypothetical lifecycle and cost review. Future EX19 execution needs native Linux and CC 7.5+, without callbacks or low precision.
- **Version gate:** Toolkit 11.8.0/12.9.2/13.3.1 and cuFFT 10.9.0.58/11.4.1.4/12.3.0.29; current JIT, LTO callback, NVRTC, and known-issue boundaries remain separately versioned.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [Q05](/en/correctness/timing-asynchronous-gpu-work/), [M07](/en/memory/stream-ordering/), [L12](/en/libraries/cufft-plans-layouts-startup/), and [EX19](/en/examples/cufft-batched-transform/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-08.

**Prompt:** A hypothetical service makes plans P and Q before disabling autoallocation, then binds the same 5 MiB allocation as workspace to both plans. Given workspace requirements are 3 MiB and 5 MiB, not queried results. It submits distinct input/output buffers in two streams, immediately frees scratch after the host calls return, and advertises a 0.5 ms cold request. A separate, serial 20-request thought experiment assumes initialization=4 ms, plan construction=12 ms, first execution=2 ms, and each later execution=0.5 ms. Finally, a proposed FP32 real-side LTO callback transform uses `N=16256`; the author says the 13.3 Update 1 warning is fixed because 8192 is a power of two. Audit all three claims.

**Goal:** Repair allocation and completion order, calculate the declared startup-inclusive cost, and apply the known-issue predicate without creating performance or runtime evidence.

**Constraints:** Workspace sizes and times are invented inputs, not measurements. The two-plan memory scenario and one-plan cost scenario are separate. The cost model omits transfers, validation, and teardown; name that boundary. No callback, real transform, or low-precision path is added to EX19.

**Expected evidence:** A lifecycle sequence, concurrent versus serialized scratch budgets, a 20-request arithmetic ledger, a cache-state checklist, and a scoped callback rejection.

**Acceptance criteria:** Disable autoallocation before make-plan, check every result, and retain exclusive scratch until the relevant stream work completes. Concurrent plans need 8 MiB of disjoint scratch under the given sizes; serialized ownership may reuse 5 MiB. Derive 27.5 ms total and 1.375 ms per request, not a measured latency. Factor 16256 and evaluate every conjunct of the known hazard; do not call it fixed or generalize it to EX19.

<details><summary>Hint 1: Host return is not completion</summary>Separate create, autoallocation policy, make-plan/size, allocation, work-area binding, stream binding, execution, completion, and cleanup. Independent data buffers do not make overlapping scratch safe.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2: Separate one-time and repeated costs</summary>Count the first execution once and the later execution cost nineteen times. A driver JIT cache, a live reusable plan, and an application result cache are different things.</details>

<details>
<summary>Separate reviewed solution: Open after your attempt</summary>

**Reviewed solution:** Use checked `cufftCreate`, then `cufftSetAutoAllocation(plan,0)` before `cufftMakePlanMany`; only after successful planning use the returned workspace requirement, allocate as needed, and call `cufftSetWorkArea` and `cufftSetStream`. Do not infer that disabling autoallocation afterward prevented planning-time allocations. Keep each plan's data and workspace valid and its scratch exclusive until an explicit stream completion boundary. On success or failure, settle already-submitted work before freeing its resources; destroy plans and release caller-owned scratch separately after completion.

Concurrent P/Q need `3+5=8 MiB` in disjoint, suitably allocated regions. A 5 MiB buffer suffices only with serialized use: finish P before Q starts using it, using one ordered stream or an explicit cross-stream event/wait dependency. A host-side queueing order alone is not that dependency. Plan reconfiguration must also wait until prior use completes.

The separate cost model yields `4+12+2+19*0.5=27.5 ms` for 20 requests and `27.5/20=1.375 ms` per request. Its first request costs `4+12+2=18 ms`; 0.5 ms describes only the supplied later-execution assumption. These are neither site timings nor a speedup claim. A future measurement must distinguish process/context initialization, cold versus populated driver JIT cache, plan construction, first execution, reused-plan execution, transfers, and completion. More PTX JIT occurs during planning since CUDA 12.0; driver caching may avoid repeated compilation but does not serialize a live plan or guarantee a latency. Record driver, Toolkit/component, GPU, environment, and cache policy instead of inventing cache hits. LTO kernels since 12.4, LTO callbacks since 12.6 Update 2, and the added NVRTC dependency since 13.2 are separate boundaries.

`16256=128*127=2^7*127` is even, is at least 8192, and has largest prime factor 127. With the proposed FP32 real-side LTO callback, it satisfies the full 13.3 Update 1 known correctness-hazard conjunction. The FP64 threshold is 4096. By contrast, `8192=2^13` lacks the prime-factor conjunct; that does not make the warning fixed or prove every other callback configuration safe. Reject this proposed configuration or remove the callback and independently validate a supported path. EX19's ordinary FP32 C2C implementation has no callback and supplies no callback verification.

**Valid alternatives and common errors:** Serial scratch reuse trades concurrency for an explicit ownership proof, not a guaranteed performance improvement. Keeping autoallocation enabled is a different declared ownership policy. Never free caller-owned scratch on asynchronous submission return, treat an owner known issue as a fixed issue, or label the invented timing ledger Runtime-Verified.

</details>

**Source basis:** Original service proposal, memory sizes, arithmetic, and hazard example; [L12](/en/libraries/cufft-plans-layouts-startup/), lifecycle contracts in [SRC-CUDA-073](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-073), and exact version/source boundaries in [SRC-CUDA-074](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-074). No owner code, benchmark, question, or result is copied or executed.

**Source date:** 2026-09-08.

<span id="pb-r4-015" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R4-015: Audit CSR descriptors, index base, and ownership

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R4-015 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Correctness debugging; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [L13](/en/libraries/cusparse-descriptors-spmv-spmm/), exactly `[L13]`.
- **Hardware gate:** None; static descriptor and arithmetic review without CUDA execution. EX20 retains independent Pending Hardware Verification runtime.
- **Version gate:** Exact Toolkit 12.9.2 Generic API baseline; ordinary FP32 NON_TRANSPOSE SpMV with `CUSPARSE_SPMV_CSR_ALG2`, not a cross-device determinism promise.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [A12](/en/algorithms/sparse-formats-spmv/), [L13](/en/libraries/cusparse-descriptors-spmv-spmm/), and [EX20](/en/examples/cusparse-spmv/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-09.

**Prompt:** A proposed `4x5` CSR matrix has six stored entries: offsets `[1,3,3,5,7]`, columns `[1,5,2,4,1,3]`, and values `[2,-1,3,4,-2,5]`. The two index arrays use signed 32-bit elements and values use FP32. An author selects ZERO base and 64-bit index types, gives X length 4 and Y length 5, and passes ordinary host arrays because the handle uses HOST pointer mode. They claim descriptor construction copies the data and fixes any duplicate coordinates. Compute the intended `y=2*A*x-y0` for `x=[1,2,3,4,5]`, `y0=[1,2,3,4]`, then repair the proposal.

**Goal:** Produce a descriptor/call ledger, an independent mathematical reference, and an ownership sequence that rejects the invalid proposal before submission.

**Constraints:** Preserve the intended matrix and its empty second row. Choose either consistent one-based arrays or an explicit conversion of both index arrays to zero base. Do not reinterpret 32-bit bytes as 64-bit elements. Use device storage for the sparse arrays and dense vectors; HOST mode applies to alpha/beta. No CUDA call, workspace query, or bitwise result has occurred.

**Expected evidence:** Repaired index arrays/types/base and vector lengths; CSR payload and total data bytes excluding workspace; four output values; lifetime and duplicate-input rejection rules.

**Acceptance criteria:** X has length 5, Y length 4, both FP32. CSR payload is 68 bytes and CSR plus X/Y is 104 bytes, excluding descriptors, padding, and workspace. Derive `A*x=[-3,0,22,13]` and `y=[-7,-2,41,22]`. Reject duplicate correctness guarantees and cross-CPU/GPU/version bitwise claims; retain caller-owned buffers through completion.

<details><summary>Hint 1: Check endpoints before multiplication</summary>For base b, offsets start at b and end at `nnz+b`. Equal adjacent offsets mean an empty row. The enum must match the actual element width, not the host machine's pointer width.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2: Separate metadata from allocation</summary>A descriptor records pointers. HOST pointer mode does not move matrix or vector storage to the host, and a successful asynchronous host return does not establish device completion.</details>

<details>
<summary>Separate reviewed solution: Open after your attempt</summary>

**Reviewed solution:** Keep the arrays with `CUSPARSE_INDEX_BASE_ONE` and `CUSPARSE_INDEX_32I` for both index arrays, or subtract one from both to obtain offsets `[0,2,2,4,6]` and columns `[0,4,1,3,0,2]` with ZERO base. Offsets have length 5, are monotonic, and end at `nnz+base`; each column lies in the declared range. Row 1 is empty. Values and X/Y use `CUDA_R_32F`; the call separately chooses NON_TRANSPOSE, FP32 compute, alpha=2, beta=-1, and `CUSPARSE_SPMV_CSR_ALG2`.

CSR data uses `5*4+6*4+6*4=68` bytes; X/Y add `(5+4)*4=36`, giving 104 bytes of problem data, not total device memory or queried workspace. Row products are `2*1-1*5=-3`, 0, `3*2+4*4=22`, and `-2*1+5*3=13`. Applying alpha/beta gives `[-7,-2,41,22]`; beta makes the empty row's output -2 rather than zero. These are exact paper results, not GPU outputs.

Validate arrays and reject duplicate coordinates under this exercise's input policy before creating descriptors; cuSPARSE offers no blanket correctness guarantee for duplicates and construction is not a sanitizer. Allocate/copy device data, create Generic descriptors with matching types and lengths, bind the stream and scalar mode, query the exact call's workspace bytes, allocate if needed, submit with checked statuses, and observe completion before reading results or releasing resources. Destroy descriptors and free caller storage separately, including on failure after submission. A real run would compare against an independently implemented reference and declared tolerance. The selected NON_TRANSPOSE CSR_ALG2 contract is bounded run-to-run determinism under fixed conditions, not equality with a CPU reduction, other GPUs, or another library version.

**Valid alternatives and common errors:** Either index base is valid with consistently converted arrays. Wider indices need newly represented arrays and a new payload ledger. Do not change only offsets, treat `nnz` as matrix dimensions, erase beta for an empty row, or infer an observed workspace size from 104 bytes.

</details>

**Source basis:** Original arrays, erroneous descriptor proposal, arithmetic, and solution; [L13](/en/libraries/cusparse-descriptors-spmv-spmm/), [TERM-195](/en/glossary/#term-195), and [SRC-CUDA-075](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-075). No owner sample, header, question, or output is copied or executed.

**Source date:** 2026-09-09.

<span id="pb-r4-016" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R4-016: Audit format choice and active preprocessing buffer lifetime

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R4-016 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [L13](/en/libraries/cusparse-descriptors-spmv-spmm/), exactly `[L13]`.
- **Hardware gate:** None; hypothetical format and lifecycle review. Future EX20 execution needs native Linux and CC 7.5+; structured sparsity and narrow precision remain separately gated.
- **Version gate:** Toolkit 12.9.2 baseline with exact 11.8.0/13.3.1 comparisons; SpMV and SpMM preprocessing have different introduction and algorithm contracts.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [A12](/en/algorithms/sparse-formats-spmv/), [A13](/en/algorithms/sparse-matrix-multiplication-preprocessing/), [L01](/en/libraries/library-primitive-dsl-custom-kernel/), [L13](/en/libraries/cusparse-descriptors-spmv-spmm/), and [EX20](/en/examples/cusparse-spmv/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-09.

**Prompt:** A hypothetical service reuses PB-R4-015's `4x5`, six-entry pattern for SpMV and separately for SpMM with three dense right-hand sides. It declares CSR fastest from payload alone. On Toolkit 11.8 it plans to call `cusparseSpMV_preprocess`. On 12.9.2 it preprocesses descriptor D with buffer P, later preprocesses the same D with Q, then treats both as active for concurrent requests. It overwrites P as unrelated scratch, edits an index, and frees Q on host return. Its SpMM branch selects `CUSPARSE_SPMM_CSR_ALG2` and assumes preprocessing accelerates it. Finally, it calls ordinary CSR a cuSPARSELt structured Tensor Core input. Audit each claim; no request has run.

**Goal:** Build a representation ledger, a valid active-buffer lifecycle, a permitted-change table, and a version/algorithm rejection list without selecting a performance winner.

**Constraints:** FP32 values and 32-bit indices each occupy four bytes. Dense matrices are ordinary contiguous row-major with NON_TRANSPOSE operations. Buffer capacities are deliberately unspecified: derive no workspace size from `nnz`. Separate SpMV's X/Y from SpMM's B/C, and do not treat one operation's preprocessing as the other's state.

**Expected evidence:** Dense/COO/CSR payloads, B/C shape and leading dimensions, six lifecycle verdicts, and allowed-versus-invalidating changes for the 12.9.2 SpMV path.

**Acceptance criteria:** Dense/COO/CSR payloads are `80/72/68` bytes, not a speed ranking. B is `5x3`, C is `4x3`, both `ld=3`. Reject SpMV preprocessing on 11.8, the two-active-buffer claim, index edits without renewed preparation, early release, the SpMM ALG2 acceleration claim, and arbitrary-CSR structured-sparsity eligibility.

<details><summary>Hint 1: Compare the complete workload</summary>COO pays for two indices per entry; CSR pays for one plus row offsets. Small payload differences cannot predict irregular loads, conversion, preprocessing, repeated execution, or dense GEMM time.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2: An allocation is not active state</summary>Preprocessing D with Q makes P inactive for D. Legal value changes differ from index/configuration changes; no permitted change authorizes racing with in-flight work.</details>

<details>
<summary>Separate reviewed solution: Open after your attempt</summary>

**Reviewed solution:** Dense A uses `4*5*4=80` bytes, COO `6*(4+4+4)=72`, and CSR `5*4+6*(4+4)=68`. These exclude vectors, descriptors, workspace, conversion, and physical traffic. SpMM needs B `5x3` and C `4x3`, each with row-major `ld=3`; last logical offsets are 14 and 11. BSR additionally requires a declared block shape, block occupancy/padding, and exact operation support; a format name or stored-entry count cannot establish a winner against CSR, COO, or matched dense GEMM.

| Proposed action | Repaired contract |
| --- | --- |
| Call SpMV preprocessing on 11.8 | Reject the unavailable symbol; use supported SpMV without preprocessing. `cusparseSpMV_preprocess` starts at 12.4 and remains optional. |
| Keep P and Q active on D | Only Q is active after the second preprocessing. Settle prior work before reconfiguration. For independent prepared states, use separate descriptors and separately owned buffers; shared unchanged matrix arrays do not make writable workspace shareable. |
| Overwrite scratch or edit an index | Active-buffer contents and matrix indices must remain unchanged. An inactive P may be repurposed only after its last use completes. Index or configuration changes require revalidation and renewed preparation if acceleration is desired. |
| Free Q after host return | Preserve the active buffer and referenced data until the relevant stream work completes; check completion and clean up safely on success or failure. |
| Accelerate SpMM CSR_ALG2 by preprocessing | `cusparseSpMM_preprocess` exists in 11.8 and helps CSR_ALG1/ALG3, not CSR_ALG2. Do not transfer SpMV's algorithm enums or introduction date to SpMM. |
| Treat arbitrary CSR as cuSPARSELt input | Reject. The independent cuSPARSELt reference needs supported structured patterns, pruning/checking and compression, types, layouts, device, and version gates; density alone supplies none of them. |

For 12.9.2 SpMV active-state reuse, the documented exceptions permit changed alpha/beta, X/Y, and matrix values while preserving matrix indices, active-buffer contents, and the remaining configuration. Preserve compatible dimensions/types and order updates after earlier reads/writes; these exceptions are not permission for races. Changing operation, compute type, algorithm, or structure requires a fresh contract check and appropriate size query/preprocessing. Query workspace for each exact call and keep returned bytes separate from observed runtime cost. A supported no-preprocess path is valid and carries no implied performance penalty here.

**Valid alternatives and common errors:** Serialize requests with explicit completion edges, or use independent prepared descriptors/buffers with safe data ownership. A future comparison must include representation/conversion, preparation amortization, workspace, correctness, and scoped repeated timing. Do not infer a sparse winner, structured Tensor Core execution, or EX20 runtime evidence from this static review.

</details>

**Source basis:** Original service proposal, payload ledger, and solution; [L13](/en/libraries/cusparse-descriptors-spmv-spmm/), [TERM-196](/en/glossary/#term-196), [SRC-CUDA-075](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-075), and [SRC-CUDA-076](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-076). No upstream question, code, figure, or benchmark is copied or executed.

**Source date:** 2026-09-09.

<span id="pb-r5-001" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R5-001: Audit bridge ownership for queued requests

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R5-001 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Correctness debugging; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [P01](/en/python/cuda-python-bridge/), exactly `[P01]`.
- **Hardware gate:** None; original paper queue/ownership review, without CUDA execution.
- **Version gate:** Ordinary CPython 3.14.7; core 1.2.0, bindings 13.4.1, pathfinder 1.8.1, NumPy 2.5.3; the single native Linux Toolkit 13.3.1 profile.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [F04](/en/foundations/host-device-lifecycle/), [M07](/en/memory/stream-ordering/), [P01](/en/python/cuda-python-bridge/), and [EX21](/en/examples/cuda-python-launch/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-12.

**Prompt:** A service keeps every Buffer, stream and kernel owner alive. Request A uses pinned `a=[1,2,3]` and `b=[0.5,-1,2]`. It computes a Python reference and queues H2D, addition and D2H on one stream. Before checking completion, the CPU overwrites that same pinned a with request B's `[4,5,6]` and queues B on the same stream. Both returned results are views of the same pinned output. The author says reference retention and stream ordering guarantee two independent request snapshots.

**Goal:** Distinguish valid allocation lifetime from stable input contents, request identity, and result readiness.

**Constraints:** Keep both mathematical requests unchanged, with b unchanged. No timing, transfer completion, or observed result is supplied. Do not predict a particular race outcome or invent a cancellation API. A solution may serialize reuse or retain independent per-request storage.

**Expected evidence:** Derive both intended vectors; annotate CPU writes versus queued CUDA reads/writes; locate the reuse boundary for each input and output; state what a returned result owns and when it becomes readable.

**Acceptance criteria:** The reference stays CPU work. Keeping an allocation alive does not freeze its bytes, and same-stream CUDA order does not order arbitrary CPU overwrites. Check completion before shared-slot reuse and copy out an independently owned result, or keep separate immutable-in-flight request storage and outputs. A failed completion yields no valid result or runtime evidence.

<details><summary>Hint 1: Draw a second timeline for the CPU</summary>The host overwrite is not a CUDA operation enqueued after request A. Ask whether A's H2D has finished reading before that write begins.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2: A result can change after becoming ready</summary>Even a completed output is not a stable request snapshot if B later writes the same storage. Decide when values are copied out or who prevents reuse.</details>

<details>
<summary>Separate reviewed solution: Open after your attempt</summary>

**Reviewed solution:** A intends `[1.5,1,5]`; B intends `[4.5,4,8]`. These are exact arithmetic predictions. The actual result of the invalid schedule is unspecified here: the problem gives no evidence about when DMA reads happen. Retained references prevent one lifetime defect but do not stop the CPU from modifying data while a queued transfer reads it.

The simplest design uses a single request slot: initialize A, submit its copies/kernel, check completion, compare and copy accepted output into an owned Python value, then initialize B. Do not return an unowned view and reuse its storage. Keeping six three-element float32 arrays consumes 36 device payload bytes and 36 pinned-host bytes, excluding other resources and owned result copies.

An independent-storage design uses separate input and output Buffers per request and retains them through their transfers and result consumption. Neither input snapshot may be modified while it is in use; readiness still needs checked completion. Two complete six-array sets use 72 device and 72 pinned-host payload bytes. Keeping both requests on one stream still serializes their CUDA work and establishes no overlap or speedup. Abandoned results require an ownership/cleanup policy, not an invented safe-cancel operation.

**Valid alternatives and common errors:** An explicit slot-lease protocol can also prevent reuse. Returning owned CPU values simplifies the caller contract but adds a copy; borrowed results can avoid that copy only while an owner prevents reuse. A future-like name, a live view, or successful launch submission provides neither a snapshot nor a completion proof.

</details>

**Source basis:** Original two-request scenario and derivations; [P01](/en/python/cuda-python-bridge/), [Borrowed View](/en/glossary/#term-200), [SRC-CUDA-077](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-077), and [SRC-CUDA-079](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-079). No upstream exercise or observed result is copied.

**Source date:** 2026-09-12.

<span id="pb-r5-002" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R5-002: Audit scalar width and premature release

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R5-002 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Correctness debugging; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [P02](/en/python/devices-contexts-launches/), exactly `[P02]`.
- **Hardware gate:** None; paper ABI, capacity and lifetime audit. A real run separately needs native Linux and a compiler-supported CC 7.5+ GPU.
- **Version gate:** Core 1.2.0 Buffer, ctypes scalar and shared-primary-context contracts; EX21's single Toolkit 13.3.1 profile.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [F07](/en/foundations/runtime-driver-api/), [P02](/en/python/devices-contexts-launches/), and [EX21](/en/examples/cuda-python-launch/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-12.

**Prompt:** Review a proposed 513-element run of the EX21 kernel. The host allocates 513 bytes per float array, passes plain Python `513`, launches `grid=513//256`, names the result of `d_c.copy_to(stream=s)` `host_c`, and dereferences it with ctypes. It compares only the first 512 values, closes the stream, frees raw Buffer addresses, and resets the primary context. Independently suppose a corrected-size output has a NaN sentinel at index 512 because the last store was missed.

**Goal:** Repair coverage, representation, copy location, complete validation, and teardown as independent contracts.

**Constraints:** Use 256-thread blocks, an unsigned 32-bit n, float32 arrays, and the canonical EX21 input formula. No invalid pointer access should be executed. The NaN is a supplied diagnostic scenario, not an observed GPU result.

**Expected evidence:** A byte ledger, grid and tail derivation, exact argument types, explicit copy destination, the reference at index 512, and normal/error release sequences.

**Acceptance criteria:** Allocate 2052 bytes per array; require three blocks with 255 inactive tail threads; use `ctypes.c_uint32(n)` and Buffer pointer arguments. Inspect every finite result only after checked sync. Distinguish owned Buffers, borrowed host views, shared library ownership and primary-context state; no double-free or generic close loop.

<details><summary>Hint 1: The first unchecked element is the interesting one</summary>Index 512 belongs to block 2, not either of the first two blocks. Count bytes separately from elements and derive its reference from both input periods.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2: Names do not move storage</summary>Omitted-destination copy allocates through the source memory resource. Only an explicit host-accessible Buffer supports the proposed CPU view, and its owner must survive the view.</details>

<details>
<summary>Separate reviewed solution: Open after your attempt</summary>

**Reviewed solution:** Each array needs `513*4=2052` bytes; three device arrays use 6156 bytes and three pinned host arrays another 6156. Ceiling division gives three blocks, 768 candidate threads and 255 inactive threads. The bounds guard protects reads and writes for indices 513 through 767. Raw Python int is pointer-width in this core, so use uint32 after checking the positive size/range policy.

Use explicit pinned `h_c`, retain it, and queue `d_c.copy_to(h_c, stream=s)`. Do not infer host accessibility from the returned variable name. At index 512, `512 % 37=31` and `512 % 19=18`, giving `7.75-9=-1.25`. The supplied NaN must be rejected, even if the first 512 entries match. In the real project the output sentinel is uploaded before launch; full finite comparison catches a missed store instead of trusting recycled memory.

On success, complete D2H, check `s.sync()`, compare all elements, stop using/drop views, close each Buffer on s, drain frees with another checked sync, then close s. On failure, stop submission, keep the first stage/error, attempt completion and all acquired-resource cleanup, retain secondary errors/warnings, and exit nonzero. Do not free raw addresses still owned by Buffers. Device has no public close; Kernel/ObjectCode use shared library ownership with no public close; the primary context is not private resettable state.

**Valid alternatives and common errors:** A broader `dev.sync()` may establish completion but can wait for unrelated work. A different block size requires a new coverage proof, not a presumed speedup. A checksum, sampled comparison, Python reference pass, or silent exception handler cannot certify the missing final output.

</details>

**Source basis:** Original uneven-size and teardown proposal; [P02](/en/python/devices-contexts-launches/), [Kernel Argument Marshalling](/en/glossary/#term-199), and [SRC-CUDA-077](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-077). No CUDA run or upstream test result is represented.

**Source date:** 2026-09-12.

<span id="pb-r5-003" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R5-003: Separate compiler and linker error surfaces

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R5-003 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Correctness debugging; intermediate.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [P03](/en/python/runtime-compilation-linking/), exactly `[P03]`.
- **Hardware gate:** None; paper error/artifact audit. Actual build requires real libcuda userspace and the selected native compiler/linker, but no GPU.
- **Version gate:** Stable bindings 13.4.1 NVRTC status tuples versus nvJitLink exceptions; core 1.2.0, native NVRTC/nvJitLink 13.3.33.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M15](/en/toolchain/nvcc-compilation-flow/), [M16](/en/toolchain/ptx-cubin-fatbinary/), [P03](/en/python/runtime-compilation-linking/), and [EX21](/en/examples/cuda-python-launch/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-12.

**Prompt:** A build dashboard always unpacks a native result as `(status, value)` and marks nonzero status as a kernel failure. It reports three hypothetical attempts: A, real libcuda discovery fails before compilation; B, `nvjitlink.version()` returns `(13,3)` and the dashboard labels 13 an error; C, core Program raises during compilation, its StringIO is empty, and the dashboard therefore displays yesterday's cubin as today's successful result. In a separate low-level link attempt, `complete(handle)` raises and `destroy(handle)` also raises before the log is saved.

**Goal:** Correct stage attribution, result-shape handling, artifact freshness, and preservation of diagnostic ownership.

**Constraints:** Treat these as supplied scenarios, not collected logs. Keep GPU-free build free of `Device()`, `cuInit`, lookup and launch. Do not substitute stubs, fake libraries, private core exception imports, or another native Toolkit version.

**Expected evidence:** A verdict table for A/B/C and the low-level failure; a correct NVRTC/nvJitLink return-shape ledger; a fresh-artifact policy; and the minimum provenance needed before recording a native build pass.

**Acceptance criteria:** No case proves a kernel ran. Version 13 is not a status. Empty core success-log storage does not erase an exception's compiler diagnostic. Read a valid failed linker's log into queried-size writable storage before one destroy attempt; retain complete failure as primary. Stale artifacts cannot satisfy the failed attempt.

<details><summary>Hint 1: Start with the producer of each value</summary>NVRTC compile returns a one-item status tuple, while nvJitLink version returns two version numbers and create returns an integer handle. Similar tuple shapes need not have the same meaning.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2: Evidence belongs to one attempt</summary>A cubin from yesterday has another production history. Preserve this attempt's original exception and any available logs before releasing the object that owns them.</details>

<details>
<summary>Separate reviewed solution: Open after your attempt</summary>

**Reviewed solution:** A is a native-library/profile blocker. Core 1.2.0 queries the driver version for PTX loadability without cuInit, so lack of GPU execution does not remove its real driver-library dependency. B is a dashboard interpretation defect: `(13,3)` is native nvJitLink major/minor, not `(status, value)` and not patch identity. C is compilation failure; preserve its exception type/message and attached log. The StringIO captures successful compilation logging and cannot overturn failure.

NVRTC create returns `(status, program)`, compile/destroy return `(status,)`, and size queries return `(status, size)`; check every status. nvJitLink create returns a handle, complete/extraction/destroy return None, and native failures raise `nvJitLinkError` with `.status`. Successful native API return also does not establish a later stage. A Python buffer conversion error or missing symbol may precede any native resource acquisition.

For the separate failed linker, keep the complete exception, query/read logs while the acquired handle remains valid using a correctly sized `bytearray`, then attempt destroy once in finally. Reporting or destruction failures are secondary. If create had failed, no valid handle would exist for either operation. Do not invent unavailable diagnostic text.

Require a fresh result directory and associate source hash, options, explicit virtual/real target, exact interpreter/packages, actual native libraries, backend, PTX/cubin hashes, cuobjdump inspection, and exit status with the same attempt. Failed compilation/linking cannot reuse yesterday's success label. A retained qualifying build can support compilation only; it cannot establish lookup, GPU completion, vector correctness, or Runtime-Verified status.

**Valid alternatives and common errors:** Separate typed adapters can normalize report fields after honoring each API contract; a universal two-item unpack cannot. A deliberately designed cache needs identity/integrity/compatibility checks, which EX21 avoids with fresh artifacts. Neither a filename extension nor an empty log proves any production stage succeeded.

</details>

**Source basis:** Original dashboard/failure scenarios; [P03](/en/python/runtime-compilation-linking/), [SRC-CUDA-077](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-077), [SRC-CUDA-078](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-078), and [SRC-CUDA-079](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-079). No log transcript or performance value is fabricated.

**Source date:** 2026-09-12.

<span id="pb-r5-004" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R5-004: Audit timing boundaries for a two-branch request

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R5-004 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [P04](/en/frameworks/queued-work-timing/), exactly `[P04]`.
- **Hardware gate:** No GPU for this paper audit. Measurement needs native Linux and one CC 8.0+ GPU with at least 8 GB.
- **Version gate:** PyTorch 2.11.0+cu128, CPython 3.12.14, native allocator; packaged runtime/CUPTI 12.8.90, no system Toolkit requirement.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M07](/en/memory/stream-ordering/), [Q05](/en/correctness/timing-asynchronous-gpu-work/), and [P04](/en/frameworks/queued-work-timing/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-12.

**Prompt:** A request produces a tensor on stream A, then forks into a matrix product on B and a checksum on C. Both results belong to the response. A dashboard records start/end events on A, with end immediately after the two host submissions. It launches the next request, synchronizes the device, and calls the elapsed event interval response latency. A second chart times only those host submissions with perf_counter and labels the difference GPU acceleration. No durations or traces are supplied.

**Goal:** Define three honest measurements without changing the request's work or silently including its successor.

**Constraints:** Keep both branches, transfer policy, shape, dtype and allocator identical across comparisons. Treat this as a proposed schedule, not an observed run. Preserve data ordering and storage ownership independently from timing. Do not repair an early event by renaming its number.

**Expected evidence:** An event dependency graph, a scope table for enqueue/response/device intervals, a successor exclusion rule, and an external Environment Manifest plus repetition plan for any future measurements.

**Acceptance criteria:** An event-enabled start precedes A's production and both branches; A joins completion events recorded after B/C work before recording end. End completion precedes elapsed_time in milliseconds. A selected-device wall interval drains prior work before starting and waits for declared work before stopping. The next request is submitted only afterward for an isolated-response claim. An unsynchronized host interval remains an enqueue-side observation, not pure kernel time or a speedup denominator.

<details><summary>Hint 1: Waiting later does not move an earlier timestamp</summary>List everything ordered before the end event itself. Device synchronization cannot insert the missing branch-to-end edges retroactively.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2: Latency and throughput need different experiments</summary>Starting the next request before the completion boundary can turn an isolated-response measurement into a mixed workload. A steady-state throughput study is valid only with that different scope declared.</details>

<details>
<summary>Separate reviewed solution: Open after your attempt</summary>

**Reviewed solution:** Record a timing-enabled start on A, produce the input, then record a readiness event. B and C wait for readiness before their work. Record a completion event after each branch, make A wait for both, and only then record the timing-enabled end. Retain all branch storage through its uses. Waiting for end makes its timestamp readable; the interval includes the graph's gaps and joins, not a sum of isolated kernel times.

For isolated response wall time, finish prior work on the selected device, start the host clock, submit the declared request and its transfers, wait for completion, then stop. Device-wide synchronization includes all streams on that device, so remove unrelated producers or explicitly include them. Submit the successor after stopping. Label the unsynchronized host chart as host submission time, including incidental blocking; there is no guaranteed ordering between that number and the event number.

Warm up the actual shapes/dtypes/libraries and allocator outside measurement. Record warmup and repetition counts, every retained interval and a distribution, workload/source revision, driver, loaded libraries, launch-blocking state and synchronization scope. Keep checksum readback, item extraction, printing and profiler collection outside timing unless intentionally included. Require finite nonnegative observations and correctness, not a minimum speedup.

**Valid alternatives and common errors:** A completion event on a dedicated join stream is valid with the same complete graph. Retained handles plus explicit completion can protect lifetime. A steady-state multi-request benchmark is another valid question, not a substitute for response latency. Merely synchronizing after the old end, summing branch durations, or dividing incomparable host/device intervals fails the audit. No GPU evidence has been produced here.

</details>

**Source basis:** Original two-branch service audit; [P04](/en/frameworks/queued-work-timing/), [SRC-CUDA-080](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-080), and [SRC-CUDA-081](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-081). No upstream exercise or measured output is reproduced.

**Source date:** 2026-09-12.

<span id="pb-r5-005" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R5-005: Repair reuse contracts for a three-stream ring slot

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R5-005 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Correctness debugging; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [P05](/en/frameworks/streams-and-storage-lifetime/), exactly `[P05]`.
- **Hardware gate:** No GPU for the proof; safe allocation-pressure runs need native Linux, CC 8.0+, and at least 8 GB.
- **Version gate:** PyTorch 2.11.0+cu128 native caching allocator, ordinary eager tensors, no external allocation or CUDA Graph pool.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [M08](/en/memory/event-dependencies-timing/), [P04](/en/frameworks/queued-work-timing/), and [P05](/en/frameworks/streams-and-storage-lifetime/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-12.

**Prompt:** A ring-slot arena is allocated on A; an offset view is filled on B and read by C. C calls wait_stream(B) before B queues the fill. B has no wait on A because the fill is write-only. The manager records the view on C and offers two reuse modes: drop all references immediately after enqueue, or retain the arena and overwrite the slot on A for the next request. It claims both modes are safe because the view was recorded and B is now its allocation origin.

**Goal:** Repair data dependencies, allocator lifetime and explicit slot mutation as separate obligations.

**Constraints:** Keep the allocation origin A and the offset view. No deliberate race execution, exact-pointer reuse assertion, global allocator substitution, or promised corruption. Future submissions after storage release are forbidden.

**Expected evidence:** An origin/producer/consumer/last-use/release ledger; corrected wait placement; separate proofs for freeing storage and reusing a still-live slot; safe pressure-test acceptance and its Environment Manifest.

**Acceptance criteria:** B waits for relevant A work before filling even uninitialized logical storage; C's wait follows the fill submission and precedes its read. Register every non-origin use B/C before release or join both back to A before final release. Recording C cannot order B's fill or prevent A's explicit overwrite. An offset view keeps the same backing allocation origin.

<details><summary>Hint 1: The view's offset does not allocate another block</summary>Identify the backing storage, not the last writer or the view's first address. Recycled memory can still have origin-stream work queued.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2: Freeing and overwriting are different hazards</summary>Allocator bookkeeping delays reuse of released storage. The application can still race by writing a live allocation, so the ring-slot lease needs its own completion edge.</details>

<details>
<summary>Separate reviewed solution: Open after your attempt</summary>

**Reviewed solution:** A remains the allocation origin. Insert B.wait_stream(A) after the relevant A submissions and before B's fill. Insert C.wait_stream(B) after B queues that fill, not before. A wait captures the argument stream's work already submitted at its call boundary; it does not subscribe to future work.

For immediate reference release, record the backing allocation's B and C uses before release and finish queuing all uses while owners remain valid. The native allocator records side-stream use and inserts completion events at deallocation; registration does not create data ordering. A shifted view registers the storage pointer, not a separately owned offset allocation. A manual alternative joins B/C back to true origin A after their final submissions but before final release, so later origin-stream reuse is ordered safely without a CPU wait.

For the retained ring slot, A must wait for C's read to finish before overwriting; recording alone cannot help because no allocator reuse is involved. With the repaired B-to-C dependency, C's final completion also covers B's earlier fill. Keep a slot lease until that boundary; if another reader is added later, extend the proof. Retaining a Python variable until enqueue is not retaining it until completion.

**Valid alternatives and common errors:** Holding storage until a checked consumer synchronization is safe but blocks the CPU. Independent slots with explicit leases can preserve concurrency. Reject treating B as origin, using a wait in the wrong direction, claiming record_stream copies data, or requiring an unsafe schedule to fail every time. Future native-allocator pressure runs must validate every output and record dependencies, allocation sizes, repetitions and the exact environment; source reasoning alone grants no runtime result.

</details>

**Source basis:** Original ring-slot and two-mode reuse proposal; [P05](/en/frameworks/streams-and-storage-lifetime/), [TERM-201](/en/glossary/#term-201), and [SRC-CUDA-081](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-081).

**Source date:** 2026-09-12.

<span id="pb-r5-006" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R5-006: Reject a misleading precision acceptance report

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R5-006 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Correctness debugging; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [P06](/en/frameworks/mixed-precision-contracts/), exactly `[P06]`.
- **Hardware gate:** Paper arithmetic needs no GPU. Numerical acceptance needs native Linux, CC 8.0+, at least 8 GB; native BF16 is checked separately.
- **Version gate:** PyTorch 2.11.0+cu128 torch.amp; CPython 3.12.14; norm error, per-element tolerance and parameter rounding remain separate contracts.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [Q02](/en/correctness/floating-point-order-reproducibility/), [L08](/en/libraries/tensor-core-precision-contracts/), and [P06](/en/frameworks/mixed-precision-contracts/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-12.

**Prompt:** A precision rollout has a supplied reference vector r=[1,0.000001,-0.000001] and candidate c=[1,0,0]. It approves candidates when Euclidean norm-relative error is at most 0.000002, then claims all elementwise contracts and a downstream decision r[1]>0 are preserved. The actual elementwise contract is atol=0.0000002 and rtol=0.001. A separate training control starts with FP32 p=1, finite unscaled gradient 1 and SGD learning rate 2^-30, with no momentum or decay. The report treats a finite gradient as proof of a changed parameter and proposes raising GradScaler to repair both discrepancies. These are paper data, not a GPU run.

**Goal:** Separate global accuracy, local error, task decisions and representable optimizer updates before approving a precision change.

**Constraints:** Keep the supplied tolerances and strict-positive decision fixed. Treat the training update as exact arithmetic followed by IEEE FP32 nearest rounding. The candidate's internal algorithm and input quantization are unspecified; do not infer them from these vectors. No benchmark or GPU execution is required.

**Expected evidence:** Maximum absolute and norm-relative errors, each element's tolerance verdict, the decision change, the ideal and stored parameter values, and separate reference/gradient/update evidence needed for a future rollout.

**Acceptance criteria:** The norm test passes at about 1.414214e-6, while both small components fail the 2.01e-7 local error bound. The strict-positive decision changes. The ideal parameter is 1-2^-30, but FP32 rounds it back to 1; finiteness does not prove a representable update. Raising backward scale repairs neither forward scores nor parameter-rounding loss after correct unscale. Reject this rollout under the stated contracts.

<details><summary>Hint 1: A large reference component can dominate a norm</summary>Compare the two small errors with their own absolute-plus-relative bounds, not with the magnitude of the first component.</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2: A finite update can disappear at storage</summary>Correct gradient unscale removes the larger backward factor. Compare the resulting SGD step with FP32 spacing immediately below 1.</details>

<details>
<summary>Separate reviewed solution: Open after your attempt</summary>

**Reviewed solution:** Maximum absolute error is 1e-6. The Euclidean error norm is sqrt(2)*1e-6 and the reference norm is sqrt(1+2e-12), so the norm-relative error is about 1.414214e-6 and passes the dashboard threshold. Element 0 is exact. For each small component, the permitted error is 2e-7+0.001*1e-6=2.01e-7, below its 1e-6 error. Both fail, and the positive decision on element 1 changes from true to false. Global norm agreement does not authorize replacing a local or task-level criterion.

The ideal SGD result is 1-2^-30. FP32 spacing immediately below 1 is 2^-24; half that gap is 2^-25. The stipulated decrement is smaller, so storing the result rounds back to 1. This is not evidence that GradScaler skipped the optimizer; it is a distinct representability problem. Correct unscale restores gradient 1 regardless of the temporary backward factor. A validation control meant to demonstrate a parameter change must choose and justify a representable update and compare actual parameters from fresh identical states.

The report cannot locate the source of the supplied forward error. Future evidence needs an FP64 reference constructed from original inputs before narrowing and a second reference from widened quantized inputs. Record finiteness, per-element errors, maximum absolute error, norm-relative error with a zero-reference convention, the task decision and exact tolerances. Separate storage/dispatch/internal arithmetic/output fields; output dtype identifies neither accumulator width nor Tensor Core use. Native BF16 needs including_emulation=False and operation-specific validation. Record IEEE FP32 baseline and reduction/precision settings without mixing old and new TF32 control families.

**Valid alternatives and common errors:** Reject the rollout, retain a validated higher-precision sensitive region, or propose a separately justified task tolerance; do not silently loosen this contract. Full FP32 is a useful control, not an automatic accuracy guarantee. GradScaler addresses backward range, cannot recover forward quantization and can legitimately fall below 1 under overflow pressure. Test a separately injected nonfinite gradient before unscale for update skipping, not by inferring skip from an unchanged rounded parameter. Exact environment, gradients, parameter deltas and raw outputs are required for runtime claims; keep readback/get_scale outside timing. The arithmetic above is a stipulated model, not execution evidence.

</details>

**Source basis:** Original rollout thresholds and parameter-rounding audit; [P06](/en/frameworks/mixed-precision-contracts/), [TERM-202](/en/glossary/#term-202), [TERM-203](/en/glossary/#term-203), and [SRC-CUDA-082](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-082).

**Source date:** 2026-09-12.

<span id="pb-r5-007" class="resource-target" aria-hidden="true"></span>

## PB-R5-007: Reject profiler correlations spliced across runs

- **ID and Publication Pair:** PB-R5-007 in the `practice-bank` Publication Pair.
- **Type and difficulty:** Evidence review; advanced.
- **Direct prerequisite:** [P07](/en/frameworks/python-to-cuda-profiling/), exactly `[P07]`.
- **Hardware gate:** No GPU for this audit. Capture needs native Linux, CC 8.0+, at least 8 GB, and working CUPTI activity tracing.
- **Version gate:** PyTorch 2.11.0+cu128, pinned Kineto, CUPTI package 12.8.90; public eager profiling, no concurrent Nsight collection.
- **Related Learning Units and resources:** [Q07](/en/correctness/timeline-first-nsight-systems/), [Q08](/en/correctness/kernel-first-nsight-compute/), and [P07](/en/frameworks/python-to-cuda-profiling/).
- **Last reviewed:** 2026-09-12.

**Prompt:** A retry service has two independent process runs R-A and R-B, each with two completed profiler windows. Both runs happen to contain a local external-correlation value 17 and the same user label. Four callbacks upload to one replaceable trace filename; only the last file survives. A dashboard keeps summary tables, resets each run's displayed clock to zero, joins rows globally on 17, and assigns an R-B kernel to R-A's request. It sums device times across runs as retry latency. R-A's table has CUDA time but its discarded timeline was never checked for kernels. All statements describe a hypothetical evidence packet, not real traces.

**Goal:** Design run-scoped correlation and trace custody so retries cannot manufacture a request-to-kernel relationship.

**Constraints:** Processes have no supplied shared clock or cross-run request mapping. Correlation values and labels are not globally unique. Data is already on-device, so transfers are not required. Do not reconstruct missing events from tables, fabricate a trace, freeze JSON internals, or collect private stacks without authorization.

**Expected evidence:** A four-slot run/window artifact inventory, a scoped join key and provenance policy, verdicts for attribution/latency/capture completeness, and the minimum new capture and unprofiled measurement required to revisit the rejected claims.

**Acceptance criteria:** Require distinct R-A/W1, R-A/W2, R-B/W1 and R-B/W2 artifacts with hashes; four callback invocations and one retained file are not four traces. Local value 17 does not authorize a cross-run association, and reset display clocks provide no elapsed retry time. Keep R-A's kernel tracing unresolved, not certified from its table. A fresh complete capture must show CPU/runtime/kernel relations within its own run and window.

<details><summary>Hint 1: Add identity before joining data</summary>Two unrelated processes can reuse the same integer. Which run, window, process and artifact produced the number, and which original correlation record supports the edge?</details>

<details><summary>Hint 2: A callback is not durable custody</summary>A later successful overwrite can erase earlier evidence. Summary totals cannot recover the original events or establish a common clock across retries.</details>

<details>
<summary>Separate reviewed solution: Open after your attempt</summary>

**Reviewed solution:** Create four separate artifact slots R-A/W1, R-A/W2, R-B/W1 and R-B/W2. Each stores source/build identity, process/run identity, active iteration range, schedule/options, capture success, byte hash and immutable retained location. Export inside on_trace_ready to a distinct destination and confirm retention before counting the window. The surviving last file proves nothing about the other three. acc_events and retained tables cannot reconstruct their timelines; rerun the missing capture if the relationship still matters.

Scope frontend/runtime/device joins to the originating capture and process, retaining window identity and original correlation records plus device/stream coordinates. The bare value 17 is not a cross-run request key; even the same user label identifies a phase, not a globally unique operation occurrence. An application request/retry identifier can label occurrences, but each run must still supply its own CPU-to-launch-to-kernel chain. One CPU operation can launch several kernels, some after its host interval ends. Do not substitute name matching, nearest timestamps or universal external-ID assertions for those links.

Retry latency needs a separately declared end-to-end host interval that covers the request and retry completion, not two reset display clocks or a sum of device durations. key_averages may aggregate overlapping events and is not a timeline. For R-A, a CUDA time column cannot settle whether CUPTI tracing worked; classify the claim as unresolved because the needed file was not inspected and retained. New capture must check activities, actual kernel/runtime events, loaded CUPTI after work, package/driver identity and host/container policy. Keep other CUPTI tools separate, avoid privilege escalation, and separate shape-recording runs from unprofiled timing because instrumentation can retain tensors and perturb lifetime.

**Valid alternatives and common errors:** A single continuous capture can answer a different, explicitly scoped same-process question. A manifest-backed collection of per-window files preserves retries without inventing a unified clock. Sanitized derivatives may be shared only with an authorized raw-to-derivative custody record; do not upload private source paths. Reject hashes without retained bytes, identical labels as identity, table timing as proof of kernel tracing, and callback counts as file counts. This audit creates no trace, retry latency or GPU evidence.

</details>

**Source basis:** Original cross-process retry and overwritten-artifact audit; [P07](/en/frameworks/python-to-cuda-profiling/), [TERM-204](/en/glossary/#term-204), and [SRC-CUDA-083](/en/sources-and-versions/#src-cuda-083).

**Source date:** 2026-09-12.
